[[P913]]

SECTION V

CHAPTER XXIV

Influence of Purāṇas on [[Dharmasastra|Dharmaśāstra]]

Literary works and society act and re-act on each other. The state of Indian society [[&|a]] few centuries before and after Christ, riven as it was by the growing strength and onslaught of Buddhism, Jainism and other dissenting sects and disturbed by invasions of the Greeks, [[Sakas,|Śakas,]] Pahlavas, [[Hūpas|Hūṇas]] and other foreign tribes, gave much food for thought to those who were devoted to the Vedic religion and induced them to write works setting forth new ideologies and practices and effecting a [[re statement|re-statement]] of the ancient Vedic and [[Smrti|Smṛti]] religion. When these works attained a position of authority and eminence, the [[endea vour|endeavour]] of the followers of the Veda was to follow them as far as possible and to adapt their practices and religious rites to the requirements of the Purāṇas. We have to see how the Purāṇas [[Bet|set]] about their task of re-orientation. We must note that current Hindu religious practices, judging from the saṅkalpa made at the beginning of every rite, are meant to confer upon the [[per former|performer]] the rewards declared by Śruti (Veda), [[Smrti|Smṛti]] and [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] ([[Sruti-smrti-purāpokta-phalaprāptyartham|Śruti-smṛti-purāṇokta-phalaprāptyartham]]). The task was [[two fold,|two-fold,]] viz. (1) to undermine the power and prestige of Buddhism and Jainism and the influence of the numerous philosophical[^1447] schools that had sprung up and (2) to wean away large sections of the masses from the attractive features of Buddhism and to convince them that they could secure in the re-orientated Hindu faith the same benefits, social and spiritual, as Buddhism promised and that the religious principles of the followers of Veda coincided with many of the teachings of Buddhism and were borrowed by Buddhism from Vedic practices. Ultimately Buddhism vanished from the land of its origin. The main causes of this disappearance of Buddhism from Bhārata will be stated at the end of this section, but this much may be said here at once that the [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] played a substantial role in [[bring ing|bringing]] [[P914]] about the decline and disappearance of Buddhism by emphasizing and assimilating some of the principles and doctrines of Buddha[^1448] such as ahiṁsā, by accepting Buddha himself as an avatāra of Viṣṇu, by adopting vegetarianism as a high form of austerity, by making use of monasteries and asceticism as stated in such smṛtis as those of [[Magu|Manu]] and Yāj.

[^1447]: It is stated in the [[Mabāvagga|Mahāvagga]] (part of Suttanipāta) that there were 63 philosophical schools at the time of [[Buddba|Buddha]] (vide SBE vol. X. part 2 p. 92).

[^1448]: Pargiter (in [['Puarāṇa|'Purāṇa]] texts of the Dynasties of the Kali age' [[P.|p.]] XVIII footnote) [[tbloks|thinks]] that it was largely through the Paurāṇika [[Litera ture|Literature]] that Hinduism secured its revival and the downfall of [[Buddhism,|Buddhism.]]

The Purāṇas set about their task by saying that for the proper understanding of the Veda, knowledge of [[Itihāga|Itihāsa]] and Purāṇa was essential. A famous verse says[^1449] 'one should [[strongthen|strengthen]] the Veda by (the study and application of) Itihāsa and Purāṇa; the Veda is afraid of the person of little learning (with the thought) that he (the man of small learning) may harm it'. Manu states[^1450] that those brāhmaṇas that have learnt according to the rules (of Veda study) the Veda together with the works that strengthen it are to be understood as [[sistas|śiṣṭas]] and are instrumental in making (the meaning of) the Veda clearly perceptible. The [[Vayu|Vāyu]][^1451] emphasizes in this connection that that brāhmaṇa who knows the four Vedas together with [[(the six )|(the six)]] ancillary lores and the Upaniṣads would not be a wise man if he did not know Purāṇas. The Upaniṣads drop brief hints about the creation of ākāśa from the one brahma (in Tai. Up. [[II, 1),|II. 1),]] of tejas (Chān. Up. VI. 2. 3), of waters (Chān. Up. VI. 2. 4). The Purāṇas explain at great length the creation and dissolution of the elements (in the order reverse of that of creation) [[o g.|e. g.]] Vāyu [[4,|4.]] 17 ff, Brahma 1-3, Agni 17, [[Brahmānda|Brahmāṇḍa]] II. 3 ff, Kūrma I. [[2,4,7,|2, 4, 7,]] 8 &c. The stories of [[Hariscandra|Hariścandra]] and [[P915]] Naciketas that [[ocour|occur]] in the Ait. Br. and the [[Kathopadiṣad|Kaṭhopaniṣad]] are explained at length in Brahmapurāṇa (chap. 104 and 150 about [[Hariscandra),|Hariścandra),]] in Sabhāparva chap. 12 (for [[Hariscandra)|Hariścandra)]] and in Anuśāsana chap. 91 (for Naciketas). The famous dialogue of Yama and [[Yami|Yamī]] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 10) is expanded in the [[Narasimha purāpa|Narasiṁha-purāṇa]] (chap. 13. 6-36). The Viṣṇupurāṇa[^1452] (IV. 6. 34 ff) sets out the story of Purūravas and Urvaśī, refers to the hymn in the [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 95 for that story and quotes the first verse of the hymn in a somewhat corrupt form.

But the claims of the extant Purāṇas go far beyond the above viz. as strengtheners of the Veda. The Kūrma[^1453] states: '(put) on one side all the Purāṇas together with [[Itihasa (Maha bhārata)|Itihāsa (Mahābhārata)]] and on another the highest Veda; it is this ([[Puranas|Purāṇas]]) that surpasses (the other viz. Veda)'. The Mahābhārata also makes a similar claim. The [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] appear to claim priority (and even [[equality )|equality)]] with the Veda. In note 1349 passages from the Matsya and other Purāṇas have been cited, stating that [[Brahma|Brahmā]] first thought of the [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] before all other śāstras and then the Vedas sprang forth from his lips. Several Purāṇas [[ara|are]] spoken of as equal to the Veda (Vedasammita) as in[^1454] Vāyu I. 11, 4. 12, Brahma 1. 29, 245. 4 and 21, Viṣṇu I. 1. 13, [[VI. 8.1%,|VI. 8. 12,]] Padma VI. 282. 116. Further, several Purāṇas claim to have been delivered by some God such as [[Brahma|Brahmā]] (Brahmapurāṇa I. 30) or by the Wind-god [[(Vāyu I. 196 )|(Vāyu I. 196)]][^1455] or by the avatāras of [[Viśṇu|Viṣṇu]] as in the case of Matsya-purāṇa [[(I. 26 )|(I. 26)]] or Varāha [[(2. 1 3).|(2. 1–3).]] The japa of Vedic texts was deemed to destroy all sins as shown in H. of Dh. vol. IV. pp. 45-50. The Purāṇas also say that reading the Purāṇas or listening to a recitation of them [[P916]] would destroy all sins.[^1456] Vide Vāyu 103. 58, Brahma 175. [[89 90,|89–90,]] [[Matsy 1|Matsya]] 290. 20, [[291, 29|291. 29]] and 31, Viṣṇu VI. 8. 3, 12. Some of the Purāṇas indulge in extravagant praise of themselves, e. g. the [[Varāha-purana|Varāha-purāṇa]] [[( 217. 12-13,|(217. 12–13,]] 217. 15-16) states that reading ten [[chaptors|chapters]] of that Purāṇa confers the merit secured by the performance of [[Agnistoma|Agniṣṭoma]] and Atirātra sacrifices. Vide Brahma [[254, 34-35,|254. 34–35,]] Agni 384. 13-30, [[Devi-Bhāgavata|Devī-Bhāgavata]] XII. 13. 11-17 in a similar strain. Moreover, the Purāṇas dwell upon the superior value and [[officacy,|efficacy,]] as compared with the Vedic sacrifices, of some of the institutions on which they lay emphasis, such as pilgrimages,[^1457] vratas, bhakti. The Padma states (I. 38. 2 and 18) that by merely going to Gayā or by taking a bath in the Phalgu river one secures the reward that the performance of Aśvamedha confers. The Skanda [[proclaimg|proclaims]][^1458] 'I have no use for sacrificial rites that are declared by the Veda, that have no life in them, that are within the domain of ignorance and that [[ontail|entail]] injury (to animals). If (a sacrifice) is performed with such inanimate things as fuel-sticks, flowers and [[kusa|kuśa]] grass, the result must be similar (inanimate), since the effect is like the cause'. Vide [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] 337 for a story on the discussion between sages and gods about offering in sacrifices merely [[pro ducts|products]] of grains or goat-flesh. Offerings of flesh in sacrifices to gods were made in the [[Rgvedic|Ṛgvedic]] period. But even in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] there are a few verses that indicate that offerings of [[gheo|ghee]] and fuel-sticks were declared to be able to win the favour of gods as much as animal sacrifices might do. 'Whoever[^1459] offers to (or worships) Agni with a fuel-stick or with an oblation of ghee or [[P917]] with (the study of) the Veda or who performs a good sacrifice with prostrations, for him alone run fleet horses and his is most brilliant fame; and no [[ovil|evil]] whether brought about by gods or by men might reach him from any side'. Another mantra[^1460] [[gays|says]] 'O Agni! we bring to you an offering accompanied by a [[śkmantra|ṛkmantra]] fashioned (or produced) by our heart. May those mantras be oxen, bulls and cows from you'.

The [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] only pursue an attitude to Veda and sacrifices that is found in some of the Upaniṣads. The [[Mundakopaniṣad|Muṇḍakopaniṣad]][^1461] says: 'one should know two vidyās [[(lores ),|(lores),]] parā (the higher) and [[apara|aparā]] (the lower); the latter comprehends the four Vedas and Phonetics, aphorisms about solemn [[saorifices,|sacrifices,]] grammar, Nirukta (derivation of words), metres, astronomy; while the highest lore is that whereby the Imperishable (Reality) is known'. The same Upaniṣad condemns aparā-vidyā when it says [["These|'These]] sacrifices are infirm [[(leaky )|(leaky)]] boats constituted by eighteen [[( persons)|(persons)]] depending on which are declared actions that are inferior; those foolish people who welcome these actions as the highest good become subject again to old age and death'. The [[Kathopaniṣad|Kaṭhopaniṣad]][^1462] states that what are known as [[avidya|avidyā]] and vidyā are far apart from each other, are contradictory and lead to different results. When Nārada approached Sanatkumāra and requested the latter to teach him, the latter said to him 'tell me what you know and then I shall tell you what is beyond that.' Then Nārada stated that he [[know|knew]] all the four Vedas, [[Itihasa-purāṇa|Itihāsa-purāṇa]] the fifth Veda and several other [[loros,|lores,]] whereupon Sanat-kumāra told him that the four Vedas and the other lores he had learned were merely a name and then he led Nārada gradually to the understanding of the Highest Self. The Bṛ. Up. [[I, 4, 10|I. 4. 10]] condemns him, who worships [[&|a]] deity thinking he is different from the devatā, that he does not know the truth, that he is like [[&|a]] (sacrificial) animal to the gods. Similarly, in [[P918]] several passages of the Upaniṣads austerities, liberality, [[straight forwardness,|straightforwardness,]] [[ahimsā|ahiṁsā]] and truthfulness are put forward as equal to or superior to the actual performance of the ceremonial of sacrifice ([[yajna|yajña]]); vide Chāndogya III. 17. 4, Praśna I. 15, [[Muṇdaka|Muṇḍaka]] I. 2. 11.

In his [[oy|भाष्य]] on Vedāntasūtra I. 2. 21 Śaṅkarācārya holds that this verse is part of the condemnation of aparā vidyā.

[^1462]: [[ita fufia fara afy T ufa preita.|दूरमेते विपरीते विषूची अविद्या या च विद्येति ज्ञाता । कठोप.]] I. 2. 4.

Though in a few passages of the Upaniṣads the knowledge of the Highest Self is put higher than the four Vedas, the [[Upa niṣads|Upaniṣads]] generally treat the Vedas as authoritative and quote Vedic verses in support of their statements. For example, the Ait. Up. II. 5 quotes [[Rgveda IV, 27. 1|Ṛgveda IV. 27. 1]] [[(taduktam-sșiṇā-Garbhe : nu &c.),|(taduktam-ṛṣiṇā-Garbhe nu &c.),]] Praśnop. I. 11 quotes [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. [[164, 12|164. 12]] (pañcapādam pitaram), [[Bș.|Bṛ.]] Up. II. 5. 15, 17 and 19 quote respectively [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 116. 12, I. 117. 22, VI. 47. 18 (rūpam rūpam pratirūpo). The Upaniṣads further emphasize[^1463] that brahmavidyā is to be imparted only to those who are śrotriya [[( who|(who]] have studied the Veda), who engage in their duties and who [[bave|have]] properly [[per formed|performed]] Śirovrata. The [[Bp.|Bṛ.]][^1464] Up. shows that Veda study, sacrifices, charity &c. are preparations for the knowledge of brahma: 'Brāhmaṇas [[(and others )|(and others)]] desire to know this [[( great|(great]] Self) by study of the Veda, by sacrifices, gifts, tapas, fasting'. These passages make it clear that study of the Veda and [[per formance|performance]] of sacrifices enjoined by it are accepted by the [[Upa niṣads|Upaniṣads]] as preparatory and as cleansing the mind of its lower passions and as making a person worthy of receiving the [[know ledge|knowledge]] of the highest truth about the One Supreme Spirit and that the Upaniṣads do not altogether condemn and give up the [[Vodas|Vedas]] and [[sacrifices,|sacrifices.]]

[^1463]: [[aaherything fēkarani: wat vraagt: Fri ya gari 42:1 Titai waragica Partian fandt u gugatge III 2. 10. Fritera|तदेतदृचाभ्युक्तम् । क्रियावन्तः श्रोत्रिया ब्रह्मनिष्ठाः स्वयं जुह्वत एकर्षिं श्रद्धयन्तः । तेषामेवैतां ब्रह्मविद्यां वदेत शिरोव्रतं विधिवद्यैस्तु चीर्णम् ॥ मुण्डकोप० III 2. 10. शिरोव्रत]] is carrying [[fre|fire]] on the head (according to Ātharvaṇa rules). The [[aeftorea (XI, 9, 12-13)|देवीभागवत (XI. 9. 12-13)]] remarks [[' H einarinna: re: a TEHTI सङ्किोलनं कुर्यात् शिरोव्रतसमायम् ॥ "यावद्वियोदयस्तावतस्य विद्या खलूत्तमा '.|‘अग्निरित्यादिभिर्मन्त्रैः षड्भिः शुद्धेन भस्मना । सर्वाङ्गोद्धूलनं कुर्यात् शिरोव्रतसमाह्वयम् ॥ ... यावद्विद्योदयस्तावत्तस्य विद्या खलूत्तमा ॥’.]] The six [[te maatras are : Maia e, aynia , a Fe, PUESTATE मस्म, व्योमेति भस्म, सर्व ह वा इदं भस्म',|अथर्वशिरस् mantras are : अग्निरिति भस्म, वायुरिति भस्म, जलमिति भस्म, स्थलमिति भस्म, व्योमेति भस्म, सर्वं ह वा इदं भस्म '.]]

[^1464]: [[Iraan TT parlament i araa TAISYTISH . 54. IV. 4. 22; vide star 18. 5 Anar a: a 1974 Arda TEL I pero n o|तमेतं वेदानुवचनेन ब्राह्मणा विविदिषन्ति यज्ञेन दानेन तपसाऽनाशकेन । बृह. उप. IV. 4. 22; vide गीता 18. 5 यज्ञदानतपःकर्म न त्याज्यं कार्यमेव तत् । यज्ञो दानं तपश्चैव]] पावनानि [[मनीषिणाम्|मनीषिणाम् ॥.]]

The [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] adopt, in spite of the claims made by them here and there about their priority to the Vedas, about their own value and efficacy, the same attitude towards the Veda as the Upaniṣads do. They treat the Vedas as authoritative and enjoin [[P919]] the employment of Vedic mantras in many rites. The author contributed a paper to the Dr. Kunhan Raja Presentation volume pp. 5-8 on the 'Vedic mantras and legends employed in Purāṇas' citing illustrations from the Brahmapurāṇa. A few illustrations from other Purāṇas may be set out here. The [[Matayapurāṇa|Matsyapurāṇa]] (chap. 93) when describing the procedure of the homa to the nine planets prescribes nine Vedic mantras, five of which are different from those provided by Yāj. (I. 300-301). Vide p. 750 above for a comparative table of the two sets of mantras. In the [[Udvahatattva|Udvāhatattva]][^1465] Raghunandana remarks that the mantras 'ā [[krspeda'|kṛṣṇena']] and others are common to persons following the four Vedas and that Bhavadevabhaṭṭa held the same view. Matsya prescribes that when inauspicious birds (like an owl) or animals cry near a house or enter it, a homa should be performed, or five brāhmaṇas should engage in a japa[^1466] of the hymn beginning with [['Duvāḥ|'Devāḥ]] kapota' (Ṛg. X. 165. 1-5). In describing the whole procedure of the establishment of images of gods or the liṅga (of Śiva), the Matsya [[(chapter 265 )|(chapter 265)]] prescribes numerous Vedic verses and [[hymna|hymns]] for the different parts of the ceremony. For example, for bringing about the purification of the image four mantras are [[presoribed|prescribed]] viz. Ṛg. [[VII49.|VII. 49.]] 1-3 and X. 9. 1; in providing for the placing of a jar full of water near the head of the bed on which the image is to be placed, two mantras [['Āpo devi'|'Āpo devī']] (Vāj. S. 12. 35, Tai. S. [[IV. 2.32 )|IV. 2. 32)]] [[andāpo|and 'āpo]] asmān [[mātarah'|mātaraḥ']] (Ṛg. X. [[17.10 )|17. 10)]] are to be recited. Matsya [[( 265.24–29)|(265. 24–29)]] prescribes for the japa of several sūktas (hymns) by four door-keepers learned in the Vedas that are to stand in the four main [[direo tions.|directions.]] The Agnipurāṇa (41. 6-8) providing for the laying down of baked brick or stones in building a temple [[proscribes|prescribes]] the [[Tecital|recital]] of many Vedic mantras, viz. [[Kg.|Ṛg.]] X. 9. 1-3, Ṛg. X. 9. 4, Ṛg. IX. 58. 1-4, Pāvamānī verses (either Ṛg. IX. 1. 1-10 or verses from Ṛg. IX), Ṛg. I. 24. 15, Ṛg. IV. 31. 1, Vāj. S. IV. 36 [[(Varupasya),|(Varuṇasya),]] Ṛg. IV. 40. 5 and the [[Srīgākta|Śrīsūkta]] (of 29 [[Verses|verses]] [[P920]] beginning with [['Hiraṇyavarṇam harinim').|'Hiraṇyavarṇām hariṇīm').]] The [[Nāradiya purāṇa|Nāradīya-purāṇa]] [[( II.|(II.]] 73. 83-90) contains at end of each of the verses parts of [[vedio|vedic]] prayer, which [[ooour|occur]] in Ṛg. VII. 66. 16, Tai. Ār. IV. 4. 2-5 and [[Vaj.|Vāj.]] S. 36. 24; Bhāgavata I. 2. 21 (bhidyate &c.) is taken from [[Mundaka|Muṇḍaka]] Up. II. 2. 8.

[^1465]: गृहहोमे तु [[आकणेनेरयाविमन्त्राः|आ कृष्णेनेत्यादिमन्त्राः]] सर्ववेदसाधारणाः पुराणोक्तत्वात् । तथा च मत्स्यपुराणम् । आ कृष्णेनेति सूर्याय होमः [[कार्यों|कार्यो]] द्विजन्मना । [["केत कृण्वनिति|... केतुं कृण्वन्निति]] कुर्यात् [[केतूना To sta ga rent au Paramell gateam|केतूनामुपशान्तये ॥ अत एव भवदेवभट्टेनापि तथा लिखितम् ॥ उद्वाहतत्त्व]] (Jiv. II. p. 126). The [[HOUSE|मत्स्यपुराण]] verses quoted are [[93, 33–37.|93. 33–37.]] The mantras are taken from the Ṛgveda, Tai. S., Vāj. S. [[Wbat gara|What रघुनन्दन]] emphasizes is that whatever Veda may be studied by a man and his family, he has to employ the mantras specified by Matsya when he performs a [[grahahoma, .|grahahoma.]]

[^1466]: [[मुगपक्षिविकारेषु कुर्याडोमं सदक्षिणम् । देवाः कपोत इति वा जसण्याः पञ्जामि. R: I AGE 237. 13.|मृगपक्षिविकारेषु कुर्याद्धोमं सदक्षिणम् । देवाः कपोत इति वा जप्तव्याः पञ्चभिर्द्विजैः ॥ मत्स्य 237. 13.]]

The Purāṇas not only prescribe Vedic mantras for various purposes, but suggest the employment of numerous [[Paurāpika|Paurāṇika]] mantras. It appears that Paurāṇika mantras came to be employed along with Vedic mantras in religious rites even of Brāhmaṇas at the beginning of the Christian era or within [[&|a]] few centuries after Christ. Yāj. [[I, 229|I. 229]] prescribes that the [[Visve Deyas|Viśve Devas]] should be invited to come to a śrāddha with the [[çk,|ṛk,]] 'O [[Visve-Devas!|Viśve-Devas!]] Come, listen to this my call, sit down on these kuśas' (Ṛg. II. 41. 13). On this the Mitākṣarā (about 1100 A. D.) remarks that along with the Vedic mantra mentioned by Yāj. [[&|a]] smārta mantra should also be employed viz. the mantra[^1467] [['āgacobantu|'āgacchantu]] mahābhāgā,' which occurs in Skanda and [[Garuḍa purāṇa.|Garuḍa-purāṇa.]] Vide H. of Dh. vol. IV. p. 440 note 984 for the [[ascrip tion|ascription]] of this verse to various authorities. The [[Vayupurāṇa|Vāyupurāṇa]][^1468] prescribes that the mantra 'adoration to the [[devatūs,|devatās,]] to [[pits,|pitṛs,]] to the great Yogins, to [[stadha|svadhā]] and svāhā; they are always present' should be recited thrice at the beginning and at the end of [[brāddha|śrāddha]] and at the time of offering [[pindus;|piṇḍas;]] the [[pitļa|pitṛs]] [[quiokly|quickly]] come when the mantra is repeated and goblins run away; this mantra protects the [[pitrs|pitṛs]] in all three worlds'. This mantra is styled 'Saptārcis' (having seven flames) in Vāyu 74. 20, [[Brah mānda III.|Brahmāṇḍa III.]] 11. 30 and in Viṣṇudharmottara [[I, 140.|I. 140.]] 68 and by Hemādri on śrāddha pp. 1079 and 1208, who notes that it occurs in seven [[Puranas.|Purāṇas.]] In chap. 206 of the Agni in the procedure of offering arghya to the star Agastya (Canopus) [[Rg. I|Ṛg. I.]] 179. 6 has been adopted as verse 13.

[^1467]: The [[Hag|मन्त्र]] is : [[T ATIT Pata at H&MT: 18 Ara: UTN are an.|आगच्छन्तु महाभागा विश्वेदेवा महाबलाः । ये यत्र विहिताः श्राद्धे सावधाना भवन्तु ते ॥.]] This is [[TEST|गरुडपुराण]] I. 218. 7, [[bat q, by turn|but q. by अपरार्क]] on p. 478 from बृहस्पति and on p. 481 from ब्रह्मपुराण.

[^1468]: The mantra is: [[data: ragaca Agrita TI M FIT farma a hry 74. 15-16.|देवताभ्यः पितृभ्यश्च महायोगिभ्य एव च । नमः स्वधायै स्वाहायै नित्यमेव भवन्त्युत ॥ वायु 74. 15–16.]] Vide H. of [[Dk.|Dh.]] vol. IV. pp. 458-459 [[goto|note]] 1020 for all the verses in relation to this mantra and the purāṇas where they [[occor.|occur.]] In the [[prioted reina|printed ब्रह्माण्ड]] III. 11. 17–18 the [[# is daaring ni JA: MIET murt fareyi heya.|मन्त्र is देवताभ्यः ... एव च । नमः स्वाहायै स्वधायै नित्यमेव भवन्त्युत.]] Some read [[Pacqar FATTA;.|नित्यमेव नमो नमः.]] The [[FAAT. OD 9.|मिता. on या.]] I. 121 says that this mantra should be employed by [[dūdras|śūdras]] in all the five daily sacrifices [[accordiog|according]] to some, while others said that the [[fudra wat oaly to atter|śūdra was only to utter]] the word नमः.

[[P921]] Not only are mantras from Vedic [[Samhitay|Saṁhitās]] prescribed by the Purāṇas for certain rites, but Upaniṣad passages also [[some times|sometimes]] with slight variance are bodily transferred into some of the Purāṇas. For example, Kūrma II. 9. 12, 13 and 18 embody Tai. Up. II. 4 [[(yato vāco nivartante ),|(yato vāco nivartante),]] [[Syetaśvataropa. III, 8|Śvetāśvataropa. III. 8]] [[(vedāham-etam puruşam )|(vedāham-etam puruṣam)]] and VI. 11 'eko devaḥ'. The [[Vigpu purāṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] VI. 5. 65 is 'dve vidye veditavye iti [[catharyani|cātharvaṇī]] śrutiḥ' and contains a [[passage:of|passage of]] the [[Mundaka|Muṇḍaka]] Up. [[I.1.4|I. 1. 4]] (vide note 1461 above). The Vāyu 20. 5 [[(prapavo|(praṇavo]] dhanuḥ) and 20. 28 (Ajām-ekām) are respectively [[Mupdakopa. II, 2.4|Muṇḍakopa. II. 2. 4]] and Śvetāśvatara-up. IV. 5. Vāyu 14. 13 (sarvataḥ pāṇipādāntam) is almost the same as [[Svetaśyatara|Śvetāśvatara]] Up. III. 16 and Vāmana [[47, 64-65|47. 64–65]] has the same verse. [[Vamana|Vāmana]] 47. 67 is almost the [[game|same]] as Ṛg. I. 10. 1.

This gives rise to interesting questions. The śūdras had no right to study the Veda. But as a matter of fact the Purāṇas contain as exemplified above a good many Vedic mantras. It is stated in the Bhāgavata[^1468a] 'women, śūdras and brāhmaṇas in name only are beyond the pale of the three Vedas; therefore the sage (Vyāsa) composed through compassion for them the [[Bharata|Bhārata]] tale'. The [[Devibhagavata|Devībhāgavata]] states 'study of the [[Voda|Veda]] [[P922]] is not accepted for women, [[tūdras|śūdras]] and [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] in name only and [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] are compiled for the purpose of benefitting them'. From this it follows that in the case of [[gūdras|śūdras]] the listening to the [[Bharata|Bhārata]] was deemed to bring about the same results that the Veda does for [[drijas|dvijas]] and that even the śūdra may acquire the knowledge of the Self (and mokṣa) from the Bhārata.

[^1468a]: [[d ai agrariaci \*\*\*TESTATOTT g aat ag i Huan I. 4. 25 g. by ṣi p. 37, which remarks agar artar षगमाद्धारतस्य घेदकार्यात्मज्ञानकारित्वसिद्धिः'. नीशूदबिजबन्धूनां न वेदश्रवणं मतम् । तेषामेव Feature Front pana al Teatre|स्त्रीशूद्रद्विजबन्धूनां त्रयी न श्रुतिगोचरा । ... तस्माद्भारतमाख्यानं कृपया मुनिना कृतम् ॥ भागवत I. 4. 25 q. by परिभाषाप्रकाश p. 37, which remarks 'वेदकार्यकारित्वावगमाद्भारतस्य वेदकार्यात्मज्ञानकारित्वसिद्धिः'. स्त्रीशूद्रद्विजबन्धूनां न वेदश्रवणं मतम् । तेषामेव हितार्थाय पुराणानि कृतानि च । देवीभागवत]] I. 3. 21. [[T er|शङ्कराचार्य]] is careful to point out on Vedāntasūtra I. 3. 38 that śūdras have no [[ao|no]] adhikāra for [[brahmavidya|brahmavidyā]] based on the study of the Veda. But he does not deny to the śūdras the knowledge of the Self altogether. He refers to the instances of Vidura and Dharmavyādha that were possessed of the knowledge of brahma due to the effects of their former lives, states that they would secure the result of [[brabma jñāna|brahma-jñāna]] (viz. mokṣa, [[fipal|final]] liberation from saṁsāra), that śūdras have the right to learn from the [[Mahabbārata|Mahābhārata]] and Purāṇas as stated in "he should read to the four varṇas' and that in that way they might secure knowledge of brahma and mokṣa. [[" pat ga: attitainetega rad स्तेषां न शक्यते फलमातिः भतिषेद्धं ज्ञानस्यैकान्तिकफलसात् । 'श्रावयेच्चतुरो वर्णान! इति चेतिहासपुराणाधिगमे चातुर्वर्ण्यस्याधिकारस्मरणात् । वेदपूर्वकस्तु नास्त्यधिकारः बाणामिति Rua Oy on a. C. I, 3, 38. On . . III, 4. 36 yuan refers to the woman वाचक्रषी as one who had the knowledge of brahma 'रैक-वाचक्नवी qua T ATO ret: 1, faren|" येषां पुनः पूर्वकृतसंस्कारवशाद्विदुरधर्मव्याधप्रभृतीनां ज्ञानोत्पत्तिस्तेषां न शक्यते फलप्राप्तिः प्रतिषेद्धुं ज्ञानस्यैकान्तिकफलत्वात् । 'श्रावयेच्चतुरो वर्णान्' इति चेतिहासपुराणाधिगमे चातुर्वर्ण्यस्याधिकारस्मरणात् । वेदपूर्वकस्तु नास्त्यधिकारः शूद्राणामिति स्थितम् ॥ भाष्य on वे. सू. I. 3. 38. On वे. सू. III, 4. 36 शङ्कराचार्य refers to the woman वाचक्नवी as one who had the knowledge of brahma 'रैक्व-वाचक्नवीप्रभृतीनामेवंभूतानामपि ब्रह्मवित्त्वश्रुत्युपलब्धेः '. गार्गी वाचक्नवी]] figures as a great seeker after brahma in the Bṛ. Up. III. 6. 1, III. 8. 1 and 12. The [[HP|महाभारत]] says that what it [[dilaten|dilates]] upon as to the पुरुषार्थs, धर्म, अर्थ, काम and मोक्ष, is found elsewhere and that what it does not contain on those subjects can be found nowhere else and that the [[Mabābhārata|Mahābhārata]] should be listened to by [[bim|him]] who desires mokṣa, by [[brāhmagas,|brāhmaṇas,]] kings and pregnant women [[(Svargārohang parva|(Svargārohaṇaparva]] 5. 50-51).

Though the [[brāhmapas|brāhmaṇas]] in the 5th and following centuries A. D. wanted to placate the śūdras who were probably a majority of the whole people and to wean them away from Buddhism, they still kept a distinction between dvijas and śūdras and the only concessions made [[wore|were]] that the śūdras could worship in the same way as dvijas did and that they could have mantras (Paurāṇika) in their rites and ceremonies. For example, in allowing bhasma-snāna [[(smoaring|(smearing]] ashes on the [[body )|body)]] the Padma [[(IV. 110. 236-289)|(IV. 110. 286-289)]] provides Vedic mantras for men of the three varṇas but Paurāṇika mantras only (Padma IV. 110. 290-293) for śūdras. The Padma[^1469] further provides that śūdras could not perform prāṇāyāma or utter the sacred syllable 'om' but that they should substitute dhyāna in place of prāṇāyāma and 'Śiva' in place of 'om'.

[^1469]: [[gorapasa qua: a fungai aptarnerari farur TUPATET|प्राणायामश्च प्रणवः शूद्रेषु न विधीयते । प्राणायामपदे ध्यानं शिवेत्योङ्कारवर्णनम् ॥ पद्म]] IV. 110. 316.

Gradually in some matters the procedure provided in the [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] came to supersede the ancient [[Vodic|Vedic]] procedure [[pre scribed|prescribed]] for them. Aparārka states (on p. 14) that in Devapūjā one should follow the procedure prescribed in the [[Narasimha purāṇa|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]][^1470] and the like and not the procedure of the [[Pasupatas|Pāśupatas]] or [[Pancarātras|Pāñcarātras]] and (on p. 15) he says the same about the [[con secration|consecration]] of temples,[^1471] images and the [[like,|like.]]

[^1470]: [[Hittego|नरसिंहपु०]] chap. 62 deals with the procedure of [[Region.|विष्णुपूजा.]]

[^1471]: एवं प्रतिष्ठायामपि [[पुराणायक्वेतिकर्तव्यता|पुराणाद्युक्तैवेतिकर्तव्यता]] ग्राह्या [[नाण्या। तेषामेव न्यामिनधर्म qarora pragare oftearata Iturraf|नान्या । तेषामेव व्यामिश्रधर्मप्रमाणत्वेन भविष्यपुराणे परिज्ञातत्वात् । अपरार्क]] p. 15.

The [[Narasimhapurāṇa|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]] (chap. 63. 5-6) says that the mantra [[\*om|'om]] namo Nārāyaṇāya' enables one to secure all objects and japa of it frees a man from all [[sing|sins]] and leads him to absorption into Viṣṇu.[^1472]

[^1472]: [[T u r i fotoaparatais rytta poi farefa साधकः । इमं मन्त्रं जपेधस्त शुचिर्भूत्वा समाहितः । सर्वपापविनिर्डको विष्णुसायुज्यमापाया। नरसिंहपु 63.6-7: किं तप महभिर्मभक्तिपस्य जनार्दने ममी नारायणायेति मन्त्रः सर्वार्थ साधकः । विष्णुर्वेषां जयस्तेषां कुतस्तेषां पराजयः । येषामिन्दीपरहवामी पपस्यो जनार्दनः । page|किं तस्य बहुभिर्मन्त्रैः किं तस्य बहुभिर्व्रतैः । ओं नमो नारायणेति मन्त्रः सर्वार्थसाधकः ॥ इमं मन्त्रं जपेद्यस्तु शुचिर्भूत्वा समाहितः । सर्वपापविनिर्मुक्तो विष्णुसायुज्यमाप्नुयात् ॥ नरसिंहपु० 63. 6–7; किं तस्य बहुभिर्मन्त्रैर्भक्तिर्यस्य जनार्दने । नमो नारायणायेति मन्त्रः सर्वार्थसाधकः । विष्णुर्येषां जयस्तेषां कुतस्तेषां पराजयः । येषामिन्दीवरश्यामो हृदयस्थो जनार्दनः ॥ वामनपु०]] 94. 58-59; the [[Hey caya't angula F\* SET 11,|मत्स्य says 'ओं नमो नारायणेति मूलमन्त्र उदाहृतः ।'.]]

[[P923]] The Agnipurāṇa [[(chap, 818) destribes|(chap. 218) describes]] the procedure of the king's coronation and (chapter 219) sets out the [[Pauranika|Paurāṇika]] mantras (about 70) employed at the coronation. Similarly, the [[Vigṇudharmottara|Viṣṇudharmottara]] (II. 21) describes the procedure of coronation with Vedic mantras and [[(in II. 22 )|(in II. 22)]] with 184 Paurāṇika [[Verses|verses]] invoking various gods, minor deities, sages, rivers [[&o.|&c.]] Medieval digests like the [[Rājanitiprakāśa|Rājanītiprakāśa]] (pp. 49–83), Nītimayūkha (pp. 1-4), Rājadharmakaustubha (pp. 318-363) describe the combined coronation procedure of Vedic and [[Paurāpika|Paurāṇika]] mantras from the [[Visnu-dharmottara|Viṣṇu-dharmottara]] (vide pp. 78-79 of the [[H. of Dh,|H. of Dh.]] vol. III. for details). The [[Rājanitiprakāśa|Rājanītiprakāśa]] (pp. 430-433) prescribes numerous mantras derived from the [[Visaudharmottara|Viṣṇudharmottara]] as prayers and as blessings.

The Padmapurāṇa[^1473] narrates the interesting story of a person called Dhanaśarmā whose father followed only the Vedic path [[(śrauta-raārga),|(śrauta-mārga),]] who did not engage in such Paurāṇika prescriptions as Vaiśākhasnāna and who therefore became a horrible and distressed preta. Some of the verses are very [[inter esting.|interesting.]] 'I performed in my ignorance only Vedic rites and I never observed [[Vaisakhasnāna|Vaiśākhasnāna]] in honour of God Mādhava [[(Vispu),|(Viṣṇu),]] nor observed a single Vaiśākha Full Moon vrata which is like an axe for cutting the tree of sins that afflicts one like a conflagration fed by [[the- fuel|the fuel]] of sinful deeds &c.; to one who studies many śāstras and several Vedas with their extensive ancillary [[Literature,|literature,]] learning does not come, if he has not studied Purāṇas'. This shows what importance came to be attached to Purāṇas not only for śūdras but even for brāhmaṇas who performed the Vedic rites prescribed for them.

[^1473]: [[\\#9 \\#hutaaminifoni teza e a face ATE ATT वैविक|मया केवलमेवैकश्रौतमार्गानुसारिणा । उद्दिश्य माधवं देवं न स्नातं मासि माधवे ॥ वैदिकं]] केवलं कर्म कृतमज्ञानतो मया । [[पापेन्धनववज्वालापापवमकुठारिका। कृषा नकापि|पापेन्धनदवज्वालापापद्रुमकुठारिका ॥ कृता नैकापि]] वैशाखी विधिना वत्स पूर्णिमा । [[अनता|अव्रता]] यस्य वैशाखी सोऽवैशाखो [[भवेक्षर ।|भवेन्नरः ।]] दश जन्मानि च स ततस्तिर्यग्योनिषु जायते ॥ [[प|पद्म]] IV. 94. 68. 88-90; [[पहुशाक|बहुशास्त्रं]] समभ्यस्य [[परवेदान् Hide Tayatsgagtiurry Fourrela ustan y|बहून्वेदान् सविस्तरान् । पुंसोऽश्रुतपुराणस्य न सम्यग्याति दर्शनम् ॥ पद्म]] IV. 105. 13.

The influence of the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] went on increasing gradually. At first it was said[^1474] that the dharma understood from the Veda was the highest, while the dharma declared in the Purāṇas [[P924]] and the like was inferior. This gradually changed and dharma was said to be of three kinds, Vaidika, [[Tantrika|Tāntrika]] and [[Mista|Miśra]] and it was said in the Bhāgavata[^1475] and the Padma that one may worship god [[Vispu|Viṣṇu]] in any one of the three ways that one desired. The Padma adds that the Vaidika and Miśraka methods are declared to be proper for [[brāhmanas|brāhmaṇas]] and the like, while the [[Tantrika|Tāntrika]] method of worship is meant for a devotee of Viṣṇu and also for śūdras. The [[Devibhāgavata|Devībhāgavata]] (XI. 1. 21–23) states that [[Sruti|Śruti]] (Veda) and [[Smrti|Smṛti]] are the eyes of dharma and Purāṇa its heart, and that that is dharma which is declared by these three and that dharma [[oan|can]] be found nowhere else than in these, that in [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] sometimes what is found in Tantras is put forward [[&8|as]] dharma, but one should not accept that.

[^1474]: [[Sta: # UCHT DAT Haarefootage|अतः स परमो धर्मो यो वेदादधिगम्यते ।]] [[Ma: Fa Pantai : gaunay Fra: 11 h 4. by Step. 9, . On a I, p. 22, H entat p. 29.|अवरः स तु विज्ञेयो यः पुराणादिषु स्मृतः ॥ व्यास q. by अपरार्क p. 9, हे. on व्रत I. p. 22, परिभाषाप्रकाश p. 29.]] The [[कृ... p. 39|कृ. र. p. 39]] reads 'अपरः स तु विज्ञेयो'. It may be noted that while [[अपाक|अपरार्क]] read [[T:|अवरः]] (inferior), the [[6. 5.|कृ. र.]] (about two centuries later than [[Sta)|अपरार्क)]] [[reada T:|reads अपरः]] [[(apother).|(another).]]

[^1475]: वैदिकस्तान्त्रिको मिश्र इति मे [[विविधो|त्रिविधो]] मखः । [[प्रयाणामीप्सितेनैव|त्रयाणामीप्सितेनैव]] विधिना मां [[FTAT \* \*XI. 27.7.9. by Fargrarrafa p. 510; \*|समर्चयेत् ॥ भागवत XI. 27. 7, q. by नित्याचारपद्धति p. 510; पद्म]] (IV. 90. 3-4) reads [[वैदिक"श्रीविष्णोतिषियो|वैदिक ... श्रीविष्णोस्त्रिविधो]] मखः । त्रयाणामुदितेनैव विधिना हरिमर्चयेत् ॥ [[वैविको|वैदिको]] मिश्रको वापि [[Paar t : 1 a Facort For salfaat: .|विप्रादीनामुदाहृतः । तान्त्रिको विष्णुभक्तस्य शूद्रस्यापि प्रकीर्तितः ॥.]] Vide [[range|अग्निपु०]] 372. 34 for almost the same words as in भागवत. Compare [[वृबहारीतस्मृति 1.77|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति 11. 77]] '[[श्रौत. स्माागमविष्णोत्रिविधं पूजन स्मृतम् । एतच्छ्रोतं ततः स्माते पौरुषेण च यत्स्मृतम् ।|श्रौतस्मार्तागमैर्विष्णोस्त्रिविधं पूजनं स्मृतम् । एतच्छ्रौतं ततः स्मार्तं पौरुषेण च यत्स्मृतम् ॥'.]]

The Bhaviṣya (Brāhmaparva [[I, 43-47)|I. 43–47)]] in a dialogue between [[Satānika|Śatānīka]] and [[Sumantu)|Sumantu]] first enumerates the 18 dharma-śāstras from Manu to Atri, states that the Vedas, the śāstras of Manu and [[otbers|others]] and the [[angas|aṅgas]] are promulgated for the three [[varpas|varṇas]] and not for the benefit of śūdras, that śūdras appear to be very much helpless; how can they be able to secure the four puruṣārthas? They are devoid of [[agama|āgama]] (traditional [[loro);|lore);]] what traditional lore was declared for them by the wise among the brāhmaṇas for enabling them to secure the three viz. dharma, artha and [[kama?|kāma?]] Sumantu replies:[^1476] listen to the [[Dharma Śāstras|Dharmaśāstras]] that were declared by wise [[mon|men]] for the benefit of all four [[Varpas|Varṇas]] and specially for Śūdras viz. the 18 Purāṇas, the life of Rāma of the Raghu race (i. e. the Rāmāyaṇa) and the Bhārata [[P925]] declared by Parāśara's son [[(Vyasa);|(Vyāsa);]] the compassionate [[Vyasa|Vyāsa]] composed a śāstra for the benefit of all four [[Varnas|Varṇas]] in which he comprehended the entire meaning of the Veda and the [[Dharmen sāstras;|Dharmaśāstras;]] it is an unparalleled boat for all that are engulfed in saṁsāra. This makes it clear that the Purāṇas, the [[Mahābhā. rata|Mahābhārata]] and the Rāmāyaṇa embody pristine traditions and [[thoughts:|thoughts]] and were deemed to have been composed as the instruments of the education and enlightenment of the common people. As [[&|a]] matter of fact we find that some Purāṇas like Agni, Matsya, Viṣṇudharmottara are [[encyclopaedio|encyclopaedic]] and include treatises on politics and government, on law, medicine, astronomy, [[astro logy,|astrology,]] poetry, music, [[soulpture|sculpture]] &c. They illustrate India's life and character as a country and exemplify [[Bharata's|Bhārata's]] achievements, weaknesses and shortcomings. Two questions that arise are: (1) could the [[Pusāpas|Purāṇas]] including the Vedic mantras quoted therein be read by the śūdras themselves; [[(%)|(2)]] supposing that Vedic mantras could not be read by the śūdras, could they, [[it'|if]] they were able to do so, by themselves read the purāṇas without a [[brāhmana's|brāhmaṇa's]] help. All writers of digests and [[commentarios|commentaries]] were agreed that śūdras could not read or listen to the Vedic mantras contained in the Purāṇas (which being meant for the benefit of all [[yarpas|varṇas]] contained Vedic mantras also), but only those that belonged to the three upper classes. Some writers, however, were agreeable to śūdras reciting Paurāṇika mantras in religious rites, relying on a passage of the Padmapurāṇa. But others like [[Kamalakarabhatta,|Kamalākarabhaṭṭa,]] author of the [[Nirnayasindhu|Nirṇayasindhu]] and [[Sūdrakamalakara,|Śūdrakamalākara,]] held relying on verses of the Bhaviṣya that Paurāṇika mantras alone were to be used by a brāhmaṇa [[in a,|in a]] religious rite for a śūdra, that the śūdra was only to listen to the reading of a purāṇa by a [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] reader. There was a third view held by [[Sridatta|Śrīdatta]] and others that a śūdra could recite a paurāṇika mantra, but he should not himself read the Purāṇas and should only listen to the reading of Purāṇas by a [[brāhmane|brāhmaṇa.]] In the times of the Dharmasūtras the only mantras employed were mostly Vedic and therefore in the case of śūdras Gautama[^1477] provided that the śūdras were allowed the [[alter native|alternative]] of saying 'namaḥ' in place of a Vedic mantra. In the centuries preceding Christ the śūdras would naturally have been attracted to Buddha's teaching as it was addressed to all including śūdras. Comparatively early orthodox writers like [[P926]] Kumārila [[know|knew]] that the śūdras formed the largest number of professed Buddhists when he says[^1478] 'the dicta of [[Sakya|Śākya]] and others were all opposed, except a [[fow|few]] relating to self-restraint, charity and the like, to all the fourteen [[gources|sources]] of learning, were promulgated by Buddha and others that [[bad|had]] strayed from the path of the three Vedas and did acts contrary (to the Veda) and that those dicta were presented by them to those who were deluded, who were outside the pale of the three Vedas, who mostly comprised the 4th varṇa (i. e. śūdra) and those that had lost caste'. Therefore, the learned brāhmaṇas who wanted to wean sections of the masses (including śūdras) away from Buddhist teachings composed [[now|new]] Paurāṇika mantras by the thousand and employed them in all religious rites like śrāddhas, vratas &c. It was, therefore, that earlier nibandha writers like [[Śridatta|Śrīdatta]] were prepared to allow śūdras to recite Paurāṇika[^1479] mantras. But when centuries had elapsed after Buddhism had disappeared from India, orthodox [[brābmana|brāhmaṇa]] writers like [[Kamalakara|Kamalākara]][^1480] (who wrote his N. S. in 1612 A. D.) showed a stiffer attitude by confining śūdras merely to the listening to Purāṇas read by a brāhmaṇa and by not allowing them even to recite a Paurāṇika mantra. It may be noted that the [[Nara simhapurāṇa|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]] in laying down the duties of śūdras provides that they should listen to the reading of purāṇas by a [[brābmapa|brāhmaṇa]] [[P927]] (reader) and should worship [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] (avatāra of [[Viṣṇu),|Viṣṇu).]] The relation of Śruti, [[smrti|Smṛti]] and [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] and their [[spheros|spheres]] of application are summed up in the [[Naradiyapurāṇa|Nāradīyapurāṇa]] [[as follows:|as follows:—]] "The Veda exists in different forms; there is the Veda which has the performance of sacrificial rites (as its sphere); [[smrtis ara|smṛtis are]] the Veda for the householder's stage; both those are centered in (or supported by) the [[Puranas.|Purāṇas.]] Just as this wonderful world sprang from the ancient Being (God), there is no doubt that all this literature arose from the [[Purāṇag.|Purāṇas.]] I hold that the meaning (or purpose) of the Purāṇas is more extensive (or superior to) than the meaning (or purpose) of the Veda. All the [[Vedai|Vedas]] always rest on the Purāṇas; the Veda is afraid of the man of little learning (with the thought that) that man may harm it. The Veda does not deal with the movements of the [[planeta,|planets,]] nor does it contain correct calculations about the proper [[times ( for|times (for]] religious acts), nor does it deal with the [[tithivrddhi|tithivṛddhi]] or [[tithikaya,|tithikṣaya,]] nor with the determination of the [[partans|parvans]] (amāvāsyā, [[pūrnima|pūrṇimā]] &c.) or eclipses. Determinations about these were formerly made in Itihāsa and Purāṇas. What is not seen in the Vedas is all noticed in the [[Smstis;|Smṛtis;]] what is [[nog geen|not seen]] in both is declared in the [[Puranas.|Purāṇas.]] What is declared by the Vedas and what is declared by the auxiliary lores [[--|—]] it is [[Voda|Veda]] that is declared by [[Smśtis|Smṛtis]] and [[Purāpas.|Purāṇas.]] The person who looks upon [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] in any other light would be born as a lower animal'.[^1481] The [[Nāradiyapurāṇa|Nāradīyapurāṇa]][^1482] further states 'the merit acquired by those wicked men who speak about Purāṇas as arthavādas [[(more|(mere]] laudatory[^1483] or condemnatory statements) are destroyed and [[P928]] the wicked man who regards as arthavādas the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] that are the means of uprooting [[( the|(the]] evil effects of) all [[acta,|acts,]] reaches Hell'.

[^1476]: चतुर्णामपि वर्णानां यानि [[मोक्तानि|प्रोक्तानि]] श्रेयसे । धर्मशास्त्राणि राजेन्द्र शृणु तानि [[सुपोत्तम|नृपोत्तम ॥]] विशेषतश्च [[शुद्धा|शूद्राणां]] पावनानि मनीषिभिः [[।|॥]] अष्टादश पुराणानि चरितं राघवस्य च । रामस्य [[कुमशार्दूल|कुरुशार्दूल]] धर्मकामार्थसिद्धये । [[तथोक्त|तथोक्तं]] भारतं वीर पाराशर्येण धीमता । [[दार्थ सकलं योग्य धर्मशाखाणि|वेदार्थं सकलं योज्य धर्मशास्त्राणि]] च प्रभो । [[कृपालना|कृपालुना]] कृतं शास्त्रं चतुर्णामिह श्रेयसे । वर्णानां भवमग्नानां कृतं [[niat ugungi fervu, argua 1, 53-57; . Pr. ml. p. 66 quotes the verses Ber f i . The weare|पोतो ह्यनुत्तमम् । भविष्य, ब्राह्मपर्व 1. 53–57; आ. क्रि. कौ. p. 66 quotes the verses अष्टादश ... धीमता. The भागवत]] also says that the [[wbole|whole]] meaning of the Veda was put forward under the name [[Bbārata,|Bhārata,]] in which [[eveo|even]] women [[nad kūdras|and śūdras]] find what dharma is : [[' raro questa maraving fora: 1 9 WAIT Antiina Hurria|'भारतव्यपदेशेन ह्याम्नायार्थश्च दर्शितः । दृश्यते यत्र धर्मादि स्त्रीशूद्रादिभिरप्युत ॥ भागवत]] I. 4. 29. Vide above p. 870 and note 1408 about the lack of [[aatiquity ja|antiquity in]] the [[c489|case]] of this passage from [[Bhavipya|Bhaviṣya]] [[( Brahmaparva),|(Brāhmaparva).]]

[^1478]: [[शाक्याविवचनानि|शाक्यादिवचनानि]] तु कतिपयदमदानादिवचनवर्जं सर्वाण्येव [[समस्तचतर्वशविद्या स्थानविरुद्धानि बीमार्गम्युत्थितविरुद्धाचरणेश्च|समस्तचतुर्दशविद्यास्थानविरुद्धानि त्रयीमार्गव्युत्थितैस्तद्विरुद्धाचरणैश्च]] बुद्धादिभिः प्रणीतानि । [[त्रयीवाोग्यश्चतुर्य वर्णनिरखसिवमायेग्यो ध्यामूढेभ्यः समर्पितानीति|त्रयीबाह्येभ्यश्चतुर्थवर्णनिरवसितप्रायेभ्यो व्यामूढेभ्यः समर्पितानीति]] न वेदमूलत्वेन [[सम्भाग्यन्ते।|सम्भाव्यन्ते ।]] तन्त्रवार्तिक on जै. I. 3. 4 p. 195 [[(Anan).|(Ānan).]] The 14 विद्यास्थानs are numerated in [[या. 1.3|या. I. 3]] quoted above and in भविष्य [[(बाहापर्व|(ब्राह्मपर्व]] 2. 6). Four more विद्यास्थानs are sometimes added, viz आयुर्वेदो धनुर्वेदो [[गान्धर्वश्चैव ते वयः । अर्थशास्त्रं चतुर्थ तु विद्या हटावशेष ता: भविन्य (मा)|गान्धर्वश्चैव ते त्रयः । अर्थशास्त्रं चतुर्थं तु विद्या ह्यष्टादशैव ताः ॥ भविष्य (ब्राह्म)]] 2. 7 and विष्णुपु० III. 6. 28. This verse and [[अनि वेदाश्चत्वारः fang UI,|अङ्गानि वेदाश्चत्वारः (विष्णु III. 6. 27)]] are quoted by [[arratt (aerano p. 2) and by . On an vol. I. p. 18 and [..|कल्पतरु (ब्रह्मचारि० p. 2) and by हे. on व्रत vol. I. p. 18 and कृ. र.]] p. 27. For [[निरवासित|निरवसित]] meaning बहिष्कृत, vide पाणिनि [['शुदाणाम निरवसिवानाम्|'शूद्राणामनिरवसितानाम्']] II. 4. 10 and the [[महाभाग्य|महाभाष्य]] thereon.

[^1479]: अत्र केचित् । [[वैदिकमन्त्री विषस्य पौराणस्तु शूवैः पठनीयः- न हि वेदेवाधि काः कचिमच्दस्य|वैदिकमन्त्रो विप्रस्य पौराणस्तु शूद्रैः पठनीयः — न हि वेदेष्वधिकारः क्वचिच्छूद्रस्य]] दृश्यते । पुराणेष्वधिकारो मे [[दशितो बाहाकारह-इति तत्रैव पायोके रित्यागोडा|दर्शितो ब्राह्मणैरिह — इति तत्रैव पाद्मोक्तेरित्याहुः । गौडा]] अप्येवम् । नि. सि. p. 392.

[^1480]: [[अध्येतन्यं|अध्येतव्यं]] ब्राह्मणेन वैश्येन क्षत्रियेण च । श्रोतव्यमेव [[शूदेण|शूद्रेण]] नाध्येतव्यं कदाचन । [[मौत स्माते चबै धर्म प्रोक्तमस्मिन्पोत्तम । तस्माईविना विप्रं न श्रोतव्यं कदाचनेति|श्रौतं स्मार्तं च वै धर्मं प्रोक्तमस्मिन्नृपोत्तम । तस्माच्छूद्रैर्विना विप्रं न श्रोतव्यं कदाचनेति]] तत्रैव पुराणाधिकारे भविष्योक्तेश्च । [[तेन|... तेन]] पौराणमन्त्राणामेव विप्रेण [[पाठोन वैदिकानामिति|पाठो न वैदिकानामिति]] सिद्धम् । नि. सि. p. 392. The printed भविष्य [[(बाहा)|(ब्राह्म)]] 1. 72-74 are [[आयेतण्यं न चाम्येन माह्मण क्षत्रिय बिना । श्रोतव्यमेव शुद्धण नाध्येतव्यं कदाचन । "श्रौतं स्मात "कदाचन ॥ These are q. by श्रा, क्रि. को. p. 16. पुराणप्रवर्ण विभावरसिंहस्य|अध्येतव्यं न चान्येन ब्राह्मणं क्षत्रियं विना । श्रोतव्यमेव शूद्रेण नाध्येतव्यं कदाचन । ... श्रौतं स्मार्तं ... कदाचन ॥ These are q. by आ. क्रि. कौ. p. 76. पुराणश्रवणं विप्रान्नरसिंहस्य]] पूजनम् । नरसिंह [[पण|पु०]] 58. 13.

[^1481]: [[एणु मोहिनि महाक्य घेदीयं|शृणु मोहिनि मद्वाक्यं वेदोयं]] बहुधा स्थितः । यज्ञकर्मक्रिया [[घेवः स्मृतिको महाभमे ॥ स्पतिवेदः क्रियायेदः|वेदः स्मृतिर्वेदो गृहाश्रमे ॥ स्मृतिर्वेदः क्रियावेदः]] पुराणेषु प्रतिष्ठितः । [[पुराणपुरुषाजातं यथेदं जगवतम् । वये पाखाय जात पुराणेभ्यो|पुराणपुरुषाज्जातं यथेदं जगदद्भुतम् ॥ तथेदं वाङ्मयं जातं पुराणेभ्यो]] न संशयः । वेदार्थादधिकं मन्ये पुराणार्थं वरानने [[। वेदाः प्रविधिमा सर्ने|॥ वेदाः प्रतिष्ठिताः सर्वे]] पुराणेष्वेव सर्वदा । [[विभेत्यल्पश्चताबेदो मामयं महरिष्यति ॥ न वेदे महसधारोन दिः काल बोधिनी ।|बिभेत्यल्पश्रुताद्वेदो मामयं प्रहरिष्यति ॥ न वेदे ग्रहसञ्चारो न शुद्धिः कालबोधिनी ।]] तिथिवृद्धिः क्षयो वापि पर्वग्रहविनिर्णयः ॥ [[इतिहासपुराणस्त निश्चयो त पूरा दिवेदेषु तत्सर्व लक्ष्यते सती । उभयोर्यन र हितपुराणैः मणीयते। "पदैनीम पत्र उपागीयते । पुराणैः स्मृतिमिव वेद एव नियते । पुराणमन्यथा मत्था निर्मायोनिमयाप्त पात् ।|इतिहासपुराणैस्तु निश्चयोयं कृतः पुरा । यन्न दृष्टं हि वेदेषु तत्सर्वं लक्ष्यते स्मृतौ ॥ उभयोर्यन्न दृष्टं हि तत्पुराणैः प्रणीयते । ... यद्वेदैर्गीयते सुभ्रु उपाङ्गैर्यत्प्रगीयते ॥ पुराणैः स्मृतिभिश्चैव वेद एव निगद्यते । पुराणमन्यथा मत्वा तिर्यग्योनिमवाप्नुयात् ॥]] नारदीयपुराण II. 24. 15-24. Some of these verses (such as [[बेवा मसिहवा "मामयं चालविष्यति and यस गीयते) oceur ia|वेदाः प्रतिष्ठिताः ... मामयं चालयिष्यति and यन्न दृष्टं ... गीयते) occur in]] स्कन्द, प्रभासखण्ड, 2. 90-92.

[^1482]: पुराणेष्वर्थवादत्वं ये वदन्ति [[नराधमा जितानि पुण्यानि कार्य प्रान्ति हिजो तमाः । समस्तकर्मनिर्मूलसाधनानि नराधमाः । पुराणापर्थवादेन (पुराणाम्यवादेन!) पुन नरकमाइते । नारदीय 1|नराधमाः । तैरर्जितानि पुण्यानि क्षयं यान्ति द्विजोत्तमाः । समस्तकर्मनिर्मूलसाधनानि नराधमाः । पुराणाम्प्यर्थवादेन (पुराणान्यर्थवादेन !) ब्रुवन् नरकमश्नुते ॥ नारदीय I.]] 1. 57-59.

[^1483]: The great [[referensirop|अर्थवादाधिकरण]] is [[.|जै.]] I. 2. 1-18. There are [[vedic passagea|Vedic passages]] like 'सोऽरोदीद्यदरोदीत्तद्रुद्रस्य [[दत्वम्। (ते. से. I. 5.1.1), 'सामना पासवश्विदत् (है.सं.1, 1.1), 'देवा देवयजनमभ्यवसाय विशो नमामानन्।.सं.|रुद्रत्वम्' (तै. सं. I. 5. 1. 1), 'स आत्मनो वपामुदक्खिदत्' (तै. सं. II. 1. 1), 'देवा वै देवयजनमध्यवसाय दिशो न प्राजानन्' (तै. सं.]] VI. 1. 5. 1), [[la arengt utsatta lat. . V. 3. 12. 2).|'तरति ब्रह्महत्यां योऽश्वमेधेन यजते' (तै. सं. V. 3. 12. 2),]] [['#grereppe fragt frantaa ari. #. V, 2. 7.|'न पृथिव्यामग्निश्चेतव्यो नान्तरिक्षे न दिवि । तै. सं. V. 2. 7.]] The question is: are these passages to be taken literally or do they [[coovey|convey]] any meaning? The reply is: [['ranta YAE ardal frentat el: ' ( 6. 1.2.7)|'विधिना त्वेकवाक्यत्वात् स्तुत्यर्थेन विधीनां स्युः' (जै. I. 2. 7)]] [[1. e,|i. e.]] they are [[landatory|laudatory]] and are meant to praise vidhis.

The Purāṇas introduced several striking changes in the religious rites, practices and ideals of the people. The most characteristic thought and the keynote of the Purāṇas [[ia|is]] to declare how great rewards and results could be secured with little effort. The Viṣṇupurāṇa (VI. 2) narrates how sages approached [[Vyāga|Vyāsa]] with the question [[in|'in]] what age does a little [[ildharma|dharma]] yield very great rewards'? Vyāsa was bathing in the Ganges; he came out, uttered 'śūdra is good and Kali is good' and then again plunged into the river; then he again came out and said 'well done, O [[Śūdra!|śūdra!]] you are blessed'; he again plunged into the river, came out and said 'women are good and blessed; who is more blessed than they'. When he finished his bath and performed his morning rites, the sages asked him to explain what he meant by calling Kali, śūdras and women good and blessed. He replied: 'a man secures in a [[sipgle|single]] day and night in Kali age as much reward of [[lapus,|tapas,]] celibacy and japa as is obtained in ten years in [[Kr̥ta|Kṛta]] age, in one year in Tretā and in a month in [[Dyāpara;|Dvāpara;]] therefore, I spoke of Kali as good; in Kali age a man secures merely by the glorification or incessant repetition of the name of Keśava what he would secure by deep meditation in Kṛta, by sacrifices in Tretā, and by worship in Dvāpara; I am pleased with Kali because a man secures great eminence of dharma with a little effort. Persons of the three higher varṇas have to study the Vedas after observing many [[atrict|strict]] rules, then they have to perform sacrifices which require wealth; they incur sin if they do not perform their duties properly; they cannot eat and drink as they please, but are dependent on the observance of many rules as to food &c.; dvijas secure higher worlds after great trouble; the śūdra secures his worlds by serving the [[thres Varnas,|three varṇas,]] he has the right to offer the pākayajñas (without mantras) and therefore he is more blessed than a dvija. He has not to observe strict rules about proper and disallowed food or drink and therefore he [[wag|was]] [[P929]] declared 'good' by me. A woman by serving her husband in thought, word, and deed secures with [[legs|less]] trouble the same worlds that her husband secures with great effort and trouble and therefore I said a third time about women that they were blessed. The [[acquistion|acquisition]] of dharma is secured with small trouble in Kali age by men who wash off all their sins by the water in the form of the qualities of their soul; śūdras do the same by being intent on service to dvijas and women also secure the [[Bame|same]] without trouble by service to their husbands. Therefore all these three are regarded by me as most blessed.'[^1484] The [[Brahmapurana|Brahmapurāṇa]] chapter 229 verses 62-80 are identical with [[Viṣṇu purana|Viṣṇu-purāṇa]] VI. 2. 15-30 and 34-36. The [[Viṣṇupurana|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] emphasizes that each one must do one's duty in the society in which one is born or one's duty which one has undertaken, that, if a person does this, he reaches the [[game|same]] higher worlds, whether he be a brāhmaṇa or a [[gūdra.|śūdra.]] This doctrine is the same as taught in the [[Bhagavadgita|Bhagavadgītā]][^1485] 18. 45 and 46 'a person secures the highest perfection (final emancipation) by being intent on carrying out the duties appropriate to him; man secures perfection (or [[bllag)|bliss)]] by worshipping with the performance of his peculiar duties [[( not|(not]] with flowers and the like or by words) Him from whom all beings proceed and by whom all this (world) is enveloped'. Ancient works like the Vedas, Jaimini's sūtras on [[Mimānsā|Mīmāṁsā]] and the [[Vedāntasutras|Vedāntasūtras]] did not consider or discuss how women or śūdras were to secure higher spiritual life and final Beatitude. The [[Vedantasutra|Vedāntasūtra]] [[(I|(I.]] 3. 34-38) denies to the śūdra the right to study the Veda and the Upaniṣads. Buddha's teaching held out the same promise of liberation from suffering [[P930]] to all men irrespective of class or [[oaste|caste]] and was therefore most attractive to śūdras. The [[Bhagavadgitā|Bhagavadgītā]] and the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] changed the whole outlook of Indian society, high or low, and promised the same higher spiritual life or worlds to all who [[dia|did]] their work under a [[senge|sense]] of social duty, did not hanker after mundane rewards and brought all their actions, in whatever avocation they might be [[ongaged,|engaged,]] as an offering to God. In the [[Padmapurāpa|Padmapurāṇa]][^1486] Vyāsa is made to say to [[Yudhisthira:|Yudhiṣṭhira:]] "It is not possible to observe in the Kali age the rules of Dharma laid down by Manu and by the Vedas; the one thing which one should do is to observe a fast on Ekādaśī in both fortnights [[(of a month ),|(of a month),]] which is an easy means (that) requires little wealth, that entails little trouble, but yields great rewards, that is the very essence (of the teachings) of all Purāṇas; he should be pure and on [[Dyādaśī|Dvādaśī]] after worshipping Keśava with flowers [[be|he]] should first feed [[brāhmanas|brāhmaṇas]] and then himself take his meal; those who desire to secure heaven should perform this vrata throughout their lives; even persons of evil conduct, the greatest sinners devoid of dharma, do not go to Yama [[( do|(do]] not fall into hell) if they fast on [[Ekādasi."|Ekādaśī."]] The [[Sūtasarhitā|Sūtasaṁhitā]] states 'effort for acquiring true knowledge (of the Self) is meant for all (for persons even lower than śūdras), that effort made by explaining in a different language (than Sanskrit) and by the lapse of enough time will tend to the good [[( of|(of]] the lowest)'. This clearly shows how the Purāṇas put before all people easy ways whereby they could attain bliss in the [[Hereafter,|Hereafter.]]

[^1484]: Some of the verses are striking and they are therefore quoted here: [[ज्यायन्कृते यजन्य नेतायो|ध्यायन्कृते यजन्यज्ञैस्त्रेतायां]] द्वापरेऽर्चयन् । [[यदामोति तदामोति कलौ संकीय केशवम्|यदाप्नोति तदाप्नोति कलौ संकीर्त्य केशवम् ॥]] [[धर्मोत्कर्षमतीवात्र पामोति|धर्मोत्कर्षमतीवात्र प्राप्नोति]] पुरुषः कलौ । [[अल्पायासेन धर्मज्ञास्तेन तुष्टोस्म्यहं काले, "जयन्ति से निजाँलोकान् केशेन महता द्विजाः। द्विजाभूषवेष पाकयज्ञाधिकारवान । निजाचायति लोकान सूबो धन्यतरस्ततः॥ भक्ष्याभल्येषु नास्यास्ति पेयापेयेषुबै पतः । नियमो निशाला सेनासौ सावितीरितः। "योषिकुषणावर्तुः कर्मणा मनसा गिरा । तद्धिता गुममामोति वसा लोक्यं यतो द्विजाः । नातिक्लेशेन महता सानेव पुरुषो यथा । तृतीयं व्याहतं तेन मया साचिति योषितः "स्वल्पेन हि प्रयत्नेन धर्मः सिष्यति वै कलो। नरैरात्मणाम्भोभिः शालिवाखिल किल्मिषेः॥ शुबैश्च विजयुषा तत्परैविजसत्तमाः । वथा नीभिरनायासात् पतिजभूषयैव हि। ततत्रितयमप्येतन्मम धन्यतरं मतम् । धर्मसम्पादने क्लेशो द्विजातीनां कृताविषु। विष्णुपु.|अल्पायासेन धर्मज्ञास्तेन तुष्टोस्म्यहं कलेः ॥ ... जयन्ति ते निजाँल्लोकान् क्लेशेन महता द्विजाः ॥ द्विजशुश्रूषयैवैष पाकयज्ञाधिकारवान् । निजाञ्जयति वै लोकान् शूद्रो धन्यतरस्ततः ॥ भक्ष्याभक्ष्येषु नास्यास्ति पेयापेयेषु वै यतः । नियमो मुनिशार्दूलास्तेनासौ साध्वितीरितः । ... योषिच्छुश्रूषणाद्भर्तुः कर्मणा मनसा गिरा । तद्धिता शुभमाप्नोति तत्सालोक्यं यतो द्विजाः ॥ नातिक्लेशेन महता तानेव पुरुषो यथा । तृतीयं व्याहृतं तेन मया साध्विति योषितः ॥ ... स्वल्पेन हि प्रयत्नेन धर्मः सिध्यति वै कलौ । नरैरात्मगुणाम्भोभिः क्षालिताखिलकिल्बिषैः ॥ शूद्रैश्च द्विजशुश्रूषा तत्परैर्द्विजसत्तमाः । तथा स्त्रीभिरनायासात् पतिशुश्रूषयैव हि । ततस्त्रितयमप्येतन्मम धन्यतरं मतम् । धर्मसम्पादने क्लेशो द्विजातीनां कृतादिषु ॥ विष्णुपु०]] VI. 2. 17-18, 22-24, 28-28, 34-36. The [[कल्पतरू|कल्पतरु]] (गृहस्थकाण्ड pp. 272-273) quotes from [[पिण्Y VI. 3.22-24 पारतन्त्र्य"रितः (तम् .1.).|विष्णु VI. 3. 22–24 पारतन्त्र्य...रितः (तम् v. l.).]] The editor of कल्पतरु was unable to trace these.

[^1485]: [[खेखे कर्मण्यभिरतः संसिलिं लभते नरः। "यतः प्रवृत्तिभूतानां येन सनिक ततम् । सकर्मणा तमग्यचर्य सिद्धिविन्वति मानः॥ भगवदीता|स्वे स्वे कर्मण्यभिरतः संसिद्धिं लभते नरः । ... यतः प्रवृत्तिर्भूतानां येन सर्वमिदं ततम् । स्वकर्मणा तमभ्यर्च्य सिद्धिं विन्दति मानवः ॥ भगवद्गीता]] 18. 45-46.

[^1486]: [[बेदव्यास उवाच। श्रुताये मानवा धर्मा वैदिकाश्च भुतास्त्वया। कलो युगे न शक्यते ते वैक नराधिप । मुखोपायमल्पधनमल्पक्लेशं महाफलम् । पुराणानां च सर्वेषां सारभूत महामते। एकादश्यां न भुजीत पक्षयोरभयोरपि। द्वादश्यां तु शुचिर्भूत्वा पुष्पैः संपूज्य केशवम् ॥ सुजीत कृतकृत्यस्त पश्चादिप्रपुरस्सरन् । "यावजीवं व्रतमिदं कर्तव्यं पुरुषर्षभ। स्वर्गति प्राप्तुमिच्छबिरन मे नास्ति संशयः ॥ अपि पापदुराचाराः पापिष्ठा धर्मवजिताः । एकादश्यां न भुखन्ति न यान्ति Tarracial T VI. 53, 4-9.|वेदव्यास उवाच । श्रुता ये मानवा धर्मा वैदिकाश्च श्रुतास्तथा । कलौ युगे न शक्यन्ते ते वै कर्तुं नराधिप ॥ सुखोपायमल्पधनमल्पक्लेशं महाफलम् । पुराणानां च सर्वेषां सारभूतं महामते ॥ एकादश्यां न भुञ्जीत पक्षयोरुभयोरपि । द्वादश्यां तु शुचिर्भूत्वा पुष्पैः संपूज्य केशवम् ॥ भुञ्जीत कृतकृत्यस्तु पश्चाद्विप्रपुरःसरम् । ... यावज्जीवं व्रतमिदं कर्तव्यं पुरुषर्षभ । स्वर्गतिं प्राप्तुमिच्छद्भिरत्र मे नास्ति संशयः ॥ अपि पापदुराचाराः पापिष्ठा धर्मवर्जिताः । एकादश्यां न भुञ्जन्ति न ते यान्ति यमान्तिकम् ॥ पद्म VI. 53. 4–9.]] These verses are quoted as from [[RETTE by X. on A&|महाभारत by हे. on व्रत]] vol. I, p. 1089. Vide [[o|p]] 44 note 108 above for some of these verses; अन्येषामपि सर्वेषां ज्ञानाम्यासो विधीयते । भाषान्तरेण कालेन तेषां [[सोप्युपकारकः तसंहिता|सोप्युपकारकः ॥ सूतसंहिता]] I. 7. 22.

The Baud.[^1487] Dh. S., Manu and [[Vasistha|Vasiṣṭha]] emphasize that one should not invite a large company of brāhmaṇas at a śrāddha, because a large company destroys these five [[(advan tages)|(advantages)]] viz. showing proper respect to invitees, propriety of place and time, cleanliness and the securing of worthy brāhmaṇas and [[P931]] it was further provided by the [[Anusagana-parva|Anuśāsana-parva]][^1488] and others that one should not go deep into examining the learning, family and character of brāhmaṇas in a rite for the gods, but in rites for the [[Manog|Manes]] close examination as to these matters is proper (or justified). The Purāṇas went against both those [[prescrip tions.|prescriptions.]] They are not tired of recommending again and again profuse expense in [[grāddhas|śrāddhas]] and [[condemaning|condemning]] stinginess (lit. roguishness in spending money) in śrāddhas and also in such other matters as the observances of [[Ekādasi.|Ekādaśī.]] For example, the Viṣṇupurāṇa[^1489] quotes nine verses (III. 14. 22-30) as uttered by the pitṛs, two of which may be translated here 'Would that [[&|a]] wise and blessed person be born in our family who not [[indulg ing|indulging]] in stinginess in spending wealth will offer [[pindas|piṇḍas]] to us and who would donate to brāhmaṇas for our sake jewels, clothes, a large conveyance, wealth and all enjoyments if he has riches'! Padma[^1490] recommends that avoiding [[stinginoss|stinginess]] brings pleasure to the [[pitys.|pitṛs.]] The Matsya (56. 11) prescribes that one should not show stinginess in the [[Kļspāstamivrata.|Kṛṣṇāṣṭamīvrata.]] The Padma says that that bad man who being possessed of wealth celebrates the jāgara on Ekādaśī in a close-fisted way loses his soul. The Brahmapurāṇa[^1491] says in a general way that whoever does a religious act with stinginess is a sinner.

II. 4,30, FAX|कूर्मपुराण II. 22. 27, बौ. ध. सू. II. 4. 30, वसिष्ठ]] XI. 28 (the last two read the 4th pāda as [['TRAP fetal.|'तस्मात्तं परिवर्जयेत्').]]

ON A P 511; da TENT Hai FYRST #RI Paguda. 82. 1-2; 2 EMIRE TE FT T Ara 14 EHTOT Tosa que quiera i el|ब्राह्मणान्न परीक्षेत क्षत्रियो दानधर्मवित् । दैवे कर्मणि पित्र्ये तु न्याय्यमाहुः परीक्षणम् ॥ अनुशासन 90. 2; q. by हे. on श्राद्ध p 511; दैवे कर्मणि ब्राह्मणं न परीक्षेत । प्रयत्नात्पित्र्ये परीक्षेत । विष्णुधर्मसू. 82. 1–2; न ब्राह्मणान् परीक्षेत सदा दैवे तु मानवः । दैवे कर्मणि पित्र्ये च श्रूयते वै परीक्षणम् । वायु]] 83. 51.

[^1491]: [[Romana otwarta FT T o 123. 174.|वित्तशाठ्येन यो धर्मं करोति स तु पातकी । ब्रह्म 123. 174.]]

Manu (III. 149) provides that 'in rites in honour of gods one who knows dharma should not [[oritically|critically]] examine the [[brāhmapas|brāhmaṇas]] to be invited for dinner, but in a rite for ancestors one should carefully investigate [[fitness of)| (the fitness of)]] [[brāhmadas'.|brāhmaṇas'.]] This does not mean that in rites for gods any one may be invited. We have to observe the general rule of Manu [[(II.|(III.]] 128) that donors should give only to a man who has studied the Veda [[P932]] [[dinger|dinner]] in rites for gods or [[pitra.|pitṛs.]] So what Manu III. 149 means is that deep investigation of ancestry [[&o,|&c.]] is not necessary in rites for [[gods,|gods.]]

It is provided by the Vāyu[^1492] that there is to be no [[exami. nation|examination]] of the qualities and character of the brāhmaṇas at Gayā, and the Varāhapurāṇa provides that all [[brāhmanas|brāhmaṇas]] of Mathurā are like gods and that a [[brāhmana|brāhmaṇa]] of Mathurā not knowing even a [[k|ṛk]] verse is superior to a brāhmaṇa of another place that has [[atudied|studied]] the four Vedas. Vide H. of Dh. vol. IV. pp. 579, 670 for details of [[Gaya|Gayā]] and Mathurā brāhmaṇas. The Padmapurāṇa and the Skanda (Kāśīkhaṇḍa 6. 56-57) recommend that at [[tirthas|tīrthas]] [[(Sacred|(sacred]] places) one should not engage in investigation [[the worth)|(of the worth)]] of [[brābmapas,|brāhmaṇas,]] and that Manu declares that brāhmaṇas appearing (at a tīrtha) and desiring food should be fed.[^1493]

'मथुरायां ये वसन्ति विष्णुरूपा हि ते नराः ॥ वराह]] 165. 55 and 57.

The first half is q. by H. fafi st. from morgere oa p. 34 and from igre OB p. 206.|तीर्थेषु ब्राह्मणं नैव परीक्षेत कथंचन । अन्नार्थिनमनुप्राप्तं भोज्यं तं मनुरब्रवीत् ॥ पद्म V. 29. 212. The first half is q. by आ. क्रि. कौ. from ब्रह्मपुराण on p. 34 and from देवीपुराण on p. 266.]]

It is not unlikely that the above passages from the Vāyu, [[Varāba|Varāha]] and Padma are later interpolations. When Buddhism was flourishing large companies of Buddhist monks were [[fod|fed]] by the people. When Buddhism declined and disappeared from India after the 12th or 13th century A. D. people came to believe that there was great merit in feeding poor brāhmaṇas just as in former times people fed monks and the Purāṇas might have only echoed and emphasized the general sentiments of the people. If the people in general had not come to believe in this, the author feels that no amount of interpolations or insistence by the [[so called|so-called]] crafty brāhmaṇas would have been effective. Writers, Western or Indian, that, relying on the notions current in the 19th and 20th centuries, pass severe and unmeasured strictures on the provisions for [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] in the Purāṇas do great injustice to the authors of Purāṇas that flourished about a thousand years ago or more. Such writers should compare Indian medieval conditions, ideas and doings of [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] with the claims of the Popes, Christian priests, Inquisitions and the state of monastic Orders in Europe from about the 10th century A. D. to the 15th century. In comparison it would be [[P933]] [['found|found]] that the conditions in Europe were terribly worse than in India in those [[conturier.|centuries.]][^1494]

[^1494]: For the barbarities and abominations in every European country of the Inquisition, particularly in Spain, one may read W. H. [[Rula's|Rule's]] [[' History|'History]] of the Inquisition' (1868), [[particulary Pp. 298–314 on Ix quisition|particularly pp. 298–314 on 'Inquisition]] in Goa' and Rafael Sabatini's work on [['Torquemeda|'Torquemada]] and the Spanish Inquisition' [[( 8th|(8th]] ed. in 1937), 'the Spanish Inquisition' by [[Prof, A, S,|Prof. A. S.]] Turberville [[( Home|(Home]] University Library, 1932), who is constrained to say on p. 235 that at the best the Holy Office in Spain [[bas|has]] a terrible record of destruction; Cambridge Medieval History vol. VI, [[cbap.|chap.]] XX on [['Harestes|'Heresies]] and the Inquisition in the Middle [[Agos'|Ages']] (1929) pp. 699–726; Cambridge Medieval History vol. VI. pp. 694–695 where it is shown that [[' Indulgencor'|'Indulgences']] [[(grantiog|(granting]] forgiveness of sins and a certificate of entry in Paradise) were freely put on sale by the highest ministers of the Christian Church in the [[bands|hands]] of licensed traders without the necessity of any confession and [[penit ence|penitence]] and that they became a formidable bar to the proper working of [[tha"|the]] penitential system of the Church.

As a consequence of the abovementioned dominating [[prinoiple|principle]] the Purāṇas strongly recommend dāna (gifts, [[parti oularly|particularly]] of food), pilgrimages and baths in sacred waters, vratas, [[ahimsa,|ahiṁsā,]] bhakti, repetition of the name of god, śrāddhas &c. These will be briefly illustrated below.

The Purāṇas institute a comparison between solemn Vedic [[Haorifices|sacrifices]] on the one hand and pilgrimages and baths at sacred places on the other. The Vanaparva[^1495] contrasts the two [[29|as]] follows, [["|"the]] solemn [[saorificos|sacrifices]] promulgated by the sages cannot be accomplished by a poor man; sacrifices require many [[imple. ments|implements]] and a collection of various materials, which are secured by kings or sometimes by rich men, but not by poor men who have to rely on themselves alone, who have no helpers and who do not possess means. Going to holy places [[conters|confers]] merit and [[Burpasses|surpasses]] sacrifices. One does not secure that reward by such sacrifices as Agniṣṭoma, in which profuse fees are distributed, which is secured by repairing to sacred [[places.|places."]] Vide H. of Dh. [[vol, IV|vol. IV]] pp. 561–564 for further encomiums of sacred places and the virtues to be cultivated for reaping the full merit of [[pilgrimages,|pilgrimages.]]

[^1495]: [[#ultramat got a Aforoven agaf 84.|तीर्थाभिगमनं पुण्यं यज्ञैरपि विशिष्यते । वनपर्व 82.]] 17; [[vida|vide]] H. of Dh. [[molo|vol.]] IV p. 561 n 1263 for the whole [[pannage,|passage.]]

In pursuance of the same dominant principle the [[Anusa. sana-parva|Anuśāsana-parva]] and Purāṇas extol fasts and [[vṛatas.|vratas.]] The [[Anuśādang|Anuśāsana-]]parva[^1496] [[P934]] [[(127. 5-6 )|(107. 5-6)]] remarks that fasts are equal to sacrifices in the matter of rewards. They are put forward as superior to sacrifices in Padma.[^1497] It says: Viṣṇuvrata is super-eminent; a hundred Vedic sacrifices are not equal to it; by performing a [[Bacrifice|sacrifice]] one may go to heaven, but one who observes the [[Kārti kavrata|Kārtikavrata]] goes to [[Vaikuntha|Vaikuṇṭha]] (the world of Viṣṇu). For the exaggerated importance attached to fasts and vratas, vide pp. 43-45 above.


[^1496]: [[इदमनिरसा|इदमङ्गिरसा]] प्रोक्तमुपवासफलात्मकम् । [[विधि|विद्धि]] [[यज्ञफलतुल्यं|यज्ञफलैस्तुल्यं]] [[तमिबोध|तन्निबोध]] युधिष्ठिर [[STLEHT|॥ अनुशासन]] 107. 5-6.

[^1497]: [[i fesuad for at a Ti prat: 1 ST F Segue Fuerte ano \#|श्रेष्ठं विष्णुव्रतं विप्र तत्तुल्या न शतं मखाः । कृत्वा क्रतुं व्रजेत्स्वर्गं वैकुण्ठं कार्तिकव्रती ॥ पद्म]] III. 21. 29. The same idea is repeated in [[\#|पद्म]] VI. 96. 25.

First dāna. High eulogies of gifts have been sung from the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] downwards. The subject of dāna (gifts) in all its aspects has been dealt with in the H. of Dh. vol. [[II,|II.]] pp. [[837-888,|837–888.]] The Mahābhārata in numerous places (particularly in the whole of Anuśāsana-parva) and the Purāṇas such as Matsya chap. [[82 92|82–92]] and 274–289, Agni chap. 208–213, Varāha 99-111, Padma V. 21. 81-213 (which agree almost verbatim with Matsya 83-92), Padma [[II,|II.]] 39-40 and 94, [[III,|III.]] 24, Kūrma II. 26 devote a great deal of space to the subject of [[dānns.|dānas.]] But here only two topics in relation to gifts will be dealt with, viz. [[.gifts|gifts]] of food and gifts to brāhmaṇas. The [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] condemns the person as merely a sinful one who does not offer food to the gods nor to his friends and uses it only to fill his own [[belly,|belly.]][^1498] The Ait. Br. and the Tai.[^1499] [[Br,|Br.]] speak of [[food'|'food']] [[asprāna'(life).|as 'prāṇa' (life).]] The Baud.[^1500] Dh. S. [[states" all|states "all]] beings depend on food, the Veda says 'food is life,' therefore food should be given [[to|(to]] others), food is the highest offering". Manu[^1501] and Viṣṇu Dh. S. [[state the|state 'the]] man who cooks food only for his own sake (and not for offering to gods and [[mon)|men)]] eats merely sin'. The [[Bhagavadgitā|Bhagavadgītā]] carries the same [[message those|message 'those]] who eat food [[loft|left]] after offering in sacrifice are [[frood|freed]] from all [[sids,|sins,]] but those who cook food for their sake alone eat sin'. The Padma has a fine passage 'those who always feed the cripple, the blind, children, old men, persons that are [[dis ogsed,|diseased,]] [[P935]] helpless and pinched by penury, always enjoy bliss in heaven; there is no end to the merit (accumulated) by [[con structing|constructing]] wells and tanks, where [[aquatio|aquatic]] animals and others moving on land drink water when they desire, for water is the life of living beings and life is centred in [[water.|water'.]] The [[distribu tion|distribution]] of food particularly to learned brāhmaṇas is highly praised in [[Brahma-parāpa|Brahma-purāṇa]][^1502] 218. 10-32, Padma V. 19. 289-307m, [[agai|Agni]] 211. 44-46. [[The|'The]] gift of food is superior to all gifts; food is the [[Tife|life]] of men, from food spring all beings; the worlds are [[depen dent|dependent]] on food, therefore food is praised; man secures heaven by the gift of food; a person who joyfully gives food acquired by just means to brāhmaṇas deeply learned in the Veda [[ia|is]] released from all [[sids'|sins']] says Brahma. The Agnipurāṇa says [["The|'The]] gifts of elephants, horses, chariots, male and female slaves and houses do not come up even to a sixteenth part of the gift of food (in merit); a person who committing [[&|a]] great sin afterwards distributes food becomes free from all sins and secures [[everlast ing|everlasting]] worlds' (211. 44-46). The Kūrma[^1503] [[prescribes a|prescribes 'a]] man should give to a [[brahmavārin|brahmacārin]] (Vedic student) food every day (i. e. when he comes begging); thereby he becomes free from all sins and reaches the abode of [[Brahma'.|Brahmā'.]] Similarly, Padma [[oalls|calls]] upon house-holders to give as much cooked food to ascetics as would fill the begging [[bowl|bowl.]] From very ancient times a [[house holder|householder]] was called upon to perform five Yajñas [[( sacrifices)|(sacrifices)]] daily, two of which were [[Balıharana|Baliharaṇa]] and honouring a guest ([[Mazu III,|Manu III.]] 70) and he was to place food on the ground for persons who had lost caste, who suffered from loathsome diseases, to [[dāṇdālas,|cāṇḍālas,]] dogs, crows and even insects[^1504] (Āp. Dh. S. II. 4. [[9.5,|9. 5,]] [[Mam|Manu]] III. 92). Vide H. of Dh. vol. [[II,|II.]] pp. 745-747 on [[Vaiśvadoya|Vaiśvadeva]] and [[Baliharana.|Baliharaṇa.]] Underlying these provisions was the noble [[senti ment|sentiment]] of universal kindliness and charity, the persistent [[ides|idea]] that, in spite of social gradations, rules and prejudices dividing [[P936]] men from each other, one Supreme Light [[poryades|pervades]] and [[illumi. nates|illuminates]] the meanest of creatures and makes the whole world kin. This spirit of a householder to regard it as his duty to [[offor|offer]] food to all needy persons and particularly to poor and deserving students and brāhmaṇas has prevailed almost to the present day, though during the last few years shortage of food, high prices and rationing have undermined it a great deal.

[^1502]: [[aprila mah ASETESTI \*\*Pront of agresort !|सर्वेषामेव दानानामन्नं श्रेष्ठमुदाहृतम् । ...प्राणा ह्यन्नं मनुष्याणां तस्माज्जन्तुः]] [[पजायते॥ असे|प्रजायते ॥ अन्ने]] प्रतिष्ठिता [[लोकास्तस्माद प्रशस्यते। "अवस्य|लोकास्तस्मादन्नं प्रशस्यते । ...अन्नस्य]] हि प्रदानेन [[स्वर्गमामोति मानवः।|स्वर्गमाप्नोति मानवः ।]] [["न्यायेनापासमकं|...न्यायेनावाप्तमन्नं]] तु नरो [[वर्षसमन्धितः। द्विजेन्यो पेदपद्धेग्यो बचा पापात्ममुच्यते। मापु|हर्षसमन्वितः । द्विजेभ्यो वेदवृद्धेभ्यो दत्त्वा पापात्प्रमुच्यते । ब्रह्मपु०]] 218. 10-13, 22-23.

[^1504]: [[Fainatate afta: para prete: 1 ST.4. a.|सर्वान् वैश्वदेवे भागिनः कुर्वीता श्वचण्डालेभ्यः । आप. ध. सू.]] II. 4. 9. [[3;|5;]] [[got maatat o superat genfrontierenrat stort serangan|शुनां च पतितानां च श्वपचां पापरोगिणाम् । वायसानां कृमीणां च शनकैर्निर्वपेद्भुवि ॥ मनु.]] [[III,|III.]] 92.

Then as to gifts to [[brāhmanas.|brāhmaṇas.]] In the first place, it must be remembered that all [[brāhmapas|brāhmaṇas]] were not priests and are not priests in modern [[timos.|times.]] Similarly, all priests in all Indian temples and shrines are not brāhmaṇas. Temple priests are comparatively a later institution and in olden times they were looked down upon and are regarded as inferior brāhmaṇas [[oven|even]] in modern times. Manu (III. 152) states that a devalaka (a brāhmaṇa who performed for remuneration service before the image in a temple) was unfit to be invited at a śrāddha or to officiate in a sacrifice to gods, if he continuously served for three years in that capacity. The ideal set before brāhmaṇas from [[Yery|very]] ancient times was of poverty, of plain living and high thinking, of abandoning the active pursuit of riches, of devotion to the study of the Veda and śāstras, of cherishing a high culture and of handing down literature and cultural outlook. [[Smrtis|Smṛtis]] like Yāj. [[I,|I.]] 213 recommend that even if a [[brābmaṇa|brāhmaṇa]] be fit for receiving a religious gift he should refuse gifts and thereby [[he-secures|he secures]] the same worlds that habitual donors secure. It was for preserving such high ideals among brāhmaṇas that [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 333 prescribes that a king should make gifts of cows, gold and land and should bestow on learned brāhmaṇas houses and requisites for marriage (maidens, expenses of marriages &c.). Vide H. of Dh. [[VOL. II. Pp.|vol. II. pp.]] 856-858 where references are given to inscriptions containing donations to [[brābmanas|brāhmaṇas]] of houses and marriage expenses. In these days every body talks of the high culture and literary traditions of ancient India. But who cultivated the vast Vedic and other Sanskrit literature, [[presoryad|preserved]] it and propagated it for several millenia? The answer would have to be that it was mostly due to some of the [[brahınanas|brāhmaṇas]] who stuck to the ancient ideal for thousands of years. If the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] can be put forward in these days as the most ancient of the literary monuments in any Aryan language, who preserved its more than ten thousand verses with unparallelled care by oral transmission without hardly any variant readings. The reply will again have to be that the [[brābmanas|brāhmaṇas]] did it by a [[P937]] self-denying ordinance. The [[Brābmanas|Brāhmaṇas]] had to study the Veda with auxiliary lores without[^1505] an eye to any ulterior motive, but simply as a duty and to understand the meaning thereof, they were to teach the Veda and other lores without demanding beforehand a fee, they had to bring up their own families, [[per form|perform]] sacrifices and make gifts themselves. The only substantial [[Bources|sources]] of income were officiating at sacrifices and religious rites and receiving gifts. These sources must always have been variable, fitful and precarious. The brāhmaṇas had no power to tax people as in the case of tithes in the West. Nor had they a regularly paid hierarchy of deacons, priests, bishops and archbishops as in the Anglican [[Church|Church.]] Therefore, the [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] are advised to approach the king or a rich person for their livelihood (vide Gautama [[IX.63|IX. 63]] 'Yogakṣemārtham-[[Isvaram|īśvaram]] adhigaccet). It should be noted that before Buddhism spread far and wide the sūtras and [[smstis|smṛtis]] emphasized that religious gifts should be made only to a worthy [[brāhmana,|brāhmaṇa,]] learned and well-conducted. For example, Āpastamba[^1506] prescribes 'one should invite for dinner in all religious acts brāhmaṇas that are pure, that have studied the Veda and that one should distribute gifts at a proper time and place, [[ori|on]] occasions of purificatory rites and when there is a worthy recipient.' To the same effect are Vas. Dh. S. III. 8, VI. 30, Manu (III. 128, 132, [[IV,|IV.]] 31), [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] [[(1.|(I.]] 201), [[Daksa (IL.|Dakṣa (III.]] 26 and 31). Not every brāhmaṇa was in those ancient times a proper recipient for a gift, but he had to possess qualities of what is called 'pātra'. A few definitions of pātra may with advantage be cited here. The [[Anugāgar parva|Anuśāsana-parva]][^1507] has a long passage emphasizing the qualities of [[deserving|a deserving]] brāhmaṇa : 'gifts made to good brāhmaṇas that are free from anger, that are intent on dharma, are devoted to truth and [[self control|self-control]] [[P938]] yield great rewards; the sages regard that [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] as pātra who studies the four Vedas with their [[argas|aṅgas]] (auxiliary lores like phonetics, grammar [[&o.),|&c.),]] who is active in doing six acts [[(viz,|(viz.]] abstaining from wine and meat, observing the bounds of morality, purity, study of the Veda, sacrifices, making gifts). Even a single eminent brāhmaṇa endowed with intellect, Vedic learning, good [[conduot|conduct]] and character saves the whole family; one should bring from afar a brāhmaṇa on hearing that he is possessed of good qualities and is approved by good men and should welcome him and honour him in all ways'. [[Yaj. furni shes|Yāj. furnishes]][^1508] a brief but striking definition of a worthy [[brāhmaṇa not|brāhmaṇa 'not]] by Vedic learning alone nor by tapas (austere life) alone worthiness arises; that person is declared to be pātra (worthy recipient for a religious gift) where both these (i. e. Vedic learning and tapas) and good conduct [[exist.|exist'.]] Manu provides that religious gifts given to a brāhmaṇa who has not studied the Veda or who is avaricious or deceitful are [[fruitlegs|fruitless]] and lead the donor to hell (IV. 192-194). The [[Bhagavadgitā|Bhagavadgītā]] also (17.22) condemns [[&|a]] gift given to an unworthy person as tāmasa [[( affected|(affected]] by tamas, arising from ignorance or delusion).

I. p. 1) quotes it as [[BITA.|आगम.]] Vide H. of [[Db.|Dh.]] [[vol, II|vol. II]] pp. 105-110 on the duties of brāhmaṇas and their sources of income in ancient times.
