[^1507]: Some of the verses of [[31gata|अनुशासन]] 22 33-41 may be cited [[bore|here]] : अक्रोधना धर्मपराः सत्यनित्या दमे [[रताः।|रताः ।]] [[तावशाः|तादृशाः]] साधवो [[विपास्तेग्यो वत्तं|विप्रास्तेभ्यो दत्तं]] [[महाफलम्।|महाफलम् ।]] [[साथ|साङ्गांश्च]] चतुरो [[वेवानधीते|वेदानधीते]] यो द्विजर्षभः । [[षड्म्यः प्रवृत्तः कर्मग्यस्त|षड्भ्यः प्रवृत्तः कर्मभ्यस्तं]] [[पात्रमुषयो|पात्रमृषयो]] विदुः [[।|॥]] [[प्रज्ञाशुताम्यांचन|प्रज्ञाश्रुताभ्यां वृत्तेन]] शीलेन च समन्वितः । [[तारयेत कुल|तारयेत् कुलं]] सर्वमेकोऽपीह द्विजोत्तमः [[! "निशम्य|। ... निशम्य]] च गुणोपेतं [[प्राण F aqla y Hero Fasano gerar a|ब्राह्मणं साधुसंमतम् । दूरादानाय्य सत्कृत्य सर्वतश्चापि पूजयेत् ॥]] verses 33, 36, 38, 41; on [[: Hi|षड्भ्यः प्रवृत्तः]] the [[com,|com.]] of [[five|नीलकण्ठ]] is [[' tutti nyaraustaafarur FRE अध्ययनयागदानेम्या, साग्याष्टात प्रमुच इत्यर्थः।|'अनुपदोक्तैः मधुमांसवर्जनमर्यादापालनशौचैः सह अध्ययनयागदानेभ्यः, तान्यनुष्ठातुं प्रवृत्त इत्यर्थः ।']]

[^1508]: [[नविधया केवलयापसापापिपात्रता। यत्र तमिमे चोमे तखि पाय प्रकीवितन । Mr.|न विद्यया केवलया तपसा वापि पात्रता । यत्र वृत्तमिमे चोभे तद्धि पात्रं प्रकीर्तितम् ॥ याज्ञ.]] I. 200.

As Buddhism [[grow|grew]] in popularity and secured [[algo|also]] royal support, the brāhmaṇas had to tackle several tasks. They had to keep the number of brāhmaṇas at a high level, they had to find maintenance for those devoted to the deep study of the Veda and they had to make accommodation with [[provalent|prevalent]] Buddhist thoughts by assimilating as many of them as possible in their [[owa|own]] writings. Every brāhmaṇa could not possess the memory, the intelligence and the persistent endeavour for long years required for memorizing and mastering even his own [[Voda|Veda]] and its subsidiary Literature. If one [[bundred|hundred]] brāhmaṇa families were patronized, hardly ten percent of them could have been masters of their own Veda, but there was always the possibility that those who were not themselves good Vedic scholars might have sons, some of whom might turn out to be profound students of the [[Voda.|Veda.]] Therefore, the number of [[brāhmapas|brāhmaṇas]] was to be increased and they had to be fed and not to be allowed to fritter away energy and time in working for their bread. It is mainly due to these factors that some of the [[Purīnas|Purāṇas]] contain [[incassant end|incessant and]] frantic appeals for gifts to [[brahmanas,|brāhmaṇas.]]

[[P939]] At the time when most of the Purāṇas were composed, the brāhmaṇas were hemmed in by great [[dificulties|difficulties]] and hostile forces. From about the 3rd century [[B.O.|B. C.]] to the 7th century A. D. Buddhism enjoyed royal patronage under such great kings as [[Asoka, Kaniska and Harga.|Aśoka, Kaniṣka and Harṣa.]] Buddhism was not really a revolt against caste, but against the sacrificial system, against the Veda and its authority to show the way to [[salvation,|salvation.]] Buddha did not found a new religion, but he was a great [[re former|reformer]] of Hinduism. He laid sole stress on moral effort, [[ahinsa,|ahiṁsā,]] satya &c. which had already been integrated into Hinduism and were part of it and which continue to this day to be part of Hinduism. Buddha in his first sermon in the deer-park near Banaras preached that one who renounced the world should shun two extremes[^1509] viz. the pursuit of pleasures and the practice of useless austerities, that it is the course discovered by him that led to wisdom and [[nirvāna.|nirvāṇa.]] He expounded the four noble Axioms or Truths [[(Aryasatyāni)|(Āryasatyāni)]] viz. suffering [[( duḥkha),|(duḥkha),]] the cause of suffering[^1510] viz. [[tanha i.e. traṇā|taṇhā i. e. tṛṣṇā]] (duḥkha-samudaya), the suppression of suffering [[( duḥkha-nirodha),|(duḥkha-nirodha),]] and the way that leads to the suppression of suffering [[(duḥkha-nirodhagamini patipada).|(duḥkha-nirodhagāminī paṭipadā).]] This last is the Noble Eight-fold path ([[agtangiko|aṣṭāṅgiko]][^1510a] [[P940]] mārgaḥ) viz. right views, right thoughts or aspirations, right speech, right actions, right living, right exertion, right [[recollec tion|recollection]] (or mindfulness), right meditation [[(i.e.|(i. e.]] briefly leading a virtuous life). These doctrines preached to all by the noble Buddha and his disciples were attractive as remarked by Rhys [[Davida,|Davids,]][^1511] particularly to the śūdras whose social position was low in the Vedic times and also in the days of the smṛtis. No one could study the Veda in the presence of a śūdra, a [[sūdra|śūdra]] could not [[parform|perform]] sacrifices and had to serve the three higher varṇas in very ancient times. Almost the same position was assigned to him by Manu (VIII. 413) viz. that he was created by God for serving the [[brāhmana,|brāhmaṇa,]] though it is doubtful whether this could be or was enforced. Vide H. of [[Dn.|Dh.]] vol. II, pp. 33-36 and pp. 154-164 for the position and disabilities of śūdras from the Vedic times up to the days of the [[Smortis.|Smṛtis.]] It should not, however, be supposed that all India had become Buddhist. Millions still remained Hindus. There was the danger and fear that with royal [[patron age|patronage]] and the attractive features of Buddha's teachings large masses might forsake their ancient faith.

[^1509]: For the two extremes to be [[shudned,|shunned,]] vide [[Dhammacakka-ppaya. ttana-satta|Dhammacakka-ppavattana-sutta]] (inauguration of the kingdom of [[Righteousness )|Righteousness)]] S. B. E. vol. XI. p. 146.

[^1510]: It may be noted that in the [[Upanisds and Tita|Upaniṣads and महाभारत]] the giving up of तृष्णा or काम is emphasized. [^1510a]: Vide [[Dhammacakkamppavattana-gatta|Dhammacakka-ppavattana-sutta]] in [[S.B. E.|S. B. E.]] vol. XI. p. 147 for the eightfold path. The [[Pāll|Pāli]] words are : [[Fm-fera, parvut, TRATE पाचा,|सम्मा-दिट्ठि, सम्मा-संकप्पो, सम्मा वाचा,]] सम्मा [[कम्मन्सो,|कम्मन्तो,]] सम्मा आजीवो, [[सम्मा-चायामो,|सम्मा-वायामो,]] सम्मा-सति ([[सम्यकसति|सम्यक् स्मृति)]] [[सम्मा A .|सम्मा-समाधि.]] Vide also [[afarqiy|दीघनिकाय]] (Pali Text Society) vol. I. p. 157, [[(Oldenberg)|महावग्ग (Oldenberg)]] vol. I. p. 10 (I. 6. [[18 )|18)]] and [[c u para|धम्म-चक्क-प्पवत्तनसुत्त para]] 4 p. 3 (ed. by Sister [[Vajiri,|Vajirā,]] Sarnath) and for [[gre, ETT, ERAI. rafata u t afaqat,|दुःख, दुःखसमुदय, दुःखनिरोध, दुःखनिरोधगामिनी पटिपदा,]] vide [[hierro|महावग्ग]] (I. [[6.19-22),|6. 19–22),]] [[sbid|ibid]] p. 10. These four are called [[arata|आर्यसत्यानि]] which may mean 'the four Noble Truths' or 'the four Truths found by the [[Ārya"|Ārya']] (Buddha). This bears a very close resemblance to the fourfold [[axions|axioms]] in Yoga and Medicine set out in [[योगरत्रमाव्य|योगसूत्रभाष्य]] 'यथा [[चिकित्साशा चतुहर-रोगो|चिकित्साशास्त्रं चतुर्व्यूहम्-रोगो]] रोगहेतुरारोग्यं भैषज्यमिति, [[एवमिदमपिशाचतुरमेव वयथा संसार संसारऐतः,|एवमिदमपि शास्त्रं चतुर्व्यूहमेव तद्यथा-संसारः संसारहेतुः,]] मोक्षः, [[मोक्षोपाया-इति|मोक्षोपायः-इति]] । [[वासवाला संसारी हेयः। प्रधानास्पयो सपोमा हेपदेतासंयोगस्पास्यन्तिकी निचिहानम्। हामोपायः|तत्र दुःखबहुलः संसारो हेयः । प्रधानपुरुषयोः संयोगो हेयहेतुः । संयोगस्यात्यन्तिकी निवृत्तिर्हानम् । हानोपायः]] सम्यग्दर्शनम् । [[योगमाया पोमन n,|योगभाष्य on योगसूत्र II.]] 15.

[^1511]: Vide his eloquent Intro to S. B. [[E,|E.]] vol. XI. p. 142 [[Never|'Never]] in the history of the world had a scheme of salvation [[beco pat|been put]] forth [[60|so]] simple in its [[datore,|nature,]] so free from any superhuman agency, so independent of, [[80|so]] even antagonistic to the belief in a soul, [[tbe|the]] belief in God, the hope for a future life. [[Buddba pat|Buddha put]] forth deliberately the doctrine of a [[galva. tion|salvation]] to be found here in this life in [[a laward|an inward]] change of [[beart|heart]] to be brought about by perseverance in a mere system of [[self.culture|self-culture]] and of [[self control.|self-control.]] That system is called the Noble Eight-fold Path.'

The brāhmaṇas of the times when Buddhism was at its peak had to strive to keep the banner of the Vedic religion flying, to deprive Buddhism of its [[bold|hold]] on the masses of the people and even on the intelligentsia and to make them stick to the old fold. Buddhism itself had changed a great deal in its ideals, [[dootrinos|doctrines]] sometime before the beginning of the Christian era and for centuries thereafter. Buddha's original doctrines were aimed at individual effort and salvation of the individual by his own effort and self-culture. Early Buddhist texts deny the existence of anything like a soul[^1512] and find no place for [[P941]] the idea of God. Though Buddha spoke of [[Nirvana|Nirvāṇa]] he did not clearly define it nor does he specify the condition of the [[indivi dual|individual]] when he enters nirvāṇa. Aśvaghoṣa compares [[nirvana|nirvāṇa]] to the extinction of a flame (Saundarananda, chap. [[XVI,|XVI.]] 28-29). [[Bocanse|Because]] the doctrine of karma was deeply rooted in the popular mind at the time of Buddha, Buddhism took over that [[dootrine,|doctrine,]] which, to men who are not Buddhists, appears to be [[contradio tory|contradictory]] of the denial of the existence of a soul. The word 'Dhamma' is used in three senses in the Pāli [[Dhammapada'|'Dhammapada']] (which being mentioned in the questions of Milinda is earlier than the 2nd century B. C.) viz. (1) the truth or law preached by Buddha, (2) thing or form, (3) way or mode of life.

[^1512]: [[Vide' Questions|Vide 'Questions]] of Milinda' [[(11,3,6) is|(II. 3. 6) in]] S. B. E. vol. [[XXXY|XXXV]] pp. 88–89 for a discussion of the doctrine that there is no soul and pp. 520, 71-77 for the Buddhist theory of kamma [[( karma)|(karma)]] and for the theory that [[wbat|what]] is reborn is [[nāma-ripa (dame|nāma-rūpa (name]] and [[form )|form)]] and not the soul. The [[Seandarādanda|Saundarānanda]] (B. I. ed. 16. [[28–29 )|28–29)]] says: [['attu Patlatargul fara|दीपो यथा निर्वृतिमभ्युपेतो नैवावनिं]] गच्छति नान्तरिक्षम् । [[विर्शन कोचिद्विविशं|दिशं न कांचिद्विदिशं]] न कांचित् [[स्नेहक्षयाकेवलमेति|स्नेहक्षयात्केवलमेति]] [[शान्तिम् । एवं कती|शान्तिम् ॥ एवं कृती निर्वृतिमभ्युपेतो नैव ... कांचित् क्लेशक्षयात्केवलमेति शान्तिम् ॥'.]]

As stated above, the original Buddhism preached by the Buddha and his followers in the first century or two after his [[parinirvana|parinirvāṇa]] was more or less a strict ethical code for individuals who sought salvation from the misery of the world. The three central conceptions of very early Buddhism [[wore|were]] the three refuges [[(or rainas )|(or ratnas)]] Buddha, Dharma and Sangha, the Four Noble Truths and the Eightfold Path. Gradually a new doctrine was evolved.[^1513] It came to be thought that to care for one's own deliverance and spend all efforts thereon was rather selfish, that as the Buddha himself out of compassion for suffering humanity worked for forty-five years to lead men to salvation by his exhortations and sermons, so a Buddhist should prefer [[tor|to]] put off his own deliverance, should work for the deliverance of his fellowmen out of compassion [[(karuna)|(karuṇā)]] for their miserable lot and in doing so should be ready to be born again and again, should not care for his own salvation and should not be afraid of [[satnsāra.|saṁsāra.]] Those holding these latter views deified Buddha, taught that Buddhahood was attained by Siddhārtha after [[under going|undergoing]] numerous births in doing service and rendering help to others and that this was a superior code of conduct (Mahāyāna, the Great Vehicle or Way) to the selfish code of salvation for an individual himself [[( which|(which]] came to be called [[Hinayāna,|Hīnayāna,]] the [[leggor|lesser]] Vehicle or Way). This extra-regarding gospel of Mahāyāna was very attractive and won great support in most countries of [[Asia,|Asia.]][^1514]

[^1513]: H. [[Koro|Kern]] in [[bis|his]] [[' Manval|'Manual]] of Buddhism' (in the [[Grandri89|Grundriss]] p. 122 holds that Mahāyānism is much indebted to the [[Bhagavad-gitā.|Bhagavad-gītā.]] Compare [[Fraaior at tent: 9|'लभन्ते ब्रह्मनिर्वाणमृषयः ... सर्वभूतहिते रताः ॥']] V. 25 with the Mahāyāna view.

[^1514]: The [[qumber|number]] of Books on Buddhism is [[legion,|legion.]] For Mahāyāna Buddhism, vide 'An Introduction to [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] Buddhism' by W. M. [[Mc Govern|McGovern]] (London, 1922); 'Aspects of Mahāyāna Buddhism' by [[Dr, M, N,|Dr. M. N.]] [[Datt|Dutt]] (1930); [[the Bodhisattva|the 'Bodhisattva]] doctrine in Sanskrit Literature' by Dr. Har Dayal (Kegan Paul, London [[1932):|1932);]] [["The|'The]] path of the Buddha' ed. by Prof. [[Keopeth|Kenneth]] W. Morgan (New York, [[1956 )|1956)]] written by several scholars from different countries (for both [[Hinayaoa|Hīnayāna]] and Mahāyāna).

    [[· A|A]] few books for those [[wbo|who]] want to know more of Buddhism in general and of [[Hinayāna|Hīnayāna]] and [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] are recommended here. [[The|'The]] central conception of Buddhism' (London, 1923), 'the conception of [[Nirvana"|Nirvāṇa']] (Leningrad, 1927), 'Buddhist Logic' vol. I (1958), all by Th. [[Stcherbatsky:|Stcherbatsky;]] [['Vedantic Boddhism|'Vedantic Buddhism]] of the Buddha' by J. G. Jennings (Oxford [[Uo.|Un.]] Press, [[1948):|1948);]] [[Creed|'Creed]] of Buddha' by Edmond Homes [[( 5th ed.):|(5th ed.);]] [[Introduction|'Introduction]] to Tantrik Buddhism' by Dr. [[Shasbi|Shashi]] Bhushan Das-Gupta (Cal. [[Voiversity,|University,]] 1950), 'the [[Flane|Flame]] and the Light' by [[Hagh I,|Hugh I.]] Faussett (London and New York, 1958); [["the|'the]] Buddha and his Dhamma' by Dr. B. [[R,|R.]] Ambedkar (1957) in which he refrains from considering Buddhist Texts except those in Pali; 'Comparative study of Buddhism and [[Christianity,|Christianity']] by [[Prof, F. Masutan!|Prof. F. Masutani]] (Tokyo, 1957). The [[Mabāyāna-sūtrālankāra|Mahāyāna-sūtrālaṅkāra]] of [[Asanga|Asaṅga]] (ed. by Prof. S. Levi) [[sommarises la|summarises in]] two verses (I. 9-10) the points (five) on which the two schools are in conflict. It-sing's [[Records|'Records]] of the Buddhist [[religion|religion']] translated by Dr. J. [[Takakasa|Takakusu]] (Oxford, 1896) surprisingly enough states (p. 15) 'These two systems are perfectly [[accord|in accord]] with the noble [[doctrine,|doctrine.]] Both equally conform to truth and lead us to [[Nirvana',|Nirvāṇa'.]] [[Baddha|Buddha]] at least ignored (if he did not positively deny) God, [[be|he]] denied the individual soul and Eternity, [[be|he]] did not emphasize the most vital [[Upa nigad|Upaniṣad]] teaching viz. [[' anando brabmeti vyajāgāt'.|'ānando brahmeti vyajānāt'.]] He imagined salvation as [[&|a]] state of [[absolate|absolute]] quiescence and therefore regarded ordinary life as misery and asserted that salvation may be attained even in this very life. He did not claim to be God, but a [[buman|human]] being. There are various kinds of Mahāyāna doctrines and great diversity of definitions. It may generally be said that works professing to teach [[Mahāyāaa|Mahāyāna]] practically forsake the ideal of a human Buddha, preach the [[worsbip|worship]] of Buddha and [[fatore|future]] Buddhas, and assert that [[Nirvana|Nirvāṇa]] cannot be attained by the ancient method, that salvation cannot come in this very life but after centuries and [[acons|aeons]] of the practice of [[virtas|virtue]] and helping others.

    Mantrayāna and [[Vajrayana|Vajrayāna]] are said by some to be branches of [[Maba gina,|Mahāyāna,]] about the latter of which a good deal will be said in the next section. According to Bhikṣu [[Rabula Sāṅktyāyada,|Rāhula Sāṅkṛtyāyana,]] [[Vajrayana ( 700-1200 A.D. )|Vajrayāna (700-1200 A.D.)]] is [[2|a]] synonym of [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] (400–700 A. D.) and [[werely|merely]] the ulterior [[dopalope ment|development]] of it (vide p. 211 of the paper ‘L' Origine du [[Vajrayina ot Laa|Vajrayāna et Les]] 84 [[Siddhar' io|Siddhas' in]] J. A. vol. 225 (1934) pp. 209-230.

[[P942]] This [[dootrine|doctrine]] of [[Bodhisattves|Bodhisattvas]] (meaning 'beings destined to be enlightened') is not consistent with the gospel of Buddha as [[proached|preached]] by him in his first sermon at Banaras. There is a difference of ideals between [[Hinayāna|Hīnayāna]] and [[Mahāyāna|Mahāyāna.]] The original gospel relies on self-effort and moral regeneration and on the elimination of suffering and misery by the extinction of all passions and of hankerings or desires and of the desire of [[P943]] life[^1515] itself. Buddha's original teaching regarded it [[ag & Waste|as a waste]] of time to ponder over such questions as 'Have I existed during the ages that are past or have I not? Shall I exist during the ages of the future? Do I after all exist or am I not?' The [[Sabbāgavasutta|Sabbāsavasutta]] (9-13) says that a wise man walking in the noble eightfold path understands what things ought to be [[con sidered|considered]] and what things ought not to be considered. Vide [[8.B.E. VOL XI,|S.B.E. vol. XI.]] pp. 298-300. Buddhism brought half of Asia under [[ito|its]] influence not only by its promise of salvation to all by self-help, but more so by its teachings of profound tenderness, of active charity, of goodness and [[gentlenesa.|gentleness.]] Mahāyāna laid great stress on doing good to all and on bhakti. Both the original teaching of [[Hinayāna|Hīnayāna]] and the [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] teaching are attractive in their own way.

[^1515]: That [[destraction|destruction]] of desires is [[nirvana|nirvāṇa]] is stated in [[Ratanaautta|Ratanasutta]] 14 and the [[similo|simile]] mentioned is that of a lamp being extinguished (by lack of [[oli).|oil).]]

Buddhism insisted on the five [[silas|śīlas]][^1516] that were binding on all Buddhists viz. prohibition of injury to and [[destruotion|destruction]] of life, of theft, of sexual impurity, of lying and of [[intoxioating|intoxicating]] liquors. Five more precepts [[( which|(which]] together with the preceding five were called [[Daśaśiksāpadas)|Daśaśikṣāpadas)]] were added for Buddhist priests, viz. prohibition of eating at forbidden hours, of attending [[wordly|worldly]] amusements such as dancing, song, music and shows, of the use of unguents and ornaments, of the use of a large or ornamented couch and of the receiving of gold and silver. About the [[silas|śīlas]] it is clear that they were adopted from the ancient [[Upaniṣadio|Upaniṣadic]] and Dharmasūtra teachings. The [[Chandogya|Chāndogya]] narrates how Aśvapati, king of Kekaya, boasted before five great householders and [[theologiang'in|theologians 'in]] my[^1517] kingdom there [[ia|is]] no thief, no miser, no drunkard, no man without a [[firs-altar|fire-altar]] in his house, [[Do|no]] ignorant person, no adulterer, much less an adulteress'. The same Upaniṣad[^1518] quotes an ancient [[verse a|verse 'a]] man who steals gold, who drinks liquor, who dishonours his guru's bed, who kills [[& brahmapathese|a brāhmaṇa—these]] four fall and a fifth also that associates with any one of [[those|these]] four.' It will be shown [[P944]] a little later how [[ahingā wag|ahiṁsā was]] emphasized even in the Upaniṣads. Thus [[ahimsa,|ahiṁsā,]] non-stealing, sexual purity, truthfulness had already been emphasized in the oldest [[Upanisads.|Upaniṣads.]] That an [[agoetic|ascetic]][^1519] had to give up all property and beg for keeping body and soul together is made clear by [[Br.|Bṛ.]] Up. III. [[5.1 and IV.4.22,|5. 1 and IV. 4. 22,]] [[Jabalopanisad 5.|Jābālopaniṣad 5,]] Gautama III. 10-13, [[Vasistha x.|Vasiṣṭha X.]] The other five precepts for priests such as not receiving gold or silver or giving up unguents, flowers, dancing, singing and music are laid down by Gautama[^1520] II. 19 and III. 4, [[Vasisthax.6&c.|Vasiṣṭha X. 6 &c.]] for Vedic students and ascetics. Vide H. Kern (in 'Manual of Indian Buddhism, Grundriss p. 70) who remarks that the superior morality for monks is nothing else but the rule of life for the [[dirija|dvija]] in the 4th [[ūśrama,|āśrama,]] when he is a yati and all the details were taken from the Dharma-sūtras and [[Dharmaśāstras,|Dharmaśāstras.]]

[^1516]: Vide [[gura 2. rafatara|खुद्दकपाठ 2, दीघनिकाय]] (II. 43 [[p. 63 )|p. 63)]] for the five [[has|śīlas]] and [[Kora's 'Maoual|Kern's 'Manual]] of Indian Buddhism' p. 70.

[^1518]: [[सदेव श्लोका|तदेष श्लोकः]] । स्तेनो हिरण्यस्य [[पुरा पिच गरोस्वल्पमावसम्बाहाचैते पवन्ति T: TA R TIR I 37. 34.|सुरां पिबंश्च गुरोस्तल्पमावसन्ब्रह्महा चैते पतन्ति चत्वारः पञ्चमश्चाचरंस्तैरिति । छा. उप.]] V. [[10.9.|10. 9.]]

III. 5. 1 (after the [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] have gained knowledge of this Self, they abstain from desire for [[50ns,|sons,]] desire for wealth and desire for the worlds and wander about as beggars); अथ [[परिवार|परिव्राड्]] विवर्णवासा मुण्डोऽपरिग्रहः [[शुचिरोही|शुचिरद्रोही]] भैक्षणो ब्रह्मभूयाय भवतीति । जाबालोप. [[5quoted|5 quoted]] by शङ्कराचार्य on [[वेदान्तस्त्र|वेदान्तसूत्र]] II. [[1.3|1. 3]] and III. 4. 20.

[[II.19:|II. 19;]] मुण्डोऽममोऽपरिग्रहः । [[वसिष्ठx.6.|वसिष्ठ X. 6.]] For the other [[silas|śīlas]] of priests, compare with गौतम II. 19 the following from [[दीनिकाय|दीघनिकाय]] (vol. I p. 64 [[Samanna-phala-sutta|Sāmañña-phala-sutta]] II. 45) 'विरतो [[विकालभोजना।|विकालभोजना ।]] [[नव-भीत-वादित-विस्कवस्सना|नच्च-गीत-वादित-विसूकदस्सना]] पटिविरतो होति । माला-गन्ध-विलेपन-धारण-मण्डण-[[विनसण स्थाणा|विभूषणट्ठाणा]] पटिविरतो होति [[उचासयन-महासयना|। उच्चासयन-महासयना]] पटिविरतो [[होति।|होति ।]] [[जातरूप-रजतपटिग्गणा पटि विरतो|जातरूप-रजतपटिग्गहणा पटिविरतो]] होति । आमक-मंस-पटिग्गहणा पटिविरतो [[होति।'|होति ।']]

[[Ahimsā -- The|Ahiṁsā—The]] Mahābhārata and the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] lay great emphasis on [[abiinsā|ahiṁsā]] (not harming or giving pain to a living creature). The [[Upanisads|Upaniṣads]] too emphasized [[ahimsā.|ahiṁsā.]] The [[Chandogya|Chāndogya]][^1521] does so in several passages. In III. [[17.4|17. 4]] it says 'tapas, charity, straight-forwardness, [[ahimsa,|ahiṁsā,]] speaking the truth-these are the fees of this (sacrifice without ceremonial)'. While describing how the person who has attained true [[know ledge|knowledge]] of the Self does not return [[( to this sainsāra),|(to this saṁsāra),]] the [[Chandogya|Chāndogya]] says that [[he|'he]] causes no pain to any creature except at [[tirthas'.|tīrthas'.]] The [[Br.|Bṛ.]] Up. [[(V.2.)|(V. 2.)]] says how [[Prajapati|Prajāpati]] told the gods, the asuras and men that the sound 'da da da' produced by thundering [[P945]] clouds conveys to the gods the necessity of self-restraint [[( dama),|(dama),]] to [[theasures|the asuras]] of compassion [[(daya)|(dayā)]] and to men of charity [[(dana).|(dāna).]] Gautama[^1522] specifies eight virtues of the soul, the first of which is compassion for all beings and states that he who has the forty [[saīskāras|saṁskāras]] performed on him but does not [[possong|possess]] the eight virtues does not secure absorption into [[brahman|brahma.]] The [[Adiparya|Ādiparva]] [[provides abimsā|provides 'ahiṁsā]] is the highest dharma for all beings; therefore a [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] should never harm (or give pain [[to )|to)]] any being'. The words '[[ahirsā|ahiṁsā]] paramo dharmaḥ' ([[ahimsā|ahiṁsā]] is the highest dharma) [[ocour|occur]] very frequently in the Mahābhārata[^1523] (e. g. in [[Dropaparva|Droṇaparva]] 192. 38, [[Santi|Śānti]] [[265.6,|265. 6,]] [[339.|329.]] 18, [[Anusasana 115,25,|Anuśāsana 115. 25,]] 116. 38, [[Aivamedhikaparva|Āśvamedhikaparva]] 28. 16-18, 43. 21). [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] [[(296.22-24)|(296. 22-24)]] enumerates thirteen virtues common to all men, of which freedom from cruelty and [[ahirsa|ahiṁsā]] are the first two. [[Vasistha|Vasiṣṭha]] [[IV.4,|IV. 4,]] Manu [[x.|X.]] 63, [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 122 prescribe certain virtues as necessary in men of all varṇas. Vide [[.|H.]] of Dh. vol. II. p. 10 notes 25-27.

[^1521]: अथ [[यचयो|यत्तपो]] [[दानमार्जवमाहिसा|दानमार्जवमहिंसा]] सत्यवचनमिति ता अस्य [[दक्षिणाः।|दक्षिणाः ।]] छा. उप. III. [[17.4;|17. 4;]] [[आचार्यकुलादेवमधीत्य "स्वाध्यायमधीयानो धार्मिकान्विदधदात्मनि सन्द्रियाणि संप्रतिक्षाप्याहिंसन् सर्वभूतान्यन्यत्र तीर्थभ्यः "न|आचार्यकुलाद्वेदमधीत्य ... स्वाध्यायमधीयानो धार्मिकान्विदधदात्मनि सर्वेन्द्रियाणि संप्रतिष्ठाप्याहिंसन् सर्वभूतान्यन्यत्र तीर्थेभ्यः ... न]] च पुनरावर्तते । छा. उप. VIII. [[15:|15;]] [[तदेतत्वर्य शिबम हान वयामिति|तदेतत्त्रयं शिक्षेद्दमं दानं दयामिति]] । [[वृह.|बृह.]] उप. [[V.2.|V. 2.]]

[^1522]: दया सर्वभूतेषु [[शान्तिरनसूया|क्षान्तिरनसूया]] शौचमनायासो मङ्गलमकार्पण्यमस्पृहेति । यस्यैते [[चत्वारिंशसंस्कारा|चत्वारिंशत्संस्कारा]] न [[चाटावाल्मगुणा|चाष्टावात्मगुणा]] न [[सब्रह्मणः|स ब्रह्मणः]] सायुज्यं सलोकतां गच्छति । [[गो.ध..|गौ. ध. सू.]] VIII. 24-25. The मत्स्य [[(52.8-11)|(52. 8–11)]] sets out these very [[atmaganas|ātmaguṇas]] that Gautama mentions[[,|;]] vide also मार्कण्डेय 25. 32-33 for almost the same [[aight ganas|eight guṇas]] including [[abimasā.|ahiṁsā.]]

[^1523]: अहिंसा परमो धर्मः [[सर्वप्राणमतां पर|सर्वप्राणभृतां वर]] । [[तस्मात्माणमृतः सर्वात हिस्यात् बाणा|तस्मात्प्राणभृतः सर्वान्न हिंस्याद् ब्राह्मणः]] [[कचित्|क्वचित्]] ॥ आदिपर्व 11. [[13-14:|13-14;]] अहिंसा सर्वभूतेषु [[धर्म|धर्मं]] ज्यायस्तरं [[वितुः|विदुः]] । द्रोण [[192.38;|192. 38;]] अहिंसा [[सर्वसूतेग्यो धर्मेन्यो|सर्वभूतेभ्यो धर्मेभ्यो]] ज्यायसी [[मता।|मता ।]] शान्ति 265. 6; न हिंस्यात्सर्वभूतानि [[मैत्रायणगतवरेत्|मैत्रायणगतश्चरेत्]] । शान्ति 329. 18; अहिंसा परमो धर्मस्तथाहिंसा परं तपः । अहिंसा परमं [[सस्य|सत्यं]] यतो धर्मः [[प्रवर्तते।|प्रवर्तते ॥]] [[अदशासन|अनुशासन]] [[115.25;|115. 25;]] अहिंसा परमो [[धनों|धर्मो]] हिंसा [[चाधर्मलाणा। आवमेधिक|चाधर्मलक्षणा । आश्वमेधिक]] 43. 21.

A few examples of emphasis on [[ahimsā|ahiṁsā]] in the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] may be cited here. The [[Vamanapurana1534|Vāmanapurāṇa]][^1524] provides : [[ahinsa,|ahiṁsā,]] [[truth fulness,|truthfulness,]] [[P946]] absence of stealing, charity, forbearance, [[self-oontrol,|self-control,]] quiescence of senses, absence of poor spirit or weakness, purity, tapas-this is the tenfold dharma [[spplicable|applicable]] to all varṇas. The Padma says : [[Dot|not]] by [[(the study of )|(the study of)]] the Vedas, nor by [[gifte|gifts]] nor by tapas, nor by sacrifices do men who kill creatures reach the goal of heaven; [[ahimsa|ahiṁsā]] is the highest dharma, the highest tapas and the highest charity--this is what the [[søges|sages]] always say; [[mon|men]] that are compassionate treat [[fios,|flies,]] reptiles, stinging insects, lice and the like and human beings as [[themselves,|themselves.]] Matsya goes so far as to [[state ;|state;]] great sages do not commend [[sacrifios|sacrifice]] in which there is killing; by donating grains of corn gathered in a field, roots, fruits, vegetables, vessel for carrying water according to their ability, sages practising [[austorities|austerities]] became established in heaven; absence of hatred and greed, self-restraint, compassion towards all beings, control of senses, celibacy, tapas, tenderness, [[farbearance|forbearance]] and firmness—this is the root of the ancient [[dharua,|dharma,]] which is difficult to accomplish. The [[Brahmānda|Brahmāṇḍa]] (II. 31. 35 [[tagmād-ahimsa|'tasmād-ahiṁsā]] dharmasya dvāram-uktam [[maharṣibhiḥ' )|maharṣibhiḥ')]] says that great sages have declared that [[ahimsā|ahiṁsā]] is the door of dharma. The Padma (V. 43. [[38 )|38)]] says [[there|'there]] is no dāna nor tapas equal to [[ahimsā'.|ahiṁsā'.]] It is interesting to note that the [[Mateya|Matsya]] and [[Brabmāṇda|Brahmāṇḍa]] regard [[ahimsā|ahiṁsā]] as 'sanātana dharma' and condemn animal sacrifices. The [[Kurma|Kūrma]][^1525] provides '[[ahirai,|ahiṁsā,]] truthfulness, non-stealing, celibacy and non-possession of [[wealth these|wealth—these]] are briefly declared yamas that produce purity of mind among [[mon,|men.]] The great [[sagos deolare|sages declare]] that [[ahinsa|ahiṁsā]] consists in [[cansing|causing]] no pain to all [[boings|beings]] at all times by thought, word or [[P947]] [[dood.|deed.]] There is no dharma superior to [[ahimah,|ahiṁsā,]] no happiness higher than [[the praotice of )|(the practice of)]] [[ahimsā;|ahiṁsā;]] the injury [[(to life )|(to life)]] that [[ia|is]] caused [[socording|according]] to (Vedic) [[pracepts|precepts]] is declared to be [[ahirnar.'|ahiṁsā.']] The Upaniṣads commended a qualified [[abimsi,|ahiṁsā,]] while the original Pali books like the [[Sāmāñña-phala-gutta|Sāmañña-phala-sutta]] forbad injury to all living beings. Most of the [[Puranas,|Purāṇas,]] in order to convince the masses that they did not lag behind the Buddhist preachings, generally insist upon unqualified [[ahimsa.|ahiṁsā.]] Time brings about strange changes. Professing Buddhists in Ceylon, China, Japan and many other countries have no objection to partaking of fish and meat, while following the insistent advice of the [[Purinas,|Purāṇas,]] millions of Indian people (not only brāhmaṇas but also others like [[vaisyas|vaiśyas]] and [[sūdras|śūdras]] if Vaiṣṇavas) have been [[striot vege tarians|strict vegetarians]] for centuries, though Buddhism vanished from India centuries ago.

[^1524]: अहिंसा [[सत्यमस्तेय वान क्षान्तिईमः|सत्यमस्तेयं दानं क्षान्तिर्दमः]] शमः । [[अकार्पण्य|अकार्पण्यं]] च [[शौच|शौचं]] च [[तपन रजनी घरवशाल|तपश्च रजनीचर । दशाङ्गो]] राक्षसश्रेष्ठ धर्मोऽसौ [[सार्ववणिकः|सार्ववर्णिकः]] [[।|॥]] वामन [[14. 1-27न|14. 1-2; न]] वेदैर्न च [[वाचन वपोनिर्म चाम्बरः। कथंचित् स्वर्गति|दानैश्च न तपोभिर्न चाध्वरैः । कथंचित् स्वर्गतिं]] यान्ति पुरुषाः [[माणिहिंसका:|प्राणिहिंसकाः ।]] अहिंसा परमो धर्मो [[हिसव|ह्यहिंसैव]] परं [[सा।|तपः ।]] अहिंसा [[परम|परमं]] [[दानमित्याहुमुनयः|दानमित्याहुर्मुनयः]] [[सदा।|सदा ।]] मशकान् [[सरीसपान वंशान्यूकायान्मानयास्तथा आलो पम्येन|सरीसृपान् दंशान्यूकाद्यान्मानवांस्तथा । आत्मौपम्येन]] पश्यन्ति मानवा ये [[दयालयः। पम I.31.|दयालवः ॥ पद्म I. 31.]] 26-28; these verses are repeated in [[पा|पद्म]] VI. [[243.69-71|243. 69-71]] (reads [[मशकान्मस्कुणान|मशकान्मत्कुणान्]] and आत्मौपम्येन [[रक्षन्ति वलात हिंसा यशंप|रक्षन्ति); तस्मान्न हिंसायज्ञं च]] प्रशंसन्ति [[महर्षयः।|महर्षयः ।]] [[उञ्छो मूल|उञ्छो मूलं]] फलं [[शाकमुक्पा|शाकमुदपात्रं]] [[तपोधना|तपोधनाः ।]] [[एवश्वखा|एतद्दत्त्वा]] विभवतः [[सर्गलोक|स्वर्गलोके]] [[प्रतिष्ठिताः।|प्रतिष्ठिताः ।]] [[अद्रोहब्वायलोभश्च वमो भूतदया|अद्रोहश्चाप्यलोभश्च दमो भूतदया]] शमः ॥ [[ब्रह्मचर्य|ब्रह्मचर्यं]] तपः [[शौचमडक्रोश (शा.|शौचमनुक्रोशं (शः ?)]] क्षमा [[धृतिः।|धृतिः ।]] सनातनस्य धर्मस्य [[मूलमेतरासदम्|मूलमेतद्दुरासदम्]] [[।|॥]] मत्स्य 143. 30-32; [[बझाण्ड|ब्रह्माण्ड]] [[II.31.|II. 31.]] 36-38 are the same as मत्स्य (except that it reads [[तस्मान|तस्मान्न]] [[यज्ञ|यज्ञं]] दानं [[पा)|वा)]] 143. [[30-32,|30-32.]] The verses [[उछमुलं फल"मुलमेवत्सनातनम्|उञ्छं मूलं फल ... मूलमेतत्सनातनम्]] occur in [[आवधिकपर्व 92.|आश्वमेधिकपर्व 91.]] 32-34. For the words सनातनधर्म, vide the Khanapur plates of [[Madhavar varmanInE.I.|Mādhavavarman in E. I.]] vol. 27 [[p.312|p. 312]] at p. 317 ([[अविस्मतिविधिवसनातमधर्मकर्मनिरताय|श्रुतिस्मृतिविहितसनातनधर्मकर्मनिरताय]]) edited by [[Prof. V, V,|Prof. V. V.]] Mirashi who [[assigas|assigns]] it to the 6th [[centary A. Dette|century A. D.]]

[^1525]: [[se va luftest I WAT: fra: e t राणाम् ।|अहिंसा सत्यमस्तेयं ब्रह्मचर्यापरिग्रहौ । यमाः संक्षेपतः प्रोक्ताश्चित्तशुद्धिप्रदा नृणाम् ।]] कर्मणा मनसा वाचा सर्वभूतेषु सर्वदा । अक्लेशजननं [[मोक्का बहिंसा परमर्षिभिः।|प्रोक्ता त्वहिंसा परमर्षिभिः ॥]] [[अहिंसाया: परो घमों नास्त्यहिंसापर|अहिंसायाः परो धर्मो नास्त्यहिंसापरं]] सुखम् । विधिना या [[भवेद्धिसा त्वहिसैव प्रकीर्तिता कर्म|भवेद्धिंसा त्वहिंसैव प्रकीर्तिता ॥ कूर्म]] II. 11. 13-15. The [[(chap.|लिङ्गपुराण (chap.]] 8. 8–9) enumerates the [[clgbt|eight]] [[sadhanas|sādhanas]] of [[oth|योग]] of which [[yo|यम]] is first and [[mentiods|mentions]] five as [[lo n.|in कूर्म.]] Yamas (abstinences) are variously [[coomerated.|enumerated.]] Kūrma appears to follow the [[II.|योगसूत्र II.]] 30-31 'अहिंसा-सत्य-अस्तेय-[[ब्रह्मचर्यापरिबहा|ब्रह्मचर्यापरिग्रहा]] यमाः । [[शोच-सन्तोष-सपा स्वाध्याय ईश्वरणि una fayat: 11.|शौच-सन्तोष-तपः-स्वाध्याय-ईश्वरप्रणिधानानि नियमाः ।'.]] [[Maga IV,|Manu IV.]] 204 provides generally that one should always practise yamas and that one may not always practise the [[alyamas,|niyamas,]] but does [[pot pame|not name]] [[them,|them.]] [[Medhatitbi|Medhātithi]] explains that yamas are prohibitions [[( viz, aot to [njare|(viz. not to injure]] life, not to steal, [[aot|not]] to tell an [[untrath,|untruth,]] not to have forbidden [[Berdal|sexual]] intercourse and not to possess wealth that belongs to another or not to accept gifts), [[whilo|while]] niyamas consist of positive [[acte,|acts,]] such as one should always study the Veda (as [[la Madu|in Manu]] IV. 147). [[Yaj. ni.|Yāj. III.]] 312–313 [[saamerates ton|enumerates ten]] yamas viz. celibacy, compassion, forbearance, charity, [[abianca|absence]] of crooked conduct, [[ahimsā,|ahiṁsā,]] [[not-steallag,|non-stealing,]] [[sweatness,|sweetness,]] restraint of [[Bon|senses]] and [[teo alyamas,|ten niyamas.]] The [[TR-FHITE|वैखानस-स्मार्तसूत्र]] (IX. 4) [[enamoratos tan|enumerates ten]] yamas.

It may be noted, however, that some of the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] are against [[varrying|carrying]] the doctrine of [[ahimsa|ahiṁsā]] to extremes. The [[Brahmapda|Brahmāṇḍa]] and [[Vayu|Vāyu]] both say that there is no sin, great or small, in killing a person (e. g. a tyrant or a [[desperado ),|desperado),]] when many will live in happiness by his [[death,|death.]][^1526]

[^1526]: [[यस्मित|यस्मिंस्तु]] निहते [[भदे|भद्रे]] जीवन्ते [[पहषः मुखम्|बहवः सुखम्]] । [[तस्मिएहते|तस्मिन् हते]] नास्ति [[मे पावकं चोप पातकम्|शुभे पातकं चोपपातकम् ॥]] ब्रह्माण्ड II. 36. 188, [[पायु|वायु]] 69. 162 (reads लभन्ते for जीवन्ते). [[Thosपुराण|The ब्रह्मपुराण]] 141.22 has the same idea in different words [[forerunaa hii garu FATTET Fraue n ).|'यस्मिन्निपातिते सौख्यं बहूनामुपजायते । मुनयस्तद्वधं प्राहुरश्वमेधशताधिकम् ॥'.]] The [[thiruar (uueroa )|कल्पतरु (गृहस्थकाण्ड)]] p. 300 quotes the [[Verse|verse]] from [[any|वायु]] (with [[alight|slight]] variations viz. [[Per|एधन्ते]] for [[stomat)|जीवन्ते).]] The editor was unable to trace it. The verse should be read as [[far|निहते भद्रे]] and [[sot fatetusa|not निहतेऽभद्रे]] as it is printed on [[p, 300,|p. 300.]]

[[Parta-The|Pūrta—The]] Purāṇas lay the greatest emphasis on what is called [[purtadharma,|pūrtadharma,]] works of public utility, charity, [[soola!|social]] service and the relief of the poor and distressed. The word [[Istāpūrta|Iṣṭāpūrta]] occurs in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] once[^1527] [[May|'May]] you be united in the highest heaven with your [[pitys ( ancestors),|pitṛs (ancestors),]] with [[Yams|Yama]] and with [[iṣtāpārta'( merit|iṣṭāpūrta' (merit]] acquired by sacrifices and works of public [[utility ).|utility).]] The word [[ista|iṣṭa]] occurs several times in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] (I. [[162, 16|162. 15,]] [[1.|I.]] 164. 15, X. 11. [[%,|2,]] [[X82.2|X. 82. 2]]), but the meaning is not certain except in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 11. 2 where it appears to mean [[sacrifice'.|'sacrifice'.]] [[Purta|Pūrta]] also occurs in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VI. 16. 18 and [[VIII,|VIII.]] 46. 21, but the meaning is not certain. [["Iṣtāpūrta'|'Iṣṭāpūrta']] occurs in several [[Upanigades|Upaniṣads.]]

[^1527]: [[Free Porn Viihen on A TREI \*.|संगच्छस्व पितृभिः सं यमेनेष्टापूर्तेन परमे व्योमन् । ऋ.]] X. 14. 8; [[metara to other 8 HUAT fa maanr.|इष्टस्य मध्ये अदितिर्न धातु नो भ्राता नो ज्येष्ठः प्रथमो वि वोचति ॥ ऋ.]] [[X,|X.]] 11. 2.

[[P948]] The [[Chandogya|Chāndogya]] states[^1528] [[But|'But]] they who, living in a village, practise [[( a life of ) sacrifions,|(a life of) sacrifices,]] works of public utility and [[alms giving|alms-giving]] reach toward smoke &c.'. Similarly, the Praśna-upaniṣad [[Aggerts|asserts]] 'those, who practise the (mode of) sacrifices and works of [[publio|public]] utility as [[aotivities|activities]] to be engaged in, reach only the world of the Moon, and it is these that again return to this world'. The [[Mundaka|Muṇḍaka]] says 'deluded people regarding sacrifices and works of public utility as the best do not know [[( recognize)|(recognize)]] any other higher good; having enjoyed [[( their|(their]] reward) on the top of heaven, they again enter this world or even a lower one'.[^1529] Manu speaks of '[[iṣta'|iṣṭa']] and 'pūrta' and recommends that one should always practise with a pleased heart sacrificial gifts and gifts of the pūrta kind according to one's ability on securing a deserving [[brābmapa.|brāhmaṇa.]] The Amarakośa defines [["iṣta'|'iṣṭa']] as sacrifices and [[pārta'|'pūrta']] as works such as digging a well or tank. The [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]][^1530] defines them as follows 'Maintaining the sacred [[firos,|fires,]] tapas, truthfulness, [[study.|study]] of the [[Voda,|Veda,]] [[hogpitality|hospitality]] and [[Vaiśvadova-these|Vaiśvadeva-these]] are called [[ista;|iṣṭa;]] digging wells and tanks and building temples and distribution of food to those that need [[it these|it—these]] are declared to be [[purta.|pūrta.]] The Agnipurāṇa has similar [[vorses.|verses.]] The Padma (VI. 243. 10–14) cites the following as [[dharmakarya|dharmakārya]] (religious works); temples of Viṣṇu and Śiva, tanks, wells, lotus ponds, forest of [[vata,|vaṭa,]] pippala, mango, kakkola, jambu and nimba trees, flower garden, distribution of food from [[mording|morning]] to [[Sunset,|sunset,]] water distribution outside towns [[&o.|&c.]] The Skanda[^1531] says: the term 'pūrta' is applied in the [[charma sūstras|dharma-śāstras]] to the erection of temples, construction of tanks, ponds and wells, laying out parks. Padma ([[VI, 244, 34-35 )|VI. 244. 34-35)]] says that those who build monasteries, cow stables, houses of rest on roads, [[P949]] dwellings for [[ascetios,|ascetics,]] cottages for the poor and [[helplegg,|helpless,]] extensive house for Veda study, houses for [[brahmanas,|brāhmaṇas,]] enter the world of Indra [[(i. o.|(i. e.]] heaven). Atri [[sayg|says]][^1532] that [[Iṣta|Iṣṭa]] and [[Parts|Pūrta]] are dharma common to all dvijas; a [[sūdra|śūdra]] is entitled to [[performi|perform]] [[purta-dharmas|pūrta-dharmas]] but not Vaidika rites [[(I. e.|(i. e.]] sacrifices). The [[Anusasana-parva (ohap.|Anuśāsana-parva (chap.]] 58) describes how parks should be [[lald|laid]] out and tanks constructed with trees on their banks. But the [[Varihapurāṇa|Varāhapurāṇa]] and some [[smrtis|smṛtis]] [[wont eo|went so]] far [[and|as]] to declare that a man secures only heaven by [[iṣta,|iṣṭa,]] but he secures [[moksa|mokṣa]] (final release from [[samsāra )|saṁsāra)]] by pūrta[^1533].

[^1533]: [[इसापूर्व|इष्टापूर्ते]] द्विजातीनां [[प्रथम धर्मसाधनम्।|प्रथमं धर्मसाधनम् ।]] इष्टेन लभते [[स्वर्ग मोचविश्वति। पराह|स्वर्गं पूर्ते मोक्षं च विन्दति । वराह]] 172. 33, [[यमस्मति|यमस्मृति]] 68, [[अनिसंहिता|अत्रिसंहिता]] 145.

Sometimes the Purāṇas express ideas that might strike us as rather modern, when they put forward social service and removal of suffering and distress as the highest dharma. In the [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]][^1534] a king solemnly states 'men do not obtain that happiness in heaven or in the world of [[Brahma,|Brahmā,]] which [[springe|springs]] from giving relief to distressed beings. Sacrifices, gifts, tapas do not conduce to the relief here and in the next world of that man whose heart is not set on relieving the distressed'. The Viṣṇu [[recommends &|recommends 'a]] wise man should say [[(and do )|(and do)]] that alone by thought, word and deed which would be for the benefit of creatures here and hereafter'. The [[Skanda-purana (Kasikhanda) aver|Skanda-purāṇa (Kāśīkhaṇḍa) avers]] 'adversities of those good men in whose heart doing good to others is awake [[(i.e.aotive)|(i. e. active)]] vanish and prosperity comes to them at every step. That purity is not secured by [[bathe|baths]] at holy places, that reward is not obtained by numerous gifts, that (result) is not obtained by severe austerities, that is obtained by doing good to others. After churning all extensive [[diota tha|dicta the]] conclusion reached is this viz. there is no dharma higher than doing good to others and there is no sin greater than [[barmning|harming]] [[P950]] others. The Brahma [[states the|states 'the]] life of the man who always strives for the good of others is fruitful (blessed); fire, water, the Sun, the earth and various kinds of crops exist for the benefit of others, and particularly the good [[( exist|(exist]] for others' [[benefit).|benefit)'.]]

[^1534]: न [[स्व बहालोके|स्वर्गे ब्रह्मलोके]] वा [[तत्सर्व|तत्सुखं]] प्राप्यते [[नरैः।|नरैः ।]] [[पवार्तजन्तानिर्वाणवानोत्यामिति|यदार्तजन्तुनिर्वाणदानात्स्यादिति]] मे मतिः [[।|॥]] [[यज्ञदानतपासीह परवचन भूतये। भवन्ति तस्य यस्यापरित्राणे न मानस मार्कोप|यज्ञदानतपांसीह परत्र च न भूतये । भवन्ति तस्य यस्यार्तपरित्राणे न मानसम् ॥ मार्कण्डेय]] [[15.57|15. 57]] and 62; [[माणिनासपकाराय यथैवेह परत्र । कर्मणा मनसा वाचा वेष मतिमान पदेव|प्राणिनामुपकाराय यथैवेह परत्र च । कर्मणा मनसा वाचा तदेव मतिमान् वदेत् ॥]] विष्णु III. [[12.45:|12. 45;]] [[परोपकरण|परोपकरणं]] येषां [[जागतिदये सताम् । पश्यन्ति विपवस्तस्य सम्पदा सपदे पदे तीर्थस्लाने सा शुद्धिहुदान तत्कलम् । तपोभियतनाप्यसपकस्य पदाप्यते । परिनिर्मव्य पान्बाले निर्णीतमिदमेव हि। नोपकारात्परो धनों पापकारापर्ष परम् । स्कन काशीमन्ड|जागर्ति हृदये सताम् । नश्यन्ति विपदस्तस्य सम्पदः स्युः पदे पदे ॥ तीर्थस्नानैर्न सा शुद्धिर्बहुदानैर्न तत्फलम् । तपोभिरुग्रैस्तत्प्राप्यमुपकृत्य यदाप्यते ॥ परिनिर्मथ्य वाग्जालं निर्णीतमिदमेव हि । नोपकारात्परो धर्मो नापकारादघं परम् ॥ स्कन्द, काशीखण्ड]] [[64-5|6. 4-5]] and [[nीपित सफलं तस्य यः परार्थाचा सवा। अमिरापो रविः पुच्ची धान्पानि विविधानिमा पराधि पर्वन देवा सर्वाचापि विषम|7; जीवितं सफलं तस्य यः परार्थोद्यतः सदा । अग्निरापो रविः पृथ्वी धान्यानि विविधानि च । परार्थे वर्तनं तेषां सतां चापि विशेषतः ॥ ब्रह्म]] 125. 36-37.

It is surprising that the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] avers what is in advance of modern [[socialistio|socialistic]] doctrines 'men have ownership over only that much as would fill their belly; he who thinks as his own what is more than that is a thief and deserves punishment [[(As|(as]] such).[^1535]

[^1535]: [[me faire soi ani argintan sistotarreta \# mal qase महति|यावद् भ्रियेत जठरं तावत्स्वत्वं हि देहिनाम् । अधिकं योऽभिमन्येत स स्तेनो दण्डमर्हति ॥]] भागवत VII, [[14.8.|14. 8.]]

[[Bhaldi-The Puranas|Bhakti—The Purāṇas]] lay very great emphasis on bhakti (religion of loving faith in God). This is not the place to dilate on the history of the cult of Bhakti from the earliest times to modern days. For that purpose there are special treatises [[( some|(some]] of which are noted below) that may be consulted. But a few words on bhakti in general may be said here before going into the [[ques tion|question]] as to what the [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] have to say thereon. Traces of the doctrine of bhakti may be discovered even in the [[Rgvedio|Ṛgvedic]] hymns and mantras, some of which are full of loving faith in God, particularly in some of the hymns and [[vorges|verses]] addressed to [[Varuna|Varuṇa]] and also to Indra. A few examples may be [[cited. All|cited. 'All]] my thoughts[^1536] (or hymns) praise Indra in unison, seeking light, longing for him, as [[wivos|wives]] embrace their husband, their [[fat|fair]] young lover, they (thoughts) embrace him (Indra), the divine giver of gifts'; 'your friendship[^1537] (with your devotee) is indestructible [[( everlasting);|(everlasting);]] to him who desires a cow, you become a cow, to him who longs for a horse, may you be a [[horso;"|horse;']] 'O Indra, you are far better (or richer) than my father or my brother who does not feed me; (you) and my mother, O Vasu, are equal and protect (me) for (conferring) riches and [[favoura';|favours';]] [["You|'You]] gave to [[Kaksivat,|Kakṣīvat,]] who offered a hymn and Soma libation to you and who had grown old, [[Vrcaya,|Vṛcayā,]] who was a young [[P951]] woman; you became the wife of [[Vrsañaśva;|Vṛṣaṇaśva;]] all these [[(tavours)|(favours)]] of yours deserve to be loudly proclaimed during the offerings of soma libations'; 'you who, shining brilliantly, come to each house assuming the form of a small man, [[( Indral)|(O Indra!)]] drink this Soma juice (produced) by being crushed with my teeth and mixed with fried grains, gruel, cake and laud'. [[Comparo Rs.|Compare Ṛg.]] III. 43. 4, [[X|X.]] 42. 11, X. 112. 10 (in all of which Indra is called 'sakhā' [[friend )|friend)]] and I. [[104.9,|104. 9,]] [[VII, 32.26|VII. 32. 26]] (in both Indra is said to be like a father). It will be clear from these passages that the Vedic [[seges|sages]] had reached the stage of sakhya-bhakti, that the sages believed that Indra was like a mother, that he assumed the form of a wife for the sake of a devotee, that Indra partook soma juice from a devotee who, in the absence of the proper [[imple ments|implements]] for crushing soma stalks, extracted soma juice from soma stalks crushed with the devotee's own [[teeth,|teeth.]] These stories in the far-off ages of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] remind us of the stories in the works of the medieval ages about [[Rama|Rāma]] having accepted [[badars|badara]] fruit from a [[Sabari|Śabarī]] (Bhil woman) devotee who first tasted them with her teeth to see whether they were [[sweat|sweet]] or sour and the story of the God [[Vithobā|Viṭhobā]] of Pandharpur, having assumed the form of [[& mahar|a mahār]] (an untouchable) and paid up to the [[Moslom|Moslem]] king of Bijapur the money equivalent to the price of corn which, [[Damaji|Dāmājī]] (a great devotee), who was in charge of the royal granary, allowed people distressed by famine to take away. Some mantras addressed to Varuṇa show the same kind of sakhya-bhakti. [[Vasiṣtha|Vasiṣṭha]][^1537a] prays 'O [[Varupa I|Varuṇa!]] what is that great offence [[foom mitted|(committed]] by me) on account of which you desire to harm me, your friend and bard; declare that to me, O invincible and self-willed God, so that [[( after|(after]] propitiating you) I shall be free from sin and may be able quickly to approach you with [[adoration ';|adoration';]] [['whare|'where]] are those friendships of ours (of you and me) which we safely enjoyed in former times, O self-willed [[Varuṇa?;I|Varuṇa?; I]] (then) went to your big dwelling house that has a thousand doors; whatever offence we mortals commit against the divine [[hosta,|hosts,]] O [[Varuna,|Varuṇa,]] whatever laws of yours we may have violated in our ignorance [[(or heedlessness ),|(or heedlessness),]] do not, O God, harm us on [[80count|account]] of that sin'. It is remarkable that in the [[Rgveda thors|Ṛgveda there]] is a verse in which [[P952]] there is an [[apotheosia|apotheosis]][^1538] of [[.namas'|'namas']] (namaskāra, adoration or [[homage );'Adoration|homage); 'Adoration]] itself is mighty, I offer service with [[adora tion;|adoration;]] adoration upholds the heaven and the earth; adoration to the gods, adoration rules these gods, whatever sin is committed [[( by|(by]] me) I worship it away with adoration'.

Though the word 'bhakti' does not occur in the principal ancient Upaniṣads, the doctrine of the bhakti schools that it is God's grace alone that saves the devotee is found in the [[Katha|Kaṭha]] and [[Mundaka Upanisads,|Muṇḍaka Upaniṣads,]] viz. 'this Supreme Soul is not to be attained by expositions (of a teacher) nor by intelligence, nor by much learning; He is to be attained by him alone whom the Supreme Soul favours, to him this Supreme Soul discloses His [[form. "|form.']][^1539] This emphasizes the doctrine that God's Grace alone brings [[balvation|salvation]] to the [[devotee,|devotee.]] The [[Svetāśvataropaniṣad|Śvetāśvataropaniṣad]] employs the word bhakti in the same sense in which it is used in the [[Gita|Gītā]] and[^1540] other works on bhakti. [[These|'These]] matters declared [[( here)|(here)]] [[roveal|reveal]] themselves to that high-souled person who has the highest faith in God and the same faith in his guru as in God'. The [[sane|same]] [[Upanisad|Upaniṣad]] emphasizes [[&|a]] doctrine of the bhakti school in [[\*I,|'I,]] desirous of [[moksa|mokṣa]] (liberation from [[samsāra ),|saṁsāra),]] surrender myself as my refuge to that God who in former times created (established) Brahmā, who transmitted to him [[(Brahma )|(Brahmā)]] the Vedas, and who illuminates the intellect of the individual [[soul',|soul'.]]

[^1540]: यस्य देवे परा [[भक्तिधा|भक्तिर्यथा]] देवे तथा [[यो|गुरौ ।]] तस्यैते [[कथितार्थाः|कथिता ह्यर्थाः]] [[प्रकाश|प्रकाशन्ते]] [[महात्मनः। वाय.|महात्मनः ॥ श्वेताश्व.]] VI. 23; यो ब्रह्माणं विदधाति [[पूर्व|पूर्वं]] यो [[पेवाश्च महिणोति तले।|वै वेदांश्च प्रहिणोति तस्मै । तं ह]] [[देवमालद्धि Han Fra n .|देवमात्मबुद्धिप्रकाशं मुमुक्षुर्वै शरणमहं प्रपद्ये ॥ श्वेताश्व.]] VI. 18. This last verse is relied upon by [[wat|स्वप्नेश्वर]] in [[hla bhayya|his bhāṣya]] on [[nitq-\*|शाण्डिल्य-भक्तिसूत्र]] I. [[1.1.|1. 1.]] Barth in his [[Roligions|'Religions]] of India' [[trapalated|translated]] by [[).|J.]] Wood (3rd ed. [[1891 )|1891)]] sets out the grounds on [[whtab Western|which western]] scholars [[( particolarly|(particularly]] [[Weber )|Weber)]] held that the religion of loving faith in [[Krmna|Kṛṣṇa]] was due to Christian [[infuence|influence]] and then [[statos|states]] that [[bo|he]] is not [[satin fied|satisfied]] with this theory (pp. [[219-223 ).|219-223).]]

The word [[prapadye'|'prapadye']] in the [[Svetāóvatara|Śvetāśvatara]] serves as the basis of the doctrine of [[prapatti'|'prapatti']] in the Vaiṣṇavite system such as that of Rāmānuja.

But among the original sources of the Bhakti cult are the [[Nārā yapiya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section (chapters [[335-351|335–351]] of [[Citraśala|Citraśālā]] ed. = cr. ed. 322–339) of the [[Sāntiparva|Śāntiparva]] and the [[Bhagavadgita.|Bhagavadgītā.]] For the [[P953]] antiquity of [[Krsṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] worship (vide pp. 129-131 above). Megasthenes states that Heracles [[(Harikroṇa ? )|(Harikṛṣṇa?)]] was worshipped by Soursenoi [[(Saurasenas )|(Śaurasenas)]] on the banks of [[Jobaros|Jobares]] (Yamunā) and had two [[oities|cities]] Methora [[(Mathurā )|(Mathurā)]] and Cleisbora [[( Kṭspapura ? ).|(Kṛṣṇapura?).]] In the [[Nārāyaniya|Nārāyaṇīya]] it is stated (in chap. [[335,|335.]] 17-24) that king Uparicara Vasu was a devotee [[( bhakta )|(bhakta)]] of Nārāyaṇa, that he worshipped the Lord of Gods according to the [[sātivata|sāttvata]] rules that were proclaimed by the Sun, that he (Vasu) consecrated his kingdom, wealth, wife and horses to God, thinking that they all belonged to the Bhagavat [[(the Adorable One )|(the Adorable One)]] and performed sacrificial rites according to the Sāttvata rules.

In the [[Sāntiparva, Satvata and Pancarātra|Śāntiparva, Sātvata and Pāñcarātra]] are identified[^1541] and it is said that the seven sages called [['citraśikhandin'|'citraśikhaṇḍin']] (lit. whose top-knots on the head were bright or wonderful) viz. [[Marici,|Marīci,]] Atri, [[Angiras,|Aṅgiras,]] Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu and [[Vasistha,|Vasiṣṭha,]] proclaimed the [[(Pāñcarātra )|(Pāñcarātra)]] śāstra and God [[Nārāyana|Nārāyaṇa]] told them that the śāstra would be authoritative in the world and that king Vasu would learn the [[sāstra|śāstra]] from [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]] to whom it would come by degrees from the seven sages. Chapter 336 of [[Santi|Śānti]] declares that to the north of the Milky Sea there was territory called [[Svetadvipa,|Śvetadvīpa,]] where dwelt devotees of [[Nārāyana|Nārāyaṇa]] who were called[^1542] [[• Ekāntin'|'Ekāntin']] and Pāñcarātra is called 'Ekānta-dharma'. [[À|A]] peculiar doctrine of the Pāñcarātra school is that of the four [[wyūhas|vyūhas]] (mūrtis or forms) viz. that the Supreme Person is [[Vasudeva,|Vāsudeva,]] the individual soul is [[Sāṅkarṣana,|Saṅkarṣaṇa,]] [[Pradyuma|Pradyumna]] is mind and springs from [[Saṅkarsana|Saṅkarṣaṇa]] and Aniruddha is [[ahankāra|ahaṅkāra]] and originates from Pradyumna.[^1543] It is this doctrine of the four forms of Vāsudeva, each springing from the preceding, that is refuted according to [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] in the Brahmasūtra II. [[%.|2.]] 42-45. The [[śānti|Śānti]] 348. 8 expressly refers to the [[Gitā|Gītā]] as already declared [[P954]] to Arjuna. In[^1544] chap. 349. 64 it is said that [[Saṅkhya,|Sāṅkhya,]] Yoga, [[Panoarātra,|Pāñcarātra,]] Vedas and Pāśupata are five lores that differ in their views and were promulgated by Kapila [[( Saṅkhya ),|(Sāṅkhya),]] [[Hiranya garbha|Hiraṇyagarbha]] (Yoga), Apāntaratamas (the Veda), Śiva [[(Pāśupata ),|(Pāśupata),]] the [[Bhagavat|Bhāgavat]] Himself [[( promulgator|(promulgator]] of Pāñcarātra). The [[Viṣṇu dharmottara|Viṣṇudharmottara]][^1545] [[remarks for|remarks 'for]] seeking Brahma (the One Reality in the Universe) there are five Siddhāntas (systems) viz. [[Saṅkhya,|Sāṅkhya,]] Yoga, [[Pāñoarātra,|Pāñcarātra,]][^1546] Vedas and Pāśupata'. Relying on [[śānti parva|Śāntiparva]] 339. 68 several writers particularly those of the Rāmānuja school assert that the whole of the Pāñcarātra system has [[Vodic|Vedic]] [[P955]] authority, while others like [[Apararka|Aparārka]] p. 13 and [[Paribhayaprakasa|Paribhāṣāprakāśa]] [[(p. 23 )|(p. 23)]] do not accept it as thoroughly [[Vodio|Vedic]] but only [[partialy.|partially.]]

[^1541]: [[catalar Tree Prat: TCAE 41: i farlo FITCHHATRATA AR|काम्यनैमित्तिका राजन् यज्ञियाः परमक्रियाः । सर्वाः सात्वतमास्थाय विधिं चक्रे]] समाहितः । [[पाशरात्रविदो|पाञ्चरात्रविदो]] मुख्यास्तस्य गेहे [[महात्मनः।|महात्मनः ।]] [[मायण भगवस्त्रोक्त मुखवे बाबभोजनम्।|प्रायणं भगवत्प्रोक्तं भुञ्जते चाग्रभोजनम् ॥ शान्ति]] 335. 24-25.
