[^1543]: यो [[धारदेवो|वासुदेवो]] [[भगवान|भगवान्]] [[क्षेत्रको|क्षेत्रज्ञो]] [[निर्गुणात्मकः। शेयः|निर्गुणात्मकः । ज्ञेयः]] स एव [[राजेन्दनीयः सार्वणः 98: pronunt alga: Ħ Teutonius Serti \* mine|राजेन्द्र जीवः सङ्कर्षणः प्रभुः ॥ सङ्कर्षणाच्च प्रद्युम्नो मनोभूतः स उच्यते । प्रद्युम्नाद्योऽनिरुद्धस्तु सोऽहङ्कारः स ईश्वरः ॥ शान्ति०]] 339. 40-41.

[^1544]: सांख्यं योगः [[पाश्चरात्रं|पाञ्चरात्रं]] [[घेवाः|वेदाः]] पाशुपतं तथा । ज्ञानान्येतानि राजर्षे [[विधि Familia tn.|विद्धि नानामतानि वै ॥ शान्ति]] 349. 64; but later on it is said [[pic474 1 pre Trafat:|सांख्यस्य वक्ता कपिलः परमर्षिः]] स [[उच्यते।|उच्यते ।]] [["उक्तवानिवमव्यग्रो|... उक्तवानिदमव्यग्रो]] ज्ञानं पाशुपतं शिवः । [[पाश्चरात्रस्य कुस्नस्य घेत्ता|पाञ्चरात्रस्य कृत्स्नस्य वेत्ता]] तु भगवान् [[pret mai guste Tray Eau la|स्वयम् । सर्वेषु च नृपश्रेष्ठ ज्ञानेष्वेतेषु दृश्यते ॥ शान्ति]] 349. 65-68 q. by [[far|परिभाषाप्रकाश]] p. 21 [[( which|(which]] reads [[a TE 4).|वक्ता तु भगवान् स्वयम्).]] These verses are borrowed almost verbatim by the योगियाज्ञवल्क्य XII. 5-6 and the same work reads '[[सांखयं|सांख्यं]] योगं [[\*\*\*Ti 14Arraifa anat faaruri|... तथा । अतिप्रमाणान्येतानि हेतुभिर्न विचारयेत् ।']] XII. 4 (q. by [[tecro|अपरार्क]] on [[p11),|p. 11),]] which means that the five systems are beyond the ordinary means of [[koow ledge|knowledge]] and they should not be disturbed by mere ratiocination.

[^1545]: सांख्यं [[योग|योगं]] पञ्चरात्रं शैवं पाशुपतं तथा । कृतान्तपञ्चकं विद्धि [[बह्मणः|ब्रह्मणः]] परिमार्गणे ॥ [[Perguntargo 1.|विष्णुधर्मोत्तरपु० I.]] 74, 34 q. by [[ft. on ara|हे. on व्रत]] vol. I. p. 25 and [[aft:1974\#TE .|परिभाषाप्रकाश p.]] 22.

[^1546]: Why the [[colt|cult]] of [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] was called [[Pāācarātra bas aot beeg|Pāñcarātra has not been]] satisfactorily explained anywhere in the English Histories of Indian [[Philo sophy|Philosophy]] so far as I know. The name leads to the inference that the cult has something to do with five matters. But why is the word rātra or kāla used? That is the difficulty. [[Io Sāati|In Śānti]] 336. 46 (cr. ed. 323. 42) [[Pañca. satra|Pañcarātra]] is referred to as [[. Pañcatala'|'Pañcakāla']] ([[ante: quo terbat:),|'तैरिष्टः पञ्चकालज्ञैर्हरिरेकान्तिभिर्नरैः').]] [[Sevoral|Several]] guesses have [[bean|been]] put forward, some of which are: (1) on five nights [[Nārāyana|Nārāyaṇa]] taught Ananta, Garuḍa, [[Viśvakseoa,|Viśvaksena,]] Brahmā [[sad|and]] Rudra; (2) The [[Parama-sambita|Parama-saṁhitā]] [[(31. 19 )|(31. 19)]] 'states that God imparted this doctrine in five nights to four sages, [[Sadatkumāra, Sadaka, Sabandana and Sanatana;|Sanatkumāra, Sanaka, Sanandana and Sanātana;]] (3) this cult blackened (rātri is dark) five teachings, viz. [[164, M, QEVE, her and m ai|सांख्य, योग, पाशुपत, बौद्ध and आर्हत;]] (4) this [[(pāñcarātra )|(pāñcarātra)]] teaches five aspects [[, , fara (1. 8. mare), auttaa, sraf|पर, व्यूह, विभव (i. e. अवतार), अन्तर्यामिन्, अर्चा]] (images); (5) it dwells upon five duties of [[Valgavas viz,|Vaiṣṇavas viz.]] tāpa (branding on the arm and other limbs), [[puṇdra|puṇḍra]] ([[up right|upright]] lines made on the forehead with some colouring substance), [[dāma|nāma]] (names of [[Vasudeva ), wantra|Vāsudeva), mantra]] (like 'Om namo Nārāyaṇāya), yāga (worship of images of [[Vāsodeva ).|Vāsudeva).]] The [[Alwar|Ālwar]] literature mentions fivefold [[aature viz, Pars|nature viz. Para]] and the others. Vide K. [[Č.|C.]] Varadachari's paper on [[Some contri butions|'Some contributions]] of [[Alwars|Ālwars]] to the philosophy of bhakti' in Silver [[Jabilee|Jubilee]] vol. of BORI, p. 621. The [[Paramasa óbitā|Paramasaṁhitā]] [[(1.|(I.]] 39-40 [[G.O. S.|G. O. S.]] ed.) states that the five great Elements, the five [[Tanmatras, Abamkāra, Baddhi and aupakta, ( five|Tanmātras, Ahaṁkāra, Buddhi and avyakta, (five]] categories or Tattvas of the [[Saṅkhya )|Sāṅkhya)]] are the night [[( as it were )|(as it were)]] of the [[Puran|Puruṣa]] and therefore this [[lastra|śāstra]] (which propounds how to be free from the [[moshes|meshes]] of these [[five )|five)]] is called [[Pancarātra.|Pāñcarātra.]]

In the Vedāntasūtra[^1547] there are four sūtras dealing with the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] or [[Panoarātra|Pāñcarātra]] system. The great [[Acāryas|Ācāryas]] are not agreed upon their interpretation, [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] saying that all the four sūtras refute some tenets of the [[Bhagavatas,|Bhāgavatas,]] [[Rāmāpnja|Rāmānuja]] saying that the first two sūtras [[( out of four )|(out of four)]] contain refutation of the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] doctrine and the other two do not. [[Saṅkard cārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] makes it clear that the [[doctrinos|doctrines]] of the [[Bhagavatag|Bhāgavatas]] that the Supreme God Vāsudeva is the Highest Truth, that [[he|He]] assumes four forms, that the worship of Vāsudeva consists in continuous single-minded contemplation of [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] are not the targets for attack, that what is refuted is the [[dootrine|doctrine]] of the Bhāgavatas that the individual soul called [[Saṅkarṣana|Saṅkarṣaṇa]] by them springs from Vāsudeva, that Pradyumna [[( mind)|(mind)]] arises from [[Śhaṅkarasana|Saṅkarṣaṇa]] and that Aniruddha [[(ahamkāra )|(ahaṁkāra)]] arises from Pradyumna. It appears from [[Śhaṅkara's|Śaṅkara's]] remarks[^1548] on II. 2. 45 that in his day [[Śāṇdilya|Śāṇḍilya]] was supposed to have promulgated the Bhāgavata or [[Pāncarātra|Pāñcarātra]] śāstra, because he did not find the [[P956]] highest [[blles|bliss]] in all the four Vedas. In the [[Dropaparya|Droṇaparva]] ( 29. 26-29) there is another and different reference to the four [[murtis|mūrtis]] of the Supreme for the benefit of the worlds viz. one performs tapas ([[asuterities|austerities]]) on the earth, the 2nd has an eye on the world's good and evil deeds; the third comes to the world in a human form and does such acts as men perform; the 4th [[alumbers|slumbers]] for a thousand years and when it arises from slumber [[conferr|confers]] the highest boons on those who deserve them.

[^1547]: The four sūtras in the Vedāntasūtra (II. 2. 42-45) are: [[para, a nd rura, ferate an aquaru:, fardura.|उत्पत्यसम्भवात्, न च कर्तुः करणम्, विज्ञानादिभावे वा तदप्रतिषेधः, विप्रतिषेधाच्च.]] Though [[Rāma puja|Rāmānuja]] enlarges upon the [[Pancarātra|Pāñcarātra]] doctrine in his comments on the last two sūtras out of these four and quotes three passages that may be called [[Panca rātra,|Pāñcarātra,]] he does not avow either in his [[Srībbāsya|Śrībhāṣya]] or in [[bis|his]] [[Vedārtha-saigraha|Vedārtha-saṅgraha]] that he is a Sātvata or Pāñcarātra.

[^1548]: वेदविप्रतिषेधश्च भवति । [[चतुर्ष|चतुर्षु]] [[घेवेषु|वेदेषु]] परं [[श्रेयोऽलग्या|श्रेयोऽलब्ध्वा]] शाण्डिल्य इदं [[शासमाधि Taurorullarreara 1 SITE T164|शास्त्रमधिगतवानित्यादिवेदनिन्दादर्शनात् । शाङ्करभाष्य]] on [[II, 2. 43.|वेदान्तसूत्र II. 2. 45.]] [[Fyrard beglas' E HITTE Argan (on TEE|शङ्कराचार्य begins 'तत्र भागवता मन्यन्ते' (on ब्रह्मसूत्र]] II. 2. 42) and [[vader n.|under II.]] 2. 44 he [[rem marks|remarks]] '[[नच पचरात्रसिद्धान्तिभिर्वासदेवाविवकस्मिन् सर्वेषु पा ज्ञानेश्वर्याविवारतम्यता कश्चिदोन्युपगम्यते।।.|न च पञ्चरात्रसिद्धान्तिभिर्वासुदेवादिष्वेकस्मिन् सर्वेषु वा ज्ञानैश्वर्यादितारतम्यकृतः कश्चिद्भेदोऽभ्युपगम्यते ।'.]] It may further [[he|be]] noted that in the [[Santiparve पाशराब|Śāntiparva पाञ्चरात्र]] is called [[her|सात्वतधर्म]] (chap. 348. 34 and 84). [[Cros la his granta ( 8th great ),|बाण in his हर्षचरित (8th उच्छ्वास),]] while describing the persons professing various religious and [[pbilosophie cal|philosophical]] doctrines gathered near the great [[acārya|ācārya]] Divākara-mitra, separately [[men tions|mentions]] Bhāgavatas and [[Pañcaratrikas|Pāñcarātrikas]] '[[foutue Payout: forfatteren सुमन कापिलैजैनलोकायातक"पौराणिकैः सासवन्तः शैवैः शादः पाशराधिकरण्यैश्चात्र MET ME FATRATEL quafi|विटपच्छायासु निषण्णैः ... भागवतैर्वर्णिभिः केशलुञ्चनैः कापिलैर्जैनैर्लोकायतिकैः ... पौराणिकैः साप्ततन्तवैः शैवैः शाब्दैः पाञ्चरात्रिकैरन्यैश्चात्र स्वान् स्वान् सिद्धान्तान् शृण्वद्भिः]] &c.'. What Bāṇa probably means is that [[14|भागवत]] stands for the general cult of bhakti (in the [[Gitā )|Gītā)]] and [[for|पाञ्चरात्र for]] one school among [[pants,|भागवतs,]] that had as its characteristic the doctrine of four [[vyūbas.|vyūhas.]] This is like [[UTE OUTHTHUTRITT.|ब्राह्मणश्रमणन्याय.]] The [[Tiara|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति]] [[(11,|(11.]] 181–192.) states that [[Sagdilya|Śāṇḍilya]] composed a work for performing the worship of [[Vigga|Viṣṇu]] by a [[non Vedic|non-Vedic]] procedure, that [[Vimu|Viṣṇu]] cursed him to remain in hell but [[rolanted|relented]] when [[Sandilya|Śāṇḍilya]] threw himself on [[bis|his]] mercy and reduced the period of [[holl-residence|hell-residence.]]


It may be noted that even in the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] [[Nārada'a|Nārada's]] name is connected with [[Pancarātra.|Pāñcarātra.]] It is said [[' this|'this]] very secret doctrine, connected with the four Vedas, having the benefit of [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] and Yoga and spoken of as Pañcarātra, was (originally) uttered by the lips of Nārāyaṇa and was again communicated by [[Narada.|Nārada.]][^1549]

Other great sources of the cult of bhakti are the [[Bhagavad gita,|Bhagavadgītā,]] which is expressly mentioned in the [[Nārāyaṇiya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section [[(348.8 %D cr.ed. 336.8),|(348. 8 = cr. ed. 336. 8),]] the [[Bhagavatapurana|Bhāgavatapurāṇa]] and the [[Viṣṇu purāṇa.|Viṣṇupurāṇa.]] The [[Gitā|Gītā]] contains the words bhakti and bhakta several dozens of times. It may be stated here that the so-called [[Narada bhaktisutras,|Nārada-bhaktisūtras,]] [[Narada-pancarātra,|Nārada-pāñcarātra,]] the [[Sandilya-bhakti-sutra|Śāṇḍilya-bhakti-sūtra]] and the several extant [[Palivaratra-samhitas|Pāñcarātra-saṁhitās]] so far published are all later than the [[Gita.|Gītā.]][^1550] The [[Agnipurana|Agnipurāṇa]] gives the names of 25 works on [[Pancaratra|Pāñcarātra]] quoted in the note below.[^1551] The [[P957]] [[Mahesvara tantra|Maheśvaratantra]] names 25 [[Pancarātra|Pāñcarātra]] tantras promulgated by Viṣṇu, which are condemned therein as having no truth ( 26. 16 ff.).

[^1549]: [[इदं महोपनिष|इदं महोपनिषदं]] चतुर्वेदसमन्वितम् । सांख्ययोगकृतं तेन [[पारावानुशादितम्। नारायणालोदीतं नारदोऽभावयत्पुनः|पञ्चरात्रानुशब्दितम् । नारायणमुखोद्गीतं नारदोऽश्रावयत्पुनः]] । शान्ति 339. [[111-112(|111-112 (]] [[=:cr.|= cr.]] ed. 326. [[100-101).|100-101 ).]] The words [[इर्द"शब्दितम्|इदं … शब्दितम्]] are q. by [[रामानुन|रामानुज]] in his [[भाग्य 00 बास्त्र|भाष्य on ब्रह्मसूत्र]] II. [[2.45|2. 45]] (42 in [[रामानुजभाज्य B.S.S.).|रामानुजभाष्य B. S. S. ).]]

[^1550]: The reasons for this statement are not relevant to this work, but by way of example, attention may be drawn to some [[Sāṇdilya-rūtras|Śāṇḍilya-sūtras]] : [[तवेव कर्मिज्ञानियोगिग्य|तदेव कर्मिज्ञानियोगिभ्य]] आधिक्यशब्दात्, [[प्रश्ननिरूपणाम्यामाधिक्यसिद्धः|प्रश्ननिरूपणाभ्यामाधिक्यसिद्धेः]] (I. 2. 22-23 in Jivanand's ed. = I. 2. 15-16 in भक्तिचन्द्रिका), which clearly refer to [[Gita|Gītā]] VI. 46-47 and [[XII, 1|XII. 1]] and 6-7 respectively. There is one sūtra where the [[Gita|Gītā]] is expressly mentioned viz. सैकान्तभावो गीतार्थप्रत्यभिज्ञानात् (II. 2. 83 in [[Jiva nand's|Jivanand's]] [[ed. -|ed. =]] II. [[2.28|2. 28]] in भक्तिचन्द्रिका). [[स्वमेश्वर|स्वप्नेश्वर]] comments on this [[सा परा भक्तिरेवैकान्तभावो नान्यः कुतः। मीतार्थेन प्रत्यभिज्ञाश्रवणात्। पथा|सा पराभक्तिरेवैकान्तभावो नान्यः । कुतः । गीतार्थेन प्रत्यभिज्ञाश्रवणात् । यथा]] नारायणीय एव [['सहोप. निषदो"ऋषिणापि धा'|'सहोपनिषदो … ऋषिणापि वा']] (शान्ति 348. 5-6) इति [[प्रक्षे प्रत्युत्तरम्।|प्रश्ने प्रत्युत्तरम् ।]] [['समुपोढेष्वनीकेषु" भंगवता खयम् ।।|'समुपोढेष्वनीकेषु … भगवता स्वयम् ।']] (शान्ति [[348.8|348. 8]]) । [[तस्मादेकान्तिता परा भक्तिरिति ।।|तस्मादेकान्तिता परा भक्तिरिति ।]]

[^1551]: [[विष्ण्वादीनां प्रतिष्ठादि पक्ष्ये ब्रह्मन् शृणुष्व मे। प्रोक्तानि पञ्चरात्राणि समराबाणि मयाज्यस्तानि सनिमिौके पञ्चविंशतिसंख्यया । हयशीर्ष तन्त्रमा तन्वं त्रैलोक्यमोहनम् । "सापर्व कापिलं च साक्ष्य नारायणीयकम् ॥ आत्रेय नारसिंहाख्यमानम्बाख्यं सधावणम् । पौधायन तथाटाविश्वोक्तं तस्य सारतः अमिपु.|विष्ण्वादीनां प्रतिष्ठादि वक्ष्ये ब्रह्मन् शृणुष्व मे । प्रोक्तानि पञ्चरात्राणि सप्तरात्राणि वै मया ॥ व्यस्तानि मुनिभिर्लोके पञ्चविंशतिसंख्यया । हयशीर्ष तन्त्रमाद्यं तन्त्रं त्रैलोक्यमोहनम् ॥ … स्वायम्भुवं कापिलं च तार्क्ष्यं नारायणीयकम् ॥ आत्रेयं नारसिंहाख्यमानन्दाख्यं तथारुणम् ॥ बौधायनं तथाष्टाङ्गं विश्वोक्तं तस्य सारतः ॥ अग्निपु०]] 39. 1-5. Does नारायणीयक in the verse above refer to the नारायणीय section of the [[महाभारत!|महाभारत ?]]

There is an extensive Literature on the Bhakti cult. Only a few of the important works in Sanskrit, their translations and works in English are noted here. Barth, Hopkins, Keith, Dr. R. G. Bhandarkar and others have propounded different theories as to what [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] represented, how he was identified with Viṣṇu who appears to be another name of the Sun in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] and came to be regarded as the highest God in [[Brahmana|Brāhmaṇa]] times (as in Ait. Br. 'Agnir-vai devānām [[avamah,|avamaḥ,]] [[Viṣṇuh|Viṣṇuḥ]] [[paramaḥ)|paramaḥ')]] and came to be identified with sacrifice ([[Yajāo|Yajño]] vai Viṣṇuḥ). When [[Kroṇa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] the friend of the [[Pandavas|Pāṇḍavas]] was identified with the Supreme Spirit, the full-fledged doctrine of Avatāras appeared as in the [[Gita.|Gītā.]] The important works on bhakti are : The [[Narayapiya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section of the [[Santiparya|Śāntiparva]] (chapters 322–351 in the Citraśālā edition and chap. 322-339 of the critical edition ); the [[Bhagavadgitā;|Bhagavadgītā;]] several Purāṇas, the most important being Viṣṇu and [[Bhagavata ;|Bhāgavata;]][^1552] the Bhaktisūtra of [[Sandilya|Śāṇḍilya]] with the bhāṣya of Svapneśvara (ed. by Jivananda, Calcutta 1876) and translation of both these ( in B. I. series ) by E. B. Cowell [[P958]] (1878); [[Śandilya's|Śāṇḍilya's]] [[samhita|saṁhitā]] ( [[Bhaktikhanda)|Bhaktikhaṇḍa)]] in the [[Sarasvati bhavan|Sarasvatībhavan]] Series, edited by Anantasastri Phadke, (1935); [[Nārada bhaktisūtra|Nārada-bhaktisūtra]] with English Translation by Nandlal Sinha ([[Panini|Pāṇini]] office, Allahabad, 1911); [[tho|the]] Nārada-pāñcarātra ( containing the [[Jõānāmrtasāra|Jñānāmṛtasāra]] section) in [[sloven|eleven]] chapters ( ed. for B. I. Series by [[O.|C.]] M. Banerji, Calcutta, 1865 ) and English translation of it by Swami Vijñānānanda (Pāṇini Office, Allahabad, 1921); Sir R. G. [[Bhadarkar's|Bhandarkar's]] [["Vaignavigm,|'Vaiṣṇavism,]] [[Saivism|Śaivism]] &c.' (1913, in the [[Enoyclopaedia|Encyclopaedia]] of Indo-Aryan Research ); 'Das-Gupta's History of Indian Philosophy' vol. IV. (1949), wherein he deals with the Bhāgavatapurāṇa and the doctrines of [[Madhya,|Madhva,]] Vallabha, Caitanya and their followers; Grierson's paper [[Gleanings|'Gleanings]] from Bhaktamāla of Nābhādāsa', in J. R. A. S. for 1909 pp. 607-644; [[• History|'History]] of [[Śrivaiṣṇavas',|Śrīvaiṣṇavas',]] by T. A. Gopinatha Rao (Madras, [[1993),|1923),]] 'the Gospel of Nārada' by Duncan Greenless (Adyar, 1951); [[Narada-bhakti-sūtras|Nārada-bhakti-sūtras]] ( text, translation and notes ) ed. by Swami [[Tyagisapanda|Tyāgīśānanda]] (Ramakrisna Math, Mylapore, Madras, 1943 ) in five adhyāyas and 84 sūtras; the [[Abirbudhnya-samhita|Ahirbudhnya-saṁhitā]] in two volumes ( Adyar, 1916 ); [[Di,|Dr.]] F. Otto Schrader's Introduction to the [[Pañcarātra|Pāñcarātra]] and [[Abirbudhnya-gamhita|Ahirbudhnya-saṁhitā]] (Adyar, 1916 ); [[Jayakhya-samhita|Jayākhya-saṁhitā]] with English and Sanskrit Introductions [[(G.O. 9.|(G. O. S.]] 1931); the [[Parama-samhita|Parama-saṁhitā]] [[(G.0. 8.,|(G. O. S.,]] 1946 ) with English Translation by Dr. S. [[K,|K.]] Aiyangar; the [[Bșhadbrahmanamhita|Bṛhadbrahmasaṁhitā]] (of [[Narada-pāžoarātra|Nārada-pāñcarātra]] ) in the [[Anandasrama|Ānandāśrama]] series, 1912); [[Bhakticandrika|Bhakticandrikā]] ( commentary on [[Sandilya's|Śāṇḍilya's]] [[Bhaktisātras)|Bhaktisūtras)]] by [[Narayanatirtha|Nārāyaṇatīrtha]] ( [[Sarasvatibhavan|Sarasvatībhavan]] series, 1921, 1938 ); [[Bhakti prakāśa|Bhaktiprakāśa]] of Mitramiśra ( Chowkhamba Series, 1934 ); [[Bhakti nirṇaya|Bhaktinirṇaya]] of [[Anantadora|Anantadeva]] (ed. by Pandit Anantasastri Phadke, Banaras, 1937). There is a good deal of Bhakti literature in South India, such as the hymns of the [[Alwārs,|Āḷwārs,]] but no reference is made here to it for several reasons.

[^1552]: It is remarkable that the [[Bhagavata,|Bhāgavata,]] [[wbich|which]] is practically the most important or sole authority for all great medieval [[Vaigava|Vaiṣṇava]] teachers like Vallabha and Caitanya and their disciples, is not quoted anywhere by [[Rāmā nuja|Rāmānuja]] ( who was born in [[sake|śake]] 1049 i. e. 1127 A. D.) in his [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] on the Brahmasūtras, when he quotes over a hundred verses from the Viṣṇupurāṇa in the same. In fact, in the [[Vedārthsaigraba|Vedārthasaṅgraha]] Rāmānuja states that [[la|in]] the [[name|same]] way as the section (anuvāka) on Nārāyaṇa among all [[fruti|śruti]] texts serves to expound the special aspect of highest Brahma so the [[Vigopurāga|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] also defines a special aspect of the highest Brahma and that all other [[Purīgas|Purāṇas]] should be so interpreted as [[aot|not]] to be in conflict with it [[at hele tatt केवलपरमाखरूपविशेषगदर्शनापैव प्रात्तो नारापणादवाकस्तयेवं वैष्णवं च पुराण"परमहा स्वरूपविशेषनिर्णयायैव प्रवृत्तम् । अन्यानि सर्वाणि पुराणान्येतदविरोधेन नेयानि। पेवार्थसंग्रह|‘यथा सर्वासु श्रुतिषु केवलपरब्रह्मस्वरूपविशेषप्रदर्शनायैव प्रवृत्तो नारायणानुवाकस्तथैवं वैष्णवं च पुराणं … परब्रह्मस्वरूपविशेषनिर्णयायैव प्रवृत्तम् । अन्यानि सर्वाणि पुराणान्येतदविरोधेन नेयानि । वेदार्थसंग्रह]] para 110-111 pp. 141-142 (D. C. ed. 1956). [[THEO meations (in weet on a r II. 2. 41 and 45 ) HochfEAT, HIGHET|रामानुज mentions (in भाष्य on वेदान्तसूत्र II. 2. 41 and 45 ) पौष्करसंहिता, सात्वतसंहिता]] and [[Tropicar|परमसंहिता]] among the [[पाश्चरावसंहिताs,|पाञ्चरात्रसंहिताs,]] but does nowhere [[afirm|affirm]] that he is a [[tollower|follower]] of the [[परात्र|पञ्चरात्र]] [[doctring.|doctrine.]] There are numerous commentaries on the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] and [[com mentaries|commentaries]] on commentaries (Das Gupta in vol. 4 pp. 1-2 lists over 40 commentaries on the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]]). It is [[donecessary|unnecessary]] in this work to refer to the numerous commentaries of the disciples and followers of Madhva and other great [[Valsnava|Vaiṣṇava]] ācāryas. The position of Vallabhācārya ( 1479–1531 A. D.) appears to be that the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] is the supreme authority in case of doubt [[पेदा श्रीकृष्णवाक्यानि ग्यासस्वाणि चैवाहि।समाधिभाषा व्यासस्य प्रमाण तश्चाष्टयम्। Tu g iterary natate fpe|‘वेदाः श्रीकृष्णवाक्यानि व्याससूत्राणि चैव हि । समाधिभाषा व्यासस्य प्रमाणं तच्चतुष्टयम् । उत्तरं पूर्वसन्देहवारकं परिकीर्तितम् ॥ तत्त्वदीपनिबन्ध]] (Ahmedabad, 1926); vide also Prof. G. H. Bhatt in I. H. Q. vol. IX, 300-306. [[Vallabbācārya's|Vallabhācārya's]] is called [[g (moaning Foot FAR)|पुष्टिमार्ग (meaning कृष्णानुग्रह)]] and he holds that even bhakti [[ia|is]] difficult to accomplish in the Kali age.

Before proceeding with the treatment of bhakti in the Purāṇas the words 'bhakti' and [['Bhagavata'|'Bhāgavata']] must be briefly defined and explained. [[Sāṇdilya|Śāṇḍilya]] defines[^1552a] 'bhakti' as [['s|'sā]] [[P959]] [[paranuraktirisyare'.|parānuraktirīśvare'.]] This may be interpreted in two [[ways the|ways 'the]] highest form of bhakti is affection fixed on God' or 'bhakti is the highest affection fixed on God'. [[Svapnegyara,|Svapneśvara,]] the author of a [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] on [[Sandilya,|Śāṇḍilya,]] prefers the first and [[Narada-bhakti sūtra,|Nārada-bhakti-sūtra,]] Tilak and others favour the second. [[Svapnesvara|Svapneśvara]] explains that in general 'bhakti' means 'affection fixed on a being that is to be won over or worshipped', but that in this [[sastra|śāstra]] it means 'a particular state of the mind having the Supreme Lord as its object' and quotes [[& verge|a verse]] from the Viṣṇupurāṇa uttered by the great devotee [[Prahlada May|Prahlāda 'May]] that fixed (or unwavering) love, which the ignorant (or unreflecting) people feel for worldly objects, [[nover|never]] depart from my heart, ever remembering you'. The [[Gitā|Gītā]] employs the word [['priti'|'prīti']] (affection) and conveys that the word 'bhakti' is derived from the root 'bhaj' to resort [[to, 'Those|to. 'Those]] whose minds are on me, whose lives are offered to me, who instruct each other, who speak of me, are always contented and happy. On these who are continuously devoted to me and serve me with affection, I bestow that knowledge by which they [[roach|reach]] me'. [[Svapnośvara|Svapneśvara]] explains that the word 'anurakti' (with prefix 'anu') is employed to convey that the affection for God arises after the devotee secures knowledge of the greatness and other attributes of the Bhagavat (Adorable One). In the Viṣṇupurāṇa the word 'anurāga' is used for 'bhakti', where, after describing the ascent of Rāma and his brothers to heaven, it says that the people of the capital of Kosala who had deep affection for those incarnate parts of the Bhagavat [[(Viṣṇu )|(Viṣṇu),]] having their minds fixed on them, reached the position of residence in the same world with them. [[Sārdilya|Śāṇḍilya]] further[^1553] says that there is the teaching that there is immortality for him who abides in Him. In the [[Chandogya|Chāndogya]] Up. it is said 'He who abides in brahman reaches immortality'. The idea is that immortality being the promise of abiding in God, there will be no indifference in the effort to know God or in the effort to [[P960]] cultivate highest affection for God. It may be noticed that the sūtras of Nārada appear to be a [[more|mere]] paraphrase of [[Sandilya's|Śāṇḍilya's]] [[aphoriams.|aphorisms.]][^1554] [[Sandilya|Śāṇḍilya]] [[furthor|further]] (sūtra 7) provides that bhakti, like knowledge, is not an action because it does not follow an effort of the will and that (sūtra 9) it is different from jñāna, as the [[Gitā|Gītā]] refers to self-surrender being attained by one having knowledge after many births [[(Gitā VII 19).|(Gītā VII. 19).]]

[^1552a]: [[Burant TITETI FT Terindai stiera|अथातो भक्तिजिज्ञासा । सा परानुरक्तिरीश्वरे । शाण्डिल्य]] I. 1. 1-2; [[\*\*\*|स्वप्नेश्वर]] comments: [[भाराग्यविषयकरागत्वमेव|आराध्यविषयकरागत्वमेव]] सा। इह तु [[परमेश्वरविषयकान्तःकरणपत्तिविशेष|परमेश्वरविषयकान्तःकरणवृत्तिविशेष]] एव [[oftal.|भक्तिः ।]] The verse relied on is: [[9 ftfacfadfitat framfaraft I ca: TÀ Gane a a Aug.|या प्रीतिरविवेकानां विषयेष्वनपायिनी । त्वामनुस्मरतः सा मे हृदयान्मापसर्पतु ॥ विष्णुपु०]] [[1.|I.]] 20. 19. [[Pisey|स्वप्नेश्वर]] also quotes [[in afara og भाणा पोषयन्तः परस्परम् । कथयन्तश्च मा नित्यं तुज्यन्ति धमन्ति च तेषां सत्वयुकाना wat afectat aarta parath o da pigemaru|गीता ‘मच्चित्ता मद्गतप्राणा बोधयन्तः परस्परम् । कथयन्तश्च मां नित्यं तुष्यन्ति च रमन्ति च ॥ तेषां सततयुक्तानां भजतां प्रीतिपूर्वकम् । ददामि बुद्धियोगं तं येन मामुपयान्ति ते ॥]] X. 9-10. On [[surf bo|अनुरक्ति he]] remarks [[o f Allaamas Tart framtranften 1.|‘भगवन्महिमादिज्ञानादनु पश्चात् जायमानाऽनुरक्तिरित्युक्तम्’.]] [[martam|स्वप्नेश्वर]] [[rofers|refers]] to [[fegyret|विष्णुपुराण]] IV. 4. 103 [[ano ang orasimas uforor: TEMATRIA Mato सस्तत्सालोक्यतामेवाः ,|‘येपि तेषु भगवदंशेष्वनुरागिणः कोसलनगरजानपदास्तेपि तन्मनसस्तत्सालोक्यतामेवापुः’.]] The भागवत holds that the highest bhakti must be [[dis interested|disinterested]] and uninterrupted; [[regeerarea a . gouterai 779|अहैतुक्यव्यवहिता या भक्तिः पुरुषोत्तमे । भागवत]] [[III|III.]] 29. 12. The next verse mentions the four stages of beatitude [[Flutuante Hraficquignenty|‘सालोक्यसार्ष्टिसामीप्यसायुज्यैकत्वमप्युत’]] ([[gency|एकत्व]] is [[Sth,|5th,]] final goal).

[^1553]: [[Fredreracadedant entoera|तत्संस्थस्यामृतत्वोपदेशात् । शाण्डिल्य]] I.1.30; [[TT explalos; una FRAT E TUTTH: 1.|स्वप्नेश्वर explains; ‘तस्मिन्नीश्वरे संस्था भक्तिर्यस्य स तथोक्तः ।’.]] The [[urta|छान्दोग्य]] declares [[parasticama: (. 23.1 )|‘ब्रह्मसंस्थोऽमृतत्वमेति’ (II. 23.1)]] and the same meaning is conveyed by [[HE I, ,7 A hma'. .|ब्रह्मसूत्र I. 1. 7 ‘तन्निष्ठस्य मोक्षोपदेशात्’.]]

[^1554]: [[Furnt of 4Te4FYTA: 1 AT ARATOTTHAEYTI S TI HIET who|अथातो भक्तिं व्याख्यास्यामः । सा त्वस्मिन्परप्रेमरूपा । अमृतस्वरूपा च । नारदभक्तिसूत्र]] 1-3.

Our ancestors had a great penchant for classifications, divisions and sub-divisions. Bhakti is divided into [[laukiki|laukikī]] (of the common people), [[Vaidiki|Vaidikī]] (laid down by the Veda) and [[adhyatmiki|ādhyātmikī]] (philosophical), as in Padma V. 15. 164; or [[mānasi|mānasī]] (mental), [[vāciki|vācikī]] (verbal) and [[kāyiki|kāyikī]] ( done with the body, such [[es|as]] fasts, vratas &c.) in Padma V. 15. 165–168; into [[Sāttviki,|Sāttvikī,]] [[Rājasi|Rājasī]] and [[Tāmasi|Tāmasī]] (as in [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] III. 29.7–10 and Padma VI. 126. 4-11); into best, middling and inferior as in [[Brahmānda|Brahmāṇḍa]] III.[^1555] 34. 38-41 ).

[^1555]: The [[mentions mig, g, stuttu, adresa, asf, , fortyor, ara, nits and gas|ब्रह्माण्ड mentions नारद, शुक, अम्बरीष, रन्तिदेव, मारुति, बलि, बिभीषण, प्रह्लाद, गोपीs and उद्धव as]] exemplars of highest [[hi fs and \#2|भक्ति; वसिष्ठ and मनु]] as practising [[Arya|मध्यमा]] and ordinary men as practising [[kanigtbā|kaniṣṭhā]] (inferior) [[fer.|भक्ति.]] The [[author|नारदभक्तिसूत्र]] 83 mentions many of these as [[CGTTE: :'|भक्त्याचार्याः : ‘इत्येवं]] [[बदन्ति|वदन्ति]] । जनजल्पनिर्भया एकमताः [[कुमारण्यासशुकशाण्डिल्यगर्गविष्णुकौण्डिल्य-शेष-उद्धव Hraforcunstaurant Rurart: 11.|कुमारव्यासशुकशाण्डिल्यगर्गविष्णुकौण्डिन्य-शेष-उद्धव-आरुणिबलिहनुमद्विभीषणादयो भक्त्याचार्याः ।’.]] [[Gare|कुमार]] stands for [[914,|नारद,]] son of [[a.|ब्रह्मा.]]

Prapatti (self-surrender) is distinguished from bhakti in works of the Rāmānuja and of other Vaiṣṇava schools. It consists of five points[^1556] viz. resolution to yield (to God's will), the abandoning of opposition, faith that God will protect (the devotee ), praying to God to save the devotee, and a feeling of helplessness shown by casting one's soul on Him. Bhakti [[bas As|has as]] synonyms the [[words' dhyāna',|words 'dhyāna',]] 'upāsanā' [[&o.|&c.]] and is subsidiary to prapatti. The [[Gita|Gītā]] explicitly makes no such distinction. In [[Gita|Gītā]] II. 7 Arjuna speaks of himself as 'prapanna' (who has approached or surrendered himself for salvation). The final advice at the end of the [[Gitā|Gītā]] [[enjoing|enjoins]] what is called prapatti in later works 'On me fix your mind, become my devotee, sacrifice [[P961]] to me, offer adoration [[tome;|to me;]] you will certainly reach me; I declare to you truly, you are dear to me. Giving up all (dharmas) duties, come to me as your (only) refuge; I shall release you from all [[ging;|sins;]] do not [[griove :|grieve'.]][^1557] Vide also [[Gita VII,|Gītā VII.]] 14, 15 and XV. 4 for other instances of the use of the root 'pra-pad'. The theory propounded in the Gītā and other works on bhakti was that bhakti led on to [['prasada'|'prasāda']] (favour or grace) of God which enables the devotee to attain mokṣa.[^1558] The [[Gitā|Gītā]] (18.56, 58, 62 ) says 'a man, though always performing all actions, but solely depending on me, obtains an imperishable and eternal place through my favour; if you fix your mind on me, you will get over all difficulties through my favour; go to Him as the refuge with all your heart, O Arjuna, through His grace you will attain the highest peace and an everlasting [[abode.|abode.']] In the [[Vispupurāṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] Prahlāda[^1559] is told by the Adorable [[One as|One 'as]] your mind is firmly and devotedly fixed on me you will by my favour attain the highest bliss'. The idea of God's grace [[oocurs|occurs]] both in the [[Katha|Kaṭha]] and [[Svetāśvatara|Śvetāśvatara]] Upaniṣads :[^1560] [[The|'The]] Self, smaller than the small and greater than the great, is hidden in the heart of the creatures; a man who is free from willing anything and free from grief sees the greatness of the Self through the favour of the Creator'.

[^1556]: [[ध्यानशब्दवाच्या भक्तिविद्याभेदाबहुविधा। ""पत्तिर्नाम-आपकल्यस्य साल्पा पातिकल्पस्य पर्जनम् । रक्षिष्यतीति विश्वासो गोमववरण तथा आत्मनिक्षेपकार्पण्यम्-पल्याच पाकयुका।|ध्यानशब्दवाच्या भक्तिर्विद्याभेदाद्बहुविधा । … ‘प्रपत्तिर्नाम–आनुकूल्यस्य सङ्कल्पः प्रातिकूल्यस्य वर्जनम् । रक्षिष्यतीति विश्वासो गोप्तृत्ववरणं तथा । आत्मनिक्षेपकार्पण्यम्–इत्याद्यङ्गपञ्चकयुक्ता ।]] यतीन्द्रमतदीपिका [[p.64.|p. 64.]] This work remarks that this [[मपत्ति|प्रपत्ति]] must [[bo|be]] learnt from the lips of a [[gurd|guru]] and therefore it does not expound it. Some read [[आमनिक्षेपः कापण्यं|आत्मनिक्षेपः कार्पण्यं]] and thus raise the [[aigas|aṅgas]] of [[प्रपति|प्रपत्ति]] to six.

There is a great difference between the [[Gita|Gītā]] and the [[Nārāyaniya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section. In the former, though the Supreme Soul is called [[Vasudeva,|Vāsudeva,]][^1561] the doctrine of the four [[vyūbas,|vyūhas,]] that is very [[P962]] characteristic of the [[Nārāyaniya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section, is totally [[absont|absent]] and further even the names of [[Saṅkarṣana,|Saṅkarṣaṇa,]] Pradyumna and [[Ani ruddha|Aniruddha]] do not occur in the [[Gita.|Gītā.]] In my opinion the [[Gita|Gītā]] is the older of the two, as it propounds the general doctrine of bhakti, while the Pāñcarātra doctrine in the [[Nārāyaniya|Nārāyaṇīya]] is only one of the several bhakti schools. Moreover, the [[Nārāyaplya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section represents that [[Gita|Gītā]] had already been proclaimed and that the knowledge brought from [[Śyetadvipa|Śvetadvīpa]] by Nārada is the same as that declared in the [[Harigita|Harigītā]] (chap. 346. 10–11, 348. 53-54). [[Santi|Śānti]] ( 348. 55-57 ) mentions that there was only one vyūha or there were two, three or four and that the Ekāntins attached great importance to [[ahimsā.|ahiṁsā.]] Besides, the worship of [[Vābudova|Vāsudeva]] is older than Pāṇini, since[^1562] Pāṇini teaches the formation of the word [[Vasudevaka|Vāsudevaka]] as meaning 'one whose object of worship is [[Vasudeva'.|Vāsudeva'.]] Vide Dr. Bhandarkar's [[Vaiṣṇavism,|'Vaiṣṇavism,]] [[Saivism|Śaivism]] &c.' paragraphs 2-10 (pp. 3–19 of vol. IV of the collected works) for the antiquity of [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] worship. The general view of most medieval writers on Dharmaśāstra about [[Pancarātra|Pāñcarātra]] is represented by the Pārijāta quoted in the K. R. that the [[Panca rātra|Pāñcarātra]][^1563] and Pāśupata śāstras are authoritative only so far as they are not opposed to the Vedas. This was the view of the [[Sūtasambitā|Sūtasaṁhitā]] also, on which a commentary was composed by the famous [[Madhavācārya.|Mādhavācārya.]]

In some Purāṇas the word [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] is not derived[^1564] from Vasudeva ( as the son of Vasudeva) but from the root [[yas'|'vas']] to [[dwell..|dwell.]] [[Vāsudeva|'Vāsudeva]] is so called because all beings dwell in the Highest Self and [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] dwells in beings as the soul of all'. Compare [[Gitā|Gītā]] 9. [[29'I|29 'I]] am alike to all beings; none is odious to me nor dear; but those who worship me with faith dwell in me and I too dwell in them'.

[^1564]: [[Heffort arma ART THRARI ng F FIAT TETTE: m a facuge VI. 5. 80, Wigo 233, 68 (reads faca TRTA).|सर्वाणि तत्र भूतानि वसन्ति परमात्मनि । भूतेषु च स सर्वात्मा वासुदेवस्ततः स्मृतः ॥ विष्णुपु० VI. 5. 80, ब्रह्मपु० 233. 68 (reads निवसन्ति परात्मनि).]] There is another verse [[gag cha U FRYE TE IUTAT for at mga aparat \*\*: \* frogge VI. 5. 82, Turgo|‘भूतेषु वसते योन्तर्वसन्त्यत्र च तानि यत् । धाता विधाता जगतां वासुदेवस्ततः प्रभुः ॥ विष्णुपु० VI. 5. 82, ब्रह्मपु०]] 233. 70 ( but it says that this verse [[contalas|contains]] what [[Prajapati|Prajāpati]] declared to great sages). [[Forage|विष्णुपु०]] (I. 2. 12-13) [[states' font For They a r: ITT! \# aaa Pag : ortove \#.'|states ‘सर्वत्रासौ समस्तं च वसत्यत्रेति वै यतः । ततः स वासुदेवेति विद्वद्भिः परिपठ्यते ॥’.]]

[[P963]] The word bhagavat [[oalls|calls]] for a brief notice. It was generally applied to [[Vasudeva.|Vāsudeva.]] The [[Viṣṇupuriṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] [[says The|says 'The]] word bhaga is applied to the six qualities collectively viz. fulness of sway, manliness (or energy), glory, auspiciousness, knowledge and indifference to [[wordly|worldly]] objects. This noble word bhagavān applies to [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] who is the Highest Brahma and to no one else'.[^1565] The [[Viṣṇupurana|Viṣṇupurāṇa]][^1566] further states that the word [[+ bhagavat'|'bhagavat']] may be applied secondarily to others on the ground of possessing special qualities " that person may be [[oalled|called]] 'bhagavān', who knows about the creation and dissolution (of the world), about the origin and final destiny of beings, and knows what is [[vidya|vidyā]] and avidyā ( nescience). Knowledge, strength power, sway, manliness, [[splendour all|splendour—all]] these in their entirety, excluding the [[thres|three]] guṇas (and their effects ) that are to be avoided, are expressed by the word bhagavat". [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] is one who worships [['Bhagavat'(ie. Vasudeva).|'Bhagavat' (i. e. Vāsudeva).]] This is an ancient word. It [[ocours|occurs]] in the [[Begnagar|Besnagar]] column inscription [[of|(of]] the 2nd century [[BC.)|B. C.)]] of Heliodora, a Greek of [[Takṣaśila|Takṣaśilā]] and ambassador of Antalikita, who calls himself a [[bhagavata|bhāgavata]] [[(adevotee|(a devotee]] of [[Vasudeva);|Vāsudeva);]] vide above p. 516 n. 742 and 'Indo-Greeks' by Prof. A. [[K,|K.]] Narain (1957), where at end [[hegives|he gives]] the full Besnagara Ins. of nine lines and its reading (Plate VI.). It appears that [['bhagayat'|'bhagavat']] was rarely applied to [[Siya|Śiva]] also. The [[Svetāśvatara|Śvetāśvatara]] Upaniṣad speaks[^1567] of [[Sive|Śiva]] as [['bhagavan'.|'bhagavān'.]] [[Patanjali|Patañjali]] in his [[bhasya|bhāṣya]] on [[Panini|Pāṇini]] [[V.2.76|V. 2. 76]] speaks of [[Śivabhagavata.|Śivabhāgavata.]][^1568] In the [[Ghostindi|Ghosūṇḍī]] stone [[P964]] Inscription[^1569] in Sanskrit ( near [[Nagari|Nagarī]] in the Chitorgadh District of Rajputana ) there is a reference to both Saṅkarṣaṇa and [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] as [[bhagarat|bhagavat]] and it calls them Sarveśvara (about 200 B. C.), while the Besnagara inscription speaks of [[Vāsudova|Vāsudeva]] alone and Heliodora calls himself [[bbāgavata.|bhāgavata.]] In some early records such as the Pikira grant of [[Simbavarman|Siṁhavarman]] ( E. I. vol. VII. p. 161 ) and in Gupta Inscription No. 4 (at p. 27) [[Simbavarinen|Siṁhavarman]] and Candragupta (II) son of Samudragupta are called [[paramabhāgauna'.|'paramabhāgavata'.]] Akrūra is styled mahābhāgavata in [[Brahma purāṇa|Brahmapurāṇa]] [[190, 20;|190. 20;]] Padma VI. 280. 27 defines a [[Mahabhāgavata|Mahābhāgavata]] as noted below.[^1570]

[^1565]: ऐश्वर्यस्य समग्रस्य वीर्यस्य यशसः [[श्रिया।|श्रियः ।]] ज्ञानवैराग्ययोश्चैव [[पण्णा|षण्णां]] भग [[इसीरणों।|इतीरणा ॥]] [[एवमेव|एवमेष]] [[महान्छब्दो|महाञ्छब्दो]] मैत्रेय भगवानिति । परमब्रह्मभूतस्य [[धासुदेवस्य|वासुदेवस्य]] [[नाग्यगः|नान्यगः]] ॥ [[विष्णुपु.|विष्णुपु०]] [[VI..|VI. 5.]] 74 and 76. The [[पहारीसस्मृति|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति]] [[VL.|VI.]] 164-165 has [[ऐश्वर्य|ऐश्वर्यं]] च तथा [[धीर्य देवः शति जुत्तमा।|वीर्यं तेजः शक्तिरनुत्तमा ।]] ज्ञानं [[पलं|बलं]] यदेतेषां षण्णां भग इतीरितः । [[एभिण: अपूर्णां|एभिर्गुणैः प्रपूर्णो]] यः स एव [[भगवाद हरित,|भगवान् हरिः ।;]] शङ्कराचार्य in भाष्य on ब्रह्मसूत्र II. [[2.44|2. 44]] remarks about the [[न्यूह|व्यूहs]] [['ईश्वरा|‘ईश्वरा]] एवैते सर्वे [[ज्ञानेश्वर्यशक्तिबलवीर्यतेजोभिरैम्वधर्मरेन्विता अग्युपगम्यन्ते'.|ज्ञानैश्वर्यशक्तिबलवीर्यतेजोभिरैश्वरैर्धर्मैरन्विता अभ्युपगम्यन्ते'.]] [[शहराचार्य|शङ्कराचार्य]] probably follows the [[facuyco|विष्णुपुराण]] VI 5. 78-79.

[^1566]: [[उत्पत्ति|उत्पत्तिं]] [[मलय|प्रलयं]] चैव [[भूतानामागति|भूतानामागतिं]] गतिम् । [[पति|वेत्ति]] [[विद्यामवियां|विद्यामविद्यां]] च [[समाग्यो भगवा नितिज्ञानशक्तिबलेश्वर्यवीर्यतेजास्पशेषतः।|स वाच्यो भगवानिति ॥ ज्ञानशक्तिबलैश्वर्यवीर्यतेजांस्यशेषतः ।]] [[भगवचन्दवास्यानि विना येणादिभिःविष्यपु.|भगवच्छब्दवाच्यानि विना हेयैर्गुणादिभिः ॥ विष्णुपु०]] VI. [[5.78-79;|5. 78-79;]] ब्रह्मपु० [[233.66-68.|233. 66-68.]]

[^1568]: [[अयाशुलवण्डाजिनाम्यां उकठो।|अयःशूलदण्डाजिनाभ्यां ठक्ठञौ ।]] पा. [[V.2.76;|V. 2. 76;]] [[महाभाज्य 'कि योऽयाले मान्विच्छति स आया शूलिकः । किंचातः । शिवभागवते मामोति । एवं सत्तरपवलोपोऽत्र वन्यः। अपामूलमिवायःशूलम् । यो सानोपायेनान्वेधण्यानाद रभसेनाविच्छति स उपयवे प्राय: शलिकः।|महाभाष्य ‘किं योऽयःशूलेनान्विच्छति स आयःशूलिकः । किं चातः । शिवभागवते प्राप्नोति । एवं तर्ह्युत्तरपदलोपोऽत्र द्रष्टव्यः । अयःशूलमिवायःशूलम् । यो मृदुनोपायेनान्विष्टव्यानर्थान् रभसेनान्विच्छति स उच्यते आयःशूलिकः ।’.]] शिवभागवत is to be explained as शिवो भगवान् भक्तिरस्य [[शिवमामवतः|शिवभागवतः]] [[... &|i. e. a]] devotee that carried with him a trident, which is a [[wonpon|weapon]] of Śiva.

[^1569]: Vide E. I. XVI pp. 25-27 and I A. vol. 61 pp. 203-205 for the [[Ghosūndi|Ghosūṇḍī]] Stone Inscription, where [[Saṅkarsana|Saṅkarṣaṇa]] and [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] are both styled [[\* Bbagavat'|'Bhagavat']] and [['Sarvesvara'.|'Sarveśvara'.]]

[^1570]: [[सापादिपञ्चसंस्कारी नवेज्याकर्मकारकःअर्थपञ्चकषिद् विमो|तापादिपञ्चसंस्कारी नवेज्याकर्मकारकः । अर्थपञ्चकविद् विप्रो]] महाभागवतः [[स्मृतः।|स्मृतः ।]] [[Trh VI280.|पद्म VI. 280.]] 27. For [[ang.|तापादि,]] vide [[sote|note]] 1546 above. The [[oine|nine]] modes of worship are [[freef|स्मरणं]] &c. quoted in note 1571. The five heads under which [[tho|the]] doctrines of the [[the|रामानुज]] school are discussed are: (1) [[itd,|जीव,]] (2) [[TT,|ईश्वर,]] (3) [[34r|उपाय]] (way to God), (4) [[Foy or gourd|फल or पुरुषार्थ]] (the goals of human life), (5) [[fatia:|विरोधिनः]] (obstructions or obstacles to the attainment of God). There is a work called [[i|अर्थपञ्चक]] by [[otru,|नारायण,]] in which each of the [[abova|above]] five heads is shown to have five sub-divisions Vide Dr. R. [[G,|G.]] Bhandarkar's paper in [[Proccadings|Proceedings]] of the International Congress of Orientalists held at Vienna in 1886, [[Afyan|Aryan]] section, pp. 101-110 for a summary of the work called [[spam.|अर्थपञ्चक.]]

Three mārgas ( paths) are spoken of in ancient works viz. karmamārga, bhaktimārga and jñānamārga. It is necessary to say here something about the path of [[bhakli|bhakti]] and that of [[jñana.|jñāna.]] Both these paths are deemed to lead to the same goal, [[viz|viz.]] mokṣa. The mode of approach in the two is, however, [[different,|different.]] In the path of knowledge (or [[ayaktajāsanā)|avyaktopāsanā)]] it is not bare book knowledge of brahman as the Supreme Soul and as [[nirguna|nirguṇa]] that will lead to mokṣa; for that purpose [[wbat|what]] is required is the [[brāhmi-sthiti|brāhmī-sthiti]] (state of identifying oneself with brahma) [[men. tioned|mentioned]] in [[Gitā|Gītā]] II. 72. This condition can be secured only by great efforts and long practice as described in [[Gitā|Gītā]] II. 55 and the following verses. In the path of knowledge whatever actions the person concerned may do are [[brahmārpana,|brahmārpaṇa,]] as described in [[Gita|Gītā]] IV. 18–24. In the path of bhakti the bhakta resigns [[him self|himself]] to God's grace and whatever [[be|he]] does [[Le|he]] [[consigos|consigns]] to the God worshipped by him such as [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] ([[saguna|saguṇa]] and [[vyakla).|vyakta).]] Arjuna asks the Lord a question in [[Gitā XII, 1|Gītā XII. 1]] [[of|'of]] the [[worship pers|worshippers]] who thus constantly devoted meditate on you and those [[wbo|who]] (meditate ) on the [[Unparceived|Unperceived]] and Indestructible, which [[P965]] best know devotion'. The reply given in XII. 2-7 [[is those|is 'those]] who, being constantly devoted and [[possogged|possessed]] of the highest devotion, worship me with a mind fixed on me are regarded by me as the most devoted. But those who, restraining the group of the senses, and with a mind equable to all, meditate on the [[indes cribable,|indescribable,]] indestructible, [[udperceived|unperceived]] (Principle), which is [[all pervading,|all-pervading,]] inconceivable, unconcerned, immoveable, and constant, they, devoted to the good of all beings, certainly attain to me. In the case of those whose minds are attached to the unperceived ( Principle or Spirit) the trouble is much greater, since the unperceived goal is attained by embodied beings with difficulty. In the case of those, however, who dedicating all their actions to me and regarding me as their highest goal worship me, meditating on me with a devotion towards none beside me and whose minds are placed on me, I, without delay, become their deliverer from the ocean of saṁsāra and death'. In chapter 9 the path of bhakti is spoken of in these [[terms it|terms 'it]] is the chief among vidyās (lores), chief among mysteries; it is the best means of sanctification; it can be directly apprehended, it is in accordance with dharma, imperishable and easy to practise'. According to the [[Gitā,|Gītā,]] therefore, the path of bhakti is easier than the path of knowledge.

The [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] says that bhakti is ninefold[^1571] [[viz,|viz.]] hearing about Viṣṇu, repeating his name, remembering him, worshipping the feet (of the image of Viṣṇu), offering [[pūja,|pūjā,]] bowing (or homage), treating oneself as the slave of Viṣṇu, treating Him as a friend and surrendering one's soul to Him. According[^1572] to [[Narada-bhakti-sūtra|Nārada-bhakti-sūtra]] it is elevenfold (as noted below). It is [[P966]] not to be supposed that all these nine methods have to be [[pra otised|practised]] at the same time. A devotee practising any one of these, [[vir,|viz.]] remembering or reciting His name, may thereby become [[&|a]] true bhakta, and may win God's favour and secure liberation [[(Sandilyasūtra|(Śāṇḍilyasūtra]] 73). The [[Gitā|Gītā]] (VII. 16-17) states 'four classes of men, who are (all) fortunate, worship me viz. one who is in distress, the seeker after knowledge, one who seeks some desired object (or seeks wealth), one who is possessed of knowledge; of these he who is possessed of knowledge, who is always devoted and who worships One (Being) only is distinguished (above others ), for to the man of knowledge I am extremely dear and he is dear to me'. [[Sāedilya|Śāṇḍilya]] provides that the four aspects (out of nine) viz. remembering God, reciting His name, [[Darrating|narrating]] stories about Him and bowing to Him (His [[imago),|image),]] find their place in the bhakti of those who are distressed, for these are said to be the means of expiation of sins, as the Viṣṇupurāṇa says (II. 6. 39) that remembering [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] is superior to all [[praya ścittas.|prāyaścittas.]] [[Sandilya|Śāṇḍilya]] further says that those guilty of mortal (grave) sins are only entitled to the [[bbakti|bhakti]] of the distressed; but when their sins have been wiped off they would be entitled to the other forms of bhakti.

[[महाद|प्रह्लाद]] says this to his father. [[स्मतिकीयों कयादेबालों मायश्चित्तभावात् । शाण्डिल्य 74: स्मरणकीर्तनकथानमस्कारादीनामार्तभक्ती निवेशः । खमेश्वर per afegint ant VEFT 82; THE EA14 talent a gatheretter: sepat पर वत्पापक्षयस्य सापेक्षयाम्यहितत्वात् । तदपगमे तु सुतरामधिकारसिबि.|स्मृतिकीर्त्योः कथादेश्चार्तौ प्रायश्चित्तभावात् । शाण्डिल्य 74; स्मरणकीर्तनकथानमस्कारादीनामार्तभक्तौ निवेशः । स्वप्नेश्वरः महापातकिनां त्वार्तौ । शाण्डिल्य 82; पतनहेतुपापरतानां च पुनरार्तिभक्तावेवाधिकारः प्रायश्चित्तवत् तत्पापक्षयस्य सर्वापेक्षाभ्यर्हितत्वात् । … तदपगमे तु सुतरामधिकारसिद्धिः ।]] The [[भक्ति (part of thala)|भक्तिप्रकाश (part of वीरमित्रोदय)]] in pp. 30–128 explains these [[aine|nine]] at great length. Even the [[amouth|तान्त्रिक]] writers took over these [[aine|nine]] modes of bhakti e. g. the [[\* (27. 103-104) provides Hotel de turi PRTOP prehrane i spelet निवेदनम् । एतजक्तिमसादेन|रुद्रयामल (27. 103-104) provides ‘मननं कीर्तनं ध्यानं स्मरणं पादसेवनम् । अर्चनं … निवेदनम् ॥ एतद्भक्तिप्रसादेन]] जीवन्मुक्तस्तु [[साधकः।.|साधकः ।’.]]

[^1572]: [[गुणमाहाल्यासक्ति रूपासक्ति पूजासक्ति-स्मरणासकि वास्याससि-सवमासाकि -पासल्यासक्ति-कान्तासक्ति आत्मनिवेदनासक्ति-सन्मयतासक्ति-परमविरक्षासक्तिरूपा एकपा wa CHTETUT Ya I MAT|गुणमाहात्म्यासक्ति-रूपासक्ति-पूजासक्ति-स्मरणासक्ति-दास्यासक्ति-सख्यासक्ति-वात्सल्यासक्ति-कान्तासक्ति-आत्मनिवेदनासक्ति-तन्मयतासक्ति-परमविरहासक्तिरूपा एकधा अपि एकादशधा भवति । नारदभक्तिसूत्र]] 83. The [[rutiara|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति]] 81-83 gives [[allghtly|slightly]] differing nine aspects of bhakti.

[[Gitā|Gītā]] does not expressly [[enumerato|enumerate]] all [[thega|these]] nine modes of bhakti, but most of them can be gathered from various passages of the [[Gitā|Gītā]] such as IX. 14, 26, 27 and from passages in other Purāṇas. For example, the Viṣṇupurāṇa[^1573] says 'whatever be the expiatory rites, consisting of austerities, deeds of charity [[&o.,|&c.,]] to [[romembor|remember]] [[Krṣna|Kṛṣṇa]] is superior to them all' and 'that repetition of His name with bhakti is the best solvent of all sins, as fire is of metals'. In the [[Bhagavata "571|Bhāgavata]][^1574] it is said 'whatever the devotee does by his body, speech, mind, organs of sense, by his [[intelleot|intellect]] or by his soul or by the force of temperament that he [[follow|follows—]] all that he should offer to Nārāyaṇa who is highest'. This is entirely in line with [[Gitā|Gītā]] IX. 27 and may be called [[\*dāsya-bhakti';|'dāsya-bhakti';]] while Arjuna's bhakti is 'sakhya-bhakti', [[28|as]] Lord [[Krspa|Kṛṣṇa]] [[himselt|himself]] speaks of him as 'my devotee and a friend' [[(Gita|(Gītā]] [[P967]] [[IV.3).|IV. 3).]] It appears that the [[Gits|Gītā]] regards the performance[^1575] of the duties of his station in life by a bhakta as worship (arcana or [[pūja)|pūjā)]] of God 'worshipping by the performance of one's duties (without an eye to the fruit or [[towards|rewards]] of them ) God from whom the world springs and by whom all this world is permeated, a man obtains perfection (and not only by offering flowers or by reciting the name of God).

[^1575]: [[a: agirerat a pret dari Faton tarqaro ferre farver H :8 fia|यतः प्रवृत्तिर्भूतानां येन सर्वमिदं ततम् । स्वकर्मणा तमभ्यर्च्य सिद्धिं विन्दति मानवः ॥ गीता]] 18. 46.

This central doctrine of the [[Gitā|Gītā]] of what is called [['niskām karma'|'niṣkāma-karma']] was accepted by the [[Purāṇas,|Purāṇas.]] The Viṣṇu [[II, 3.25|II. 3. 25]] adopts it. The Agnipurāṇa in chapter 381 gives a summary of [[the . Gitā|the Gītā]] in 58 verses which are mostly made up of passages from the [[Gitā.|Gītā.]] It winds up the summary with the verse quoted in note 1575 and emphasizes bhakti in the last verse.[^1576] The Garuḍa-purāṇa summarises the Gītā in 28 verses (I. 237-238). The Padma (in VI. 171-188) contains the [[mahatmya|māhātmya]] of each of the 18 chapters of the [[Gitā|Gītā]] interspersed with legends about the fruit of reading each [[Gitā|Gītā]] chapter (1005 verses in all). A few further examples may be cited. The Kūrma [[saya|says]][^1577] 'therefore even [[&|a]] man not possessed of (high) wisdom should perform all his duties (or actions ) abandoning with all efforts the fruit (or [[roward)|reward)]] of actions; he obtains (high) abode after some time'; [[\*actions|'actions]] done after offering them to me do not tend to bind down the [[door|doer]] but tend to mukti [[(liberation).|(liberation)'.]] The [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] also refers to [['niskāma-karma'.|'niṣkāma-karma'.]] The [[Bhagavata-purāña|Bhāgavata-purāṇa]] provides ‘a man performing (the rites) [[proscribed|prescribed]] by the Veda but [[with out|without]] attachment (not caring for the reward) and surrendering them to God [[Secures|secures]] freedom from the bondage of karma and the [[deolaration|declaration]] of the [[roward|reward]] (in the Veda) is only meant for stimulating effort.'

[^1577]: तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन [[त्यक्ता कर्माभितं फलम्। अविहानपि कुर्भात कर्मामोति चि- । PER|त्यक्त्वा कर्माश्रितं फलम् । अविद्वानपि कुर्वीत कर्माप्नोति चिरात्पदम् ॥ कूर्म]] I. 3. 21; [[' Azultatea altor agrera fo i l \*|‘मय्यर्पितानि कर्माणि न बन्धाय विमुक्तये ।’ कूर्म]] II. 7. 28; [[बग्धाय तत्कर्म भवत्पनभिसन्धितम् । मार्क०93,13:दोक्कमेव कुर्वाणो नि:सहलेपितमीश्वरे। Hopeina forte auf exa: \# &|न च बन्धाय तत्कर्म भवत्यनभिसन्धितम् । मार्क० 92. 15; ‘वेदोक्तमेव कुर्वाणो निःसङ्गोऽर्पितमीश्वरे । नैष्कर्म्याल्लभते सिद्धिं रोचनार्था फलश्रुतिः ॥ भागवत]] XI. [[3,|3.]] 46.

The doctrine of [[adraila|advaita]] in the Upaniṣads (such as in [[Isa|Īśā]] 16, Tai. Up. III. 4 and 8, Bṛ. Up. II. 4. 14, IV. 3. 30-31, IV. 5. 15) was for the wise. They offer very little to the ordinary man [[P968]] about God or the ultimate destiny of man or the way to God and did not solve the common man's problems. The [[Gita|Gītā]] took up the problems of the common man; it shows to the lowliest of the lowly that there is hope for him, that the One and the True Being can enter into his life, if he consecrates all the daily duties and [[Botions|actions]] of his station in life to God and that salvation will come to every one if one has loving faith in God and surrenders [[him. self|himself]] entirely to his Grace. The [[Gita|Gītā]] proclaims (IX. 30-32) 'If even a man that has been very badly conducted worships me without worshipping any one else he should be regarded as a good man, since he has resolved upon the right course; he quickly becomes a righteous soul and reaches everlasting peace: O Arjuna! you may affirm that my devotee is never ruined. By taking refuge with me even those that are born in despised castes, also women, vaiśyas and [[sūdras|śūdras]] reach the highest goal'. The [[Sandilya-bhakti-sūtra|Śāṇḍilya-bhakti-sūtra]] provides[^1578] that all down to persons of the despised [[castos|castes]] are entitled (to follow the path of bhakti) as they are capable of learning bhakti at second hand just [[ag|as]] they can learn the common rules (of [[ahimsā,|ahiṁsā,]] truthfulness [[&o.).|&c.).]] The [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] speak in the same strain as the [[Gita|Gītā]] and are [[some times|sometimes]] even more explicit and emphatic. The Brahma-purāṇa paraphrases [[Gita IX, 32|Gītā IX. 32]] and proceeds 'my devotee even if he be [[& candāla|a cāṇḍāla]] attains the desired beatitude, if he be endowed with the right faith; what need be said about others?' The Padma[^1579] [[states A|states 'A]] Pulkasa or even a [[svāpaka|śvāpaka]] and other persons [[belong. ing|belonging]] to Mleccha tribes become eminent and worthy of being honoured, if they are solely devoted to the worship of the feet of Hari'; [[' Even|'Even]] a śvapāka is a Vaiṣṇava if on his lips there is the name of Hari, in whose heart there is [[Visgu|Viṣṇu]] and in whose stomach goes food offered to Viṣṇu'. The [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] [[containgist|contains]][^1580] [[P969]] the following remarkable [[statement: Homage|statement: 'Homage]] to that Supreme Lord, by resorting to whom as refuge Kirātas[^1581] [[(mountain dwellers|(mountain-dwellers]] like Bhils), the Hūṇas, Andhras, Pulindas, Pulkasas, [[Abhiras,|Ābhīras,]] [[Kankas,|Kaṅkas,]] Yavanas, Khasas and the like and other degraded men are purified.' These sentiments were not mere platitudes, but had been very largely acted upon. Even in the medieval ages in India we have women saints like [[Mirabai|Mirābai]] and [[Āṇdāļ|Āṇḍāḷ]] (in South India), untouchables as saints like Nanda, [[Cokhāmeļā|Cokhāmeḷā]] (in [[Mahārāṣtra),|Mahārāṣṭra),]] Rai Das (a chāmār disciple of Rāmānanda), sinners like Ajāmila honoured as [[saints, Saints|saints. Saints]] like Kabir[^1582] (a Moslem weaver) and [[Tukārām.had|Tukārām had]] probably not much book learning, but their hymns are recited by Hindus including orthodox brāhmaṇas.

[^1580]: किरातहूणान्ध्रपुलिन्दपुल्कसा [[आभीरकका|आभीरकङ्का]] यवनाः [[खसादयः।|खसादयः ।]] येऽन्ये च पापा [[q urur: geura ulature A: \# Ta|यदुपाश्रयाश्रयाः शुष्यन्ति तस्मै प्रभविष्णवे नमः ॥ भागवत]] II. 4. 18; [[mag|विश्वामित्र]] [[pronounc ed|pronounced]] a curse on his 50 elder sons, who did not consent to accepting [[Sunahsepa|Śunaḥśepa]] adopted as a son by [[Viāvāmitra|Viśvāmitra]] as their eldest brother, that their progeny would be Andhras, be reduced to the status of the lowest class and they would be [[Sabaras|Śabaras]] &c. and mostly dasyus as [[follows:|follows :]] [['amagerie AT: 31 martea 1 त पतेमाः पुण्डाः शपरा पुलिन्दा मूतिबा इत्युवस्या बहवो भवन्ति वैश्वामित्रा बना out. 7. VII, 18|‘ताननुव्याजहारान्त्याः प्रजा भक्षीष्टेति । त एतेऽन्ध्राः पुण्ड्राः शबराः पुलिन्दा मूतिबा इत्युदन्त्या बहवो भवन्ति वैश्वामित्रा दस्यूनां भूयिष्ठाः । ऐ. ब्रा. VII. 18]] (chap. 33. 6). In [[E, I.|E. I.]] VIII p. 88 there is an [[Ins cription|Inscription]] of the 9th year of king [[lóvarasena,|Īśvarasena,]] [[ad Abhira,|an Ābhīra,]] son of [[sivadatta,|Śivadatta,]] an [[abhira,|ābhīra,]] in the Nasik caves [[(No. 1) plato|(No. 15 plate]] VII).

[^1581]: [[Palkasas|Pulkasas]] and [[Svapākas|Śvapākas]] were degraded and untouchable [[castes,|castes.]] Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. II pp. 88-89 for Pulkasa and p. 97 for [[Svapāka|Śvapāka]] (lit. who subsists on [[dog-ment).|dog-meat).]] Pulkasa occurs in Vāj. S. 30. 17. Kirātas are assigned to caves in [[vaj.|Vāj.]] S. 30. 16. The [[Abbīras|Ābhīras]] are described as dasyus and mlecchas in Mausala-parva 7. 46-63, they are said to have attacked Arjuna in Pañcanada when he was taking women with him after [[Krona's|Kṛṣṇa's]] passing away and to have carried away [[Vrani|Vṛṣṇi]] women (Mausala 8. 16-17). Vide also the same account in [[Vinnupurāṇa V,|Viṣṇupurāṇa V.]] 38. 12-28, Matsya [[273, 18|273. 18]] speaks of ten [[Abhira|Ābhīra]] kings. The Khasa tribe had the custom of a brother [[war- . rying|marrying]] his deceased brother's widow. Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. III. p. 861 [[A.|n.]] 1671.

[^1582]: Vide G. [[H,|H.]] Westcott on 'Kabir and [[Kabir-panth|Kabir-panth']] [[(Cawapore,|(Cawnpore,]] 1907) and 'Kabir and his followers' by F. E. Keay (1931). Kabir's [[teach ing|teaching]] was an amalgam of both Hindu and Moslem ideas. He [[preached, ā|preached a]] doctrine of theism that did not tolerate polytheism, incarnations and idols. Kabir is said to have been a disciple of [[Rāmāpanda|Rāmānanda]] (who lived about 1400-1470 A. D.), an ascetic who preached at Benares that God should be worshiped under the name of Rāma. Kabir [[beld|held]] to the doctrines of Karma and Transmigration. He believed in the unity of God, but makes use of many names such as Rāma, Khudā, Allah, [[Sakti lo|Śakti in]] speaking of [[Him. :!:|Him.]]

The invasions and gradual conquest of India by Moslem [[in vaders|invaders]] from the Northwest [[throw|threw]] down a challenge to Hindus from 11th century A.D. onwards. It was met in various ways. The first was the composition of comprehensive digests of [[amstis|smṛtis]] of which the earliest extant one is the [[Kștyakalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] of [[Lakṣmidhara|Lakṣmīdhara]] (about 1110-1130 A.D.) in the North and Hemādri in the [[Docoan|Deccan]] (third quarter of 13th century). The second and the most important way was on the spiritual front. From the 13th to the 17th century there was a great revival of spirituality on an unprecedented scale which produced saints and [[mystios|mystics]] in all parts of India such as [[Jõāneśvara,|Jñāneśvara,]] Nāmadeva, Rāmānanda, Kabir,[^1582] Caitanya, [[P970]] [[Dadu|Dādu]] (in Rajasthan), [[Nanak,|Nānak,]] [[Vallabhacārya,|Vallabhācārya,]] Ekanātha, Tukārām, Rāmadāsa ( and many others of lesser [[famo)|fame)]] who all agreed on fundamentals viz. unity of God, the need of self-purification, [[com demnation|condemnation]] of the pride of caste and of formalities of worship, and [[Surrender|surrender]] to the Deity for salvation. The third was the creation of such independent kingdoms as that of Vijayanagara [[(1330|(1330–]] 1565 A. D.), of [[Mahārāstra|Mahārāṣṭra]] (under Shivaji and the Peshwas) and of the Sikhs in Punjab. This last cannot be dealt with way in this work. The doctrine of bhakti had a great appeal to all sections of the Hindu community and its propagation by the Purāṇas went a long [[away|way]] in weaning away Hindus from Buddhism. Not only so, Mahāyāna Buddhism took over the doctrine of bhakti and works like the [[Questions|'Questions]] of Milinda' and the [[Saddharmapuṇdarika|Saddharmapuṇḍarīka]][^1583] contain passages which agree closely with the [[Gita.|Gītā.]] The [[Gitā|Gītā]] exhibits a wonderful spirit of tolerance and accommodation, not found in the scriptures of other religions which were founded by great prophets. It says[^1584] [[even|'even]] those, who are devotees of other deities and worship them with faith, ( indirectly) worship me only but with a [[pon-śāstric|non-śāstric]] (or irregular ) procedure.' The Bhāgavata-purāṇa elaborates the same [[idea; O|idea; 'O]] Adorable One! others worship you alone in the form of Śiva while following the path promulgated by Śiva and propounded in different ways by several [[acūryas,|ācāryas,]] all worship you who are Lord and who comprehend in yourself all gods; those also who are devotees of other deities and appear to have different ideas do in the end reach you, just as all rivers rising in mountains and flooded by rains enter the ocean from all sides'. The [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]][^1585] also contains a similar [[idea Those|idea 'Those]] who worship [[Brahma,|Brahmā,]] Śiva or other deities and whose conduct (or practice ) is intelligent will (ultimately ) come to me, who am the Highest'. This doctrine has [[ita|its]] [[gorms|germs]] in the [[Roveda|Ṛgveda]][^1586] [[P971]] itself where it is [[said the|said 'the]] One Reality the [[Bages|sages]] speak of under [[Various|various]] names; they call it Agni, Yama, [[Matari van|Mātariśvan]] (wind-god )'. This work cannot afford, for reasons of space, to go into the several bhakti schools such as those of Rāmānuja, [[Madhya|Madhva]] (who postulated five eternal bhedas), Caitanya and Vallabha ( that gave rise to an erotico-mystical brand of bhakti ) and others.

[^1583]: Vide H. [[Kera's|Kern's]] Introduction to the translation of the [[Saddharma puṇdarika|Saddharmapuṇḍarīka]] in SBE, vol. [[21.|21,]] pp. [[XXVI-XXVIII|XXVI–XXVIII]] and XXIX [[a. 2.|n. 2,]] XXXI [[a.|n.]] 1, XXXI [[1.|n.]] 2.

[[23:खामेवान्ये|23; त्वामेवान्ये]] शिवोक्तेन मार्गेण [[शिवरूपिणम्।|शिवरूपिणम् ।]] [[बलाचार्यविभेदेन भगवन्स. मुपासते।|बह्वाचार्यविभेदेन भगवन्समुपासते ॥]] सर्व एव यजन्ति त्वां सर्वदेवमयेश्वरम् । येऽप्यन्यदेवताभक्ता यद्यप्यन्यधियः [[प्रभो। यथाद्रिमभवा नयः|प्रभो ॥ यथाद्रिप्रभवा नद्यः]] पर्जन्यापूरिताः [[प्रभो।|प्रभो ।]] विशन्ति सर्वतः सिन्धुं [[तवया गतयोऽन्ततः|तद्वत्त्वां गतयोऽन्ततः ॥]] भागवत X. 40. 8-10.
