[^1586]: [[HEA TUTTaraft or ArafaTTATS: \# \*. I. 164, 46,|एकं सद्विप्रा बहुधा वदन्त्यग्निं यमं मातरिश्वानमाहुः ॥ ऋ. I. 164. 46.]]

In their [[zoal|zeal]] for the spread of the [[oult|cult]] of bhakti the Purāṇas are sometimes guilty of gross exaggeration. The [[Brahma purāṇa|Brahmapurāṇa]][^1587] says [['mon|'men]] even after having committed many sins under the influence of error (or delusion ), do not go to Hell, if they worship Hari who removes all [[sing;|sins;]] those men also who always remember Janārdana, though they may be guilty of roguery, reach, after they die, the happy world of Viṣṇu. Even a man who is habituated to flying into extreme rage, if he recites the name of Hari, has his faults destroyed and attains mukti (liberation ) as the king of Cedi country did'. The [[Vāmana purāṇa|Vāmanapurāṇa]][^1588] observes “what has that person got to do with many mantras [[(i, e,|(i. e.]] he has no use for those), who is a bhakta of Viṣṇu ? The mantra 'namo Nārāyaṇāya' is able to accomplish all objects. Success is for those who have bhakti for Viṣṇu; how can there be failure for them in whose heart is enthroned Janārdana dark like a blue lotus ?" The [[Vamana|Vāmana]] and Padma Purāṇas say that a man secures the same results by repeating the names of [[Viṣğu|Viṣṇu]] that he would secure by visiting all the [[tirthas|tīrthas]] ( sacred places) and holy [[shrinos|shrines]][^1589] in the world.

[^1587]: [[Fatah: TP ATT HNEFAPat: 18 Tata T atuet Pau|कृत्वापि बहुशः पापं नरा मोहसमन्विताः । न यान्ति नरकं नत्वा सर्वपापहरं हरिम् ॥]] [[शाम्बेनापि|शाठ्येनापि]] नरा नित्यं ये स्मरन्ति जनार्दनम् । तेपि यान्ति [[तर्नु|तनुं]] त्यक्त्वा [[विष्णलोकमनामयम् ।|विष्णुलोकमनामयम् ॥]] [[अत्यन्तकोसक्तोऽपि|अत्यन्तक्रोधसक्तोऽपि]] कदाचित्कीर्तयेद्धरिम् । सोपि [[दोषक्षयान्मुक्ति लभेच्छेविपतियेचा बा 216.87-89.|दोषक्षयान्मुक्तिं लभेच्चेदिपतिर्यथा ॥ ब्रह्म 216. 87-89.]] चेदिपति appears to refer to शिशुपाल, whose story occurs in सभापर्व chapters 43-45. He was the sister's son of [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] who had promised to [[forgivo|forgive]] 100 aparādhas of his and at last killed him at the [[Rājasõya|Rājasūya]] sacrifice of [[Yudhisthira.|Yudhiṣṭhira.]] The story of [[figures|शिशुपाल]] also [[occars|occurs]] in [[Rawang. IV. 15, 1-17|विष्णुपु० IV. 15. 1-17]] and it is said that [[flygre|शिशुपाल]] always took the name of [[Krapa|Kṛṣṇa]] and always thought of him though as an enemy, and therefore he ultimately reached the Lord [['nRR|‘अयं हि]] भगवान् कीर्तितश्च [[संस्थतश्च द्वेषानुवन्धेनापि अखिलसुरासुरादिलेभं|संस्मृतश्च द्वेषानुबन्धेनापि अखिलसुरासुरादिदुर्लभं]] फलं प्रयच्छति किमुत [[Fyrftri Haradifa 11 17.|सम्यग्भक्तिमतामपीति ।’ 17.]]

[^1588]: किं तस्य बहुभिर्मन्त्रैर्भक्तिर्यस्य [[जनार्दने।|जनार्दने ।]] नमो नारायणायेति मन्त्रः [[सर्वार्थ साधकः|सर्वार्थसाधकः]] ॥ [[विष्णुपेषां|विष्णोर्येषां]] जयस्तेषां कुतस्तेषां [[पराजयः।|पराजयः ।]] येषामिन्दीवरश्यामो [[बदयस्थो जनार्दनः।|हृदयस्थो जनार्दनः ॥]] [[THAT|वामन]] 94. 58–59. For the first verse, vide also [[FEHET 63, 6.|नरसिंहपु० 63. 6.]]

[^1589]: पृथिव्यां यानि तीर्थानि [[पुण्याग्यायतनानि|पुण्यान्यायतनानि]] [[च।|च ।]] तानि [[साण्यवानोति विष्णोर्मामाइ ATT TA|सर्वाण्यवाप्नोति विष्णोर्नामानुकीर्तनात् ॥ वामन]] 94. [[63,VI,|63, पद्म VI.]] 245. 85.

Many Purāṇas, particularly the Viṣṇu and the [[Bhāgavata, ·|Bhāgavata,]] are replete with the eulogies, the theory and practice of bhakti [[P972]] and illustrative stories about it. For reasons of space it is not possible to go into this matter at any length. A [[fow|few]] noteworthy points alone will be touched. A few [[versos|verses]] eulogising the [[Bhagavatapurāpa|Bhāgavatapurāṇa]] may be cited as samples of extreme [[exaggera tion.|exaggeration.]] [[Thousands|'Thousands]][^1590] of Aśvamedhas and hundreds of [[Vājapoya|Vājapeya]] sacrifices are not equal to even the 16th part of the story narrated by [[Suka;|Śuka;]] he who always [[recitos|recites]] a half or a quarter verse of the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] [[secure|secures]] the merit of [[Rajasūya|Rājasūya]] and Aśvamedha; he who listens to the words of the śāstra of [[Suka|Śuka]] ( i.e. [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] ) at the time of death, Govinda being pleased with him confers on him [[Vaikuntha;|Vaikuṇṭha;]] this is a good expiation for all sinners viz. uttering the name of Viṣṇu, since ( at that time) their mind has [[Viṣpu|Viṣṇu]] as the only object of thought'.[^1591] Another point is the story of Ajāmila in the Bhāgavata (VI. 1. 20 ff and VI. 2), [[Padma(|Padma (]] [[L 31.|I. 31.]] 109 and VI. [[87.7|87. 7]] ) and other Purāṇas. Ajāmila[^1592] ( who [[aban doned|abandoned]] his [[brāhmana|brāhmaṇa]] wife and kept a mistress) was a moral wreck addicted to gambling and thieving. When on his [[death bed|death-bed]] at the age of 80 he loudly called upon his youngest son named [[Nārāyana|Nārāyaṇa]] (out of ten sons born of the mistress ) with affection and recited that name and thought of that alone, he became free from his [[sing|sins]] and attained a high position difficult to attain'. Such stories have given rise to the common belief that the last thought at one's death leads to a new birth [[appro priate|appropriate]] to that thought [[(unte|(ante]] matih sā [[gatih).|gatiḥ).]] The Upaniṣads contain the germ of the idea of the last thought; [[vide,|vide]] Chān. Up. [[III, 14, 1,|III. 14. 1,]] VIII. 2. 10, [[Bļ.|Bṛ.]] Up. IV. 4. 5. In the 'Questions of Milinda' (SBE vol. 35 pp. 123–124) this idea of the importance of the last thought is taken up. It may be that a single heart-felt invocation of the name of God, a single act of faith after [[P973]] repentance and complete surrender to God's will, cancels the result of a whole life of crime and sin. This is the moral of Ajāmila's [[atory,|story,]] but it is liable to be grossly misapprehended and might create a complacent belief that a man may commit as many sins in his life as possible, but if he remembers God at his death and repeats His name then, all sins of his are cleared away and wiped out. This is a rather dangerous doctrine. The [[Gita|Gītā]] ( VIII. 5-7) puts the matter in a clear light. [["That|'That]] person, who remembers me at the time of death and passes from the world leaving the body, attains my essence, there is no doubt about that. Whatever form a man remembers when he leaves the body, to that he goes, because he was always engrossed in that form. Therefore [[remem ber|remember]] me at all times and engage in battle; there is no doubt that having fixed your mind and intellect on me you will attain to me'. This passage of the [[Gṛtā|Gītā]] suggests that a person will remember the name of God at the time of his death only if he had been [[80|so]] doing all his life, while engaged in actively performing all his duties and not caring for the rewards thereof and that it is extremely rare or almost impossible for a man to remember God at the last moment when throughout his life he had been ungodly and a great sinner. The above idea is again emphasized in verses 10-13 of chap. VIII (of the [[Gitā)|Gītā)]] and in chap. [[XIII, 3|XVII. 3]] (yo yac-chraddhaḥ sa [[ova|eva]] saḥ).

[^1590]: [[ATHERIO 915Touragi yangila y wist niepoet andere भीम् ॥ श्लोका श्लोकपादं वा पठेवागवतं च यः। नित्यं पुण्यमवामोति राजस्याश्वमेधयोः । अन्तकाले तु येनेष भूयते शुकशासवान। प्रीत्या तस्यैव चैकुण्ठं गोविन्दोपि भयछति पर|अश्वमेधसहस्राणि वाजपेयशतानि च । शुकशास्त्रकथायाश्च कलां नार्हन्ति षोडशीम् ॥ श्लोकार्धं श्लोकपादं वा पठेद्भागवतं च यः । नित्यं पुण्यमवाप्नोति राजसूयाश्वमेधयोः ॥ अन्तकाले तु येनैव श्रूयते शुकशास्त्रवाक् । प्रीत्या तस्यैव वैकुण्ठं गोविन्दोपि प्रयच्छति ॥ पद्म]] VI. [[191, 32, 39, 41,|191. 32, 39, 41.]]

[^1591]: The [[Padmapurāṇa|Padma-purāṇa]] ([[VI,|VI.]] [[189-194,|189–194,]] 518 verses) [[contaios|contains]] a lengthy eulogy of the [[Bhagavata-purana|Bhāgavata-purāṇa]] and of listening to its recitation for seven days [[( saptaha )|(saptāha)]] &c.

[^1592]: कान्यकुब्जे [[द्विजःकश्चिद् दासीपतिरजामिल। नाम्नानष्टसदाचारोधारयाः संसर्ग Para: 1 camaathreat fra Sta: 1 aparata|द्विजः कश्चिद् दासीपतिरजामिलः । नाम्ना नष्टसदाचारो दास्याः संसर्गदूषितः ॥ बन्द्यक्षकैतवैश्चौर्यैर्गर्हितां वृत्तिमाश्रितः । भागवत]] VI. 1. 21-22; [[ferrament Stafa Torggatan 35AMCAT Te gati HUT JUFT II 7148|म्रियमाणो हरेर्नाम गृणन् पुत्रोपचारितम् । अजामिलोप्यगाद्धाम किं पुनः श्रद्धया गृणन् ॥ भागवत]] VI. 2. 49; [[सर्वेषामप्यपवतामिदमेव सुनिष्कृतम् । नामव्याहरणं विष्णोर्यतस्तद्विषया मतिः। भागवत|सर्वेषामप्यघवतामिदमेव सुनिष्कृतम् । नामव्याहरणं विष्णोर्यतस्तद्विषया मतिः ॥ भागवत]] VI. 2. [[103; नियमाणः सुतस्नेहारमोच्य नारायणेति च। तध्याननामग्रहणास्पदं लेभे सुदुर्लभम् । पत्र|10; म्रियमाणः सुतस्नेहात्प्रोच्य नारायणेति च । तद्ध्याननामग्रहणात्पदं लेभे सुदुर्लभम् ॥ पद्म]] IV. 87. 7; [[fata granruofamina arta Frator rare grann \#ru I. 31, 109.|विक्रुश्य पुत्रमघवान्यदजामिलोपि नारायणेति म्रियमाण इयाय मुक्तिम् ॥ पद्म I. 31. 109.]]

In spite of the doctrine that God is one[^1593] and the doctrine that whatever form of the deity one may worship, the worship reaches the Supreme Being, Vaiṣṇavas and Śaivas have been wrangling and abusing one another. For example, the [[P974]] [[Varāha purana|Varāhapurāṇa]] [[makos|makes]] Rudra declare the [[supremaoy|supremacy]] of Viṣṇu ([[obap.|chap.]][^1594] [[70.14|70. 14]] [[Narayanah|Nārāyaṇaḥ]] paro [[devah),|devaḥ),]] denounce [[Śaiva-siddhantas|Śaiva-siddhāntas]] that are outside the pale of the Vedas and put forward the outstanding view 'that such non-Vedic views were promulgated by Śiva himself at the request of Viṣṇu in order to delude [[people.|people'.]] Some Purāṇas started by saying that the Bauddhas and Jainas[^1595] were asuras and enemies of gods that were purposely deluded by god. For example, the Matsya (24. 43-49) says that the sons of Raji deprived Indra of his kingdom and share in sacrifices, that [[Brhaspati,|Bṛhaspati,]] at Indra's request, deluded the sons of Raji by composing a treatise of Jina-dharma which was opposed to the Veda and then Indra killed them. Vāyu 96. 230-32, Matsya 47. 11-12, [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] I. 3. 24 appear to suggest that Viṣṇu himself deluded the people. The Agni (16. 1-4) also says with reference to the Bauddhas that Viṣṇu deluded them. The [[Viṣṇu purana|Viṣṇupurāṇa]][^1596] (III. 17-18) narrates that when the gods were [[P975]] defeated by the asuras (who also practised tapas and studied the Veda) they approached Viṣṇu and prayed to him to help them and to destroy the asuras and that then Viṣṇu [[produoed|produced]] from his body Māyā-moha (lit. who causes delusion by wiles) and made him over to the gods. Māyāmoha, who was naked, had shaved his head and held a bunch of peacock feathers in his hand, went to the asuras that were practising tapas on the banks of the Narmadā (III. 18. 1-2) and told them that if they followed his words they would secure mukti, led them away from the path of the [[Voda,|Veda,]] taught them formulas of sceptical reasoning and weaned them away from their dharma. Then he approached other asuras and taught them that [[sacri ficing|sacrificing]] animals was sinful, taught them nirvāṇa and the doctrines of [[vijnanavāda.|vijñānavāda.]] Some of the passages are very striking 'In a short time the asuras were deluded by Māyāmoha and abandoned all concern with the path depending on the three Vedas. Some condemned the Vedas, some condemned the gods, while others condemned the body of sacrificial rites and brāhmaṇas. (They thought or said) The statement that killing an animal (in sacrifices) is desirable for the sake of Dharma (for accumulating merit) does not stand to reason; to say that offerings burnt by fire would conduce to reward (in the next world) is childish talk; ( if it be said that ) Indra attained the position of God by means of many sacrifices and enjoys the fuel-sticks of the [[sami|śamī]] tree, then an animal ( that subsists on leaves) is superior to Indra (who enjoys hard and thorny [[sami|śamī]] samidhs). If it is desired (by the Veda) that the animal killed in [[&|a]] sacrifice attains heaven, then why does not the sacrificer kill his own father in [[&|a]] sacrifice (and send him to heaven)? If one (the son) performs śrāddha (with the thought) that what is eaten by one (brāhmaṇa diner at śrāddha) tends to give satisfaction to another [[(i, e.|(i. e.]] the deceased father of the [[offeror|offerer]] of śrāddha), then travellers would not carry food ( on their [[baoka)|backs)]] which causes weariness to them.' These are the arguments used by atheists [[(oārvākas).|(cārvākas).]] It is remarkable that even tāntrik works like the Kulārṇavatantra make Śiva say that he declared certain [[sāstras|śāstras]] purely for deluding certain bad people who did not know the Kaula dharma.[^1597]

[^1593]: The proposition that God is only one, that He is worshipped under various perceptible forms for concentrating the mind on Him, worship or contemplation of a formless object being difficult, are doctrines taught in the [[Purāgas|Purāṇas]] in spite of the fact that [[tbey|they]] preach worship of [[Viṣaa,|Viṣṇu,]] [[Kroa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Śiva, [[Devi|Devī]] and others. Vide [[H, of Dh,|H. of Dh.]] vol. II pp. 714-715 and pp. 118-119 above. The [[facusema|विष्णुधर्मोत्तर]] I. 65. 32 states: [[Firehta|साकारे बद्धलक्षस्तु शून्यं]] [[शक्रोति|शक्नोति]] चिन्तितुम् । अन्यथा तु [[सुकट स्यानिरालम्बस्य|सुकष्टं स्यान्निरालम्बस्य]] चिन्तनम् [[।|॥;]] [[ride|vide]] also [[विष्णुधर्मोचर|विष्णुधर्मोत्तर]] III. [[108, 23|108. 23]] ff, particularly the verse [[Aral T AERUM FIT uta ta: Haut wala man TIE \#|आदौ कृत्वा महाभागे साकारे लक्षबन्धनम् । ततः समर्थो भवति शून्ये ध्याने नरोत्तम ॥]] 26. Indian theologians and philosophers deliberately spared lower forms of worship with the thought that it was better to begin at a lower rung of the ladder to the highest goal than not to have a footing at all. A well-known verse is : [[gut ferat a ant at Hoitforum TAATETTU Stat vifarani na R: \# RESTOT 62. 5 q. by spices|अग्नौ क्रियावतां देवो दिवि देवो मनीषिणाम् । प्रतिमास्वल्पबुद्धीनां योगिनां हृदये हरिः ॥ नृसिंहपुराण 62. 5 q. by अपरार्क]] on p. 140.

[^1594]: [[वंच|त्वं च]] रुद्र महाबाहो [[मोहशाखाणि|मोहशास्त्राणि]] कारय । अल्पायासं [[वर्शयित्वा मोहयात्रु महेश्वरः (२)|दर्शयित्वा मोहयाशु महेश्वरः (र ?) ॥]] वराह 70. 36; this verse and several more are quoted from वराह by अपरार्क p. 1052; ये वेदमार्गनिर्मुक्तास्तेषां मोहार्थमेव च । [[नयसिद्धान्तसंज्ञाभिर्मया शाकंतु वशिवम् ।|नयसिद्धान्तसञ्ज्ञाभिर्मया शास्त्रं तु दर्शितम् ॥]] वराह 70. 41; [[वं हि कद्र|त्वं हि रुद्र]] महाबाहो मोहनार्थे सुरद्विषाम् । पाखण्डाचरणं [[धर्म कुरुष्य महामते|धर्मं कुरुष्व च महामते ॥]] तामसानि पुराणानि कथयात्र च तान् [[प्रति।|प्रति ।]] मोहनानि च [[शास्राणि कुरुष्व च महामते पस|शास्त्राणि कुरुष्व च महामते ॥ पद्म]] VI. [[263. 24-25:|263. 24–25;]] तथा पाशुपतं शास्त्रं त्वमेव कुरु [[मुवत। कङ्कालशेवपापण्ड महाशैवादिभेदतः|सुव्रत । कङ्कालशैवपाषण्डमहाशैवादिभेदतः]] । अवलक्ष्य मतं [[सम्यम्वेदबा|सम्यग्वेदबाह्यं]] द्विजाधमाः । भस्मास्थिधारिणः सर्वे भविष्यन्ति न [[संशयः पम VI. 263.32-33. कङ्कालशs|संशयः ॥ पद्म VI. 263. 32–33. कङ्कालशैवs]] are the कापालिकs. Compare similar passages in [[Kurma|Kūrma]] I. 12. 261-62; [[Devibhagavata|Devībhāgavata]] VII. 39. 26-31 ([[अन्यानि पानि सामाणि लोकस्मिन् विविधानिच श्रुतिस्मृतिविरुद्धानि तामसान्येव सर्वशः। वामकापालक चैव कौलफ मैरवागमः ॥ शिवेन मोहनार्थाय प्रणीतो नान्यहेतुकः। "शेवाश्च पैम्णवाब सौराः शाकास्तथैव च । गाणपत्या आगमाश्च प्रणीता शङ्करेण तु तत्र वेदाविरुद्धोंशोप्युक्त एवं कचिद कचित् । वैदिकैस्तद्ग्रहे दोषो न भवत्येव कार्दचित्).|अन्यानि यानि शास्त्राणि लोकेऽस्मिन् विविधानि च ॥ श्रुतिस्मृतिविरुद्धानि तामसान्येव सर्वशः । वामकापालकं चैव कौलकं भैरवागमः ॥ शिवेन मोहनार्थाय प्रणीतो नान्यहेतुकः । … शैवाश्च वैष्णवाश्चैव सौराः शाक्तास्तथैव च । गाणपत्या आगमाश्च प्रणीता शङ्करेण तु ॥ तत्र वेदाविरुद्धोंशोप्युक्त एव क्वचित् क्वचित् । वैदिकैस्तद्ग्रहे दोषो न भवत्येव कर्हिचित् ॥).]]

[^1595]: [[विम्बाससामयं धर्मो धौर्य बहुवाससाम् । इल्पनेकान्तवाद च मायामोहन नेकधा तेन दर्शयता दैत्याः स्वधर्म त्याजिता द्विज विष्णुपु|दिग्वाससामयं धर्मो धर्मोयं बहुवाससाम् । इत्यनेकान्तवादं च मायामोहेन नैकधा ॥ तेन दर्शयता दैत्याः स्वधर्मं त्याजिता द्विज ॥ विष्णुपु०]] III. 18. 11-12. This is an obvious reference to the Jain doctrine of [[समभङ्गीनय.|सप्तभङ्गीनय.]]

[^1596]: स्वल्पेनैव हि कालेन मायामोहेन तेऽसुराः । [[मोहितास्तस्युञ्जः सा प्रयीमाभिता कथाम् केचिद्विनिन्दा|मोहितास्तत्यजुः सर्वां त्रयीमार्गाश्रितां कथाम् ॥ केचिद्विनिन्दां]] वेदानां देवानामपरे द्विज । यज्ञकर्मकलापस्य तथान्ये च द्विजन्मनाम् ॥ [[नेवअक्तिसई वाक्यं|नैतद्युक्तिसहं वाक्यं]] हिंसा धर्माय [[चेष्यते।|चेष्यते ।]] [[हीन्यनलदग्धानि फलायेत्यभकोदितम् । यी रनेकैवत्वमवाप्येन्द्रेण|हवींष्यनलदग्धानि फलायेत्यर्भकोदितम् । यज्ञैरनेकैर्दैवत्वमवाप्येन्द्रेण]] भुज्यते । शम्यादि यदि चेत्काष्ठं [[तहरं पत्रभुक|तद्वरं पत्रभुक्]] पशुः । निहतस्य [[पशोर्यजे स्वर्गप्रातिर्यदीप्यते । स्वपिता यजमानेन किन्नु तस्मास हण्यते । तृप्तये जायते इंसो भुक्तमायेन चेचतः कुर्यास्ट्राद्धं श्रमाया न बहेयुः प्रवासिनः । विष्णुपु.|पशोर्यज्ञे स्वर्गप्राप्तिर्यदीष्यते । स्वपिता यजमानेन किन्तु तस्मान्न हन्यते । तृप्तये जायते पुंसो भुक्तमन्येन चेत्ततः । कुर्याच्छ्राद्धं श्रमायान्नं न वहेयुः प्रवासिनः ॥ विष्णुपु०]] III. 18. 24-29. [[Videa|Vide a]] similar narrative about [[Māyāmoba|Māyāmoha]] or [[Mabāmoba|Mahāmoha]] and his being [[daked,|naked,]] shaved, carrying peacock feathers &c. in [[Ta|पद्म]] V. 3. 346-390 (this last refers to 24 [[तीर्थकरs).|तीर्थंकरs).]] In the सर्वदर्शनसंग्रह (ed. by M. M. Vasudevashastri Abhyankar, 1924) under [[चार्वाकवर्शन|चार्वाकदर्शन]] certain verses are quoted from [[वृहस्पति|बृहस्पति]] which closely resemble the above, e. g. [[पशुश्वनिहतः स्वर्ग ज्योतिधोमे गमिष्यति। स्वपिता यजमानेन तत्र कस्मात हिस्पते ॥p. 13. videप|पशुश्चेन्निहतः स्वर्गं ज्योतिष्टोमे गमिष्यति । स्वपिता यजमानेन तत्र कस्मान्न हिंस्यते ॥ p. 13. Vide पद्म]] V. 13. 370-374 for almost the same verses as in विष्णुपुराण.

[^1597]: [[MAET PER ar yra: perterri g a ra . Ia मानिनः|भ्रमिता हि मया देवि पशवः शास्त्रकोटिषु । कुलधर्मं न जानन्ति वृथा शास्त्राभिमानिनः ॥]] [[पशुशामाणि|पशुशास्त्राणि]] सर्वाणि मयैव [[कथितानिहि।|कथितानि हि ।]] मूर्त्यन्तरं तु गत्वैव मोहनाय दुरात्मनाम् [[। giulia|॥ कुलार्णवतन्त्र]] II. 96-97 (ed. by Arthur Avalon ).

From condemning the Jainas and Bauddhas some [[Puranas,|Purāṇas,]] entirely unmindful of what the Gītā declared ( in note 1584 ), [[P976]] proceeded so far as to say[^1598] that a [[brāhmapa|brāhmaṇa]] who is not a [[Valspava|Vaiṣṇava]] is a heretic, that [[Vispu|Viṣṇu]] himself assuming the form of Buddha proclaimed a false [[sastra,|śāstra,]] and that all [[sastras|śāstras]] such as the [[Pasupata,|Pāśupata,]] [[Vaibeṣika|Vaiśeṣika]] of [[Kanāda,|Kaṇāda,]] Nyāya of Gautama, [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] of Kapila, [[Carvaka|Cārvāka]] of [[Bṛbaspati|Bṛhaspati]] are [[tāmgan;|tāmasa;]] that the māyāvāda (of [[Śhaṅkara)|Śaṅkara)]] is a false [[sastra|śāstra]] and is disguised Bauddha (doctrine) and the extensive śāstra [[(Pūrvamlmā gā)|(Pūrvamīmāṁsā)]] of Jaimini is [[con demned,|condemned,]] since it made gods as of no use ( in his system ). The words of the Padma-purāṇa are ; 'Listen O Goddess, while I declare to you in order the [[tā masa|tāmasa]] śāstras, by merely remembering which even wise men become [[singers.|sinners.]] First of all I promulgated [[Saiya|Śaiva]] [[sastras|śāstras]] such as the [[Pasupata|Pāśupata]] system, then the following were declared by brāhmaṇas that were possessed (or engrossed ) by my [[sakti|śakti]] (power); viz. Kaṇāda proclaimed the great Vaiśeṣika system, the Nyāya and [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] systems were declared respectively by Gautama and Kapila : the much despised [[Carpāka|Cārvāka]] doctrine was declared by [[Bphaspati,|Bṛhaspati,]] while Viṣṇu, assuming the form of Buddha, promulgated, for bringing about the destruction of [[dailyas,|daityas,]] the false doctrine of [[Bauddbas|Bauddhas]] that go about naked or dressed in blue garments; I myself, O goddess, assuming the form of a [[brahmapa|brāhmaṇa]] declared in the Kali age the false [[gāstras|śāstras]] of the doctrine of Māyā which is bauddha in disguise. The brāhmaṇa Jaimini composed the great system of [[Pūrvamimāṁsa,|Pūrvamīmāṁsa,]] which is unmeaning on account of its atheistic discourse'. Vijñāna-bhikṣu in his [[Saṅkhya-pravacanabhāsya|Sāṅkhya-pravacanabhāṣya]] ( who flourished about 1550 A. D.) quotes eleven [[verges|verses]] from the Padma VI. 263 and holds the peculiar view that no śāstra that is [[ūstika|āstika]] (admits [[&|a]] soul ) is unauthoritative nor is there any contradiction, each śāstra being of full force and true in its own sphere. The original [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] sūtra, on which he comments, tries to establish the impossible thesis that the teachings of the [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] are not in real or irreconcilable contradiction with the doctrine of all-pervading oneness of brahma or with the doctrine that brahma is bliss (ānanda) or the system of theism (i. e. a personal God ). Sectarian exclusiveness and bigotry went [[80|so]] far that the [[Brabmanda|Brahmāṇḍa]][^1599] contains a dialogue between sage Agastya and [[P977]] [[Rama|Rāma]] in which it is said that the 108 names of [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] (who is acknowledged by all to be an incarnation of Viṣṇu ) are so potent that the merit [[securgd|secured]] by repeating thrice the 1008 names of Viṣṇu is secured by repeating only once one of the 108 names (of [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] ).[^1600]

[^1598]: [[Hacerata u fara: \# Tique: maila: I a|अवैष्णवस्तु यो विप्रः स पाषण्डः प्रकीर्तितः । पद्म]] VI. 262. 27. The [[ertaria|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति]] also requires [[tbe|the]] zealous [[Vaiṣgava|Vaiṣṇava]] not to pay homage to or worship another god nor to enter the temple of another [[godama g u जाम्पं देवं प्रपूजयेत् । नान्यप्रसाद मुशीत नाम्यदायतनं विशेत् ॥|god ‘नान्यं देवं नमस्कुर्यान्नान्यं देवं प्रपूजयेत् । नान्यप्रसादं भुञ्जीत नान्यदायतनं विशेत् ॥]] 8. 85-86.

[^1599]: शृणु देवि [[प्रवक्यामि|प्रवक्ष्यामि]] तामसानि यथाक्रमम् । येषां स्मरणमात्रेण [[पातिव्यं ज्ञानिना. मपि प्रथम हिमया चोक्तं शेष पाइपवादिकम् । मच्छवस्यावेशितेविमः मोजानिचवाराणु|पातित्यं ज्ञानिनामपि ॥ प्रथमं हि मया चोक्तं शैवं पाशुपतादिकम् । मच्छक्त्यावेशितैर्विप्रैः प्रोक्तानि च ततः शृणु ॥]] कणादेन तु [[संमोक्तं शार्य|संप्रोक्तं शास्त्रं]] वैशेषिकं महत् । गौतमेन तथा न्यायं सांख्यं तु कपिलेन [[वै ।|वै ॥]] धिषणेन तथा प्रोक्तं [[चार्वाकमतिगहितम्|चार्वाकमतिगर्हितम्]] । दैत्यानां नाशनार्थाय विष्णुना बुद्धरूपिणा । [[बौखशाखमसत्मोक्तं नग्रनीलपटादिकम् । मायावादमसरछात्रं प्रच्छन|बौद्धशास्त्रमसत्प्रोक्तं नग्ननीलपटादिकम् । मायावादमसच्छास्त्रं प्रच्छन्नं]] बौद्धमुच्यते । मयैव कथितं देवि कलौ [[बाह्मण रूपिणा । "द्विजन्मना जोमिनिना पूर्व चेदमपार्थकम् । निरीश्वरेण वादेन कुतं शान महत्तरम् । पE|ब्राह्मणरूपिणा । … द्विजन्मना जैमिनिना पूर्वं चेदमपार्थकम् । निरीश्वरेण वादेन कृतं शास्त्रं महत्तरम् ॥ पद्म]] VI. 263. 67-71 and 75-76, quoted by विज्ञानभिक्षु in सांख्यप्रवचनभाष्य pp. 6-7 (B.I. edition).

[^1600]: शृणु देवि प्रवक्ष्यामि [[नाम्नामष्टोत्तर शतम्|नाम्नामष्टोत्तरं शतम्]] । सहस्रनाम्नां पुण्यानां त्रिरावृत्या तु यत्फलम् । एकावृत्या तु कृष्णस्य [[नामक तत्मयच्छति|नामैकं तत्प्रयच्छति]] । तस्मात्पुण्यतरं चैतत् स्तोत्रं [[पातक नाशनम्|पातकनाशनम्]] ॥ ब्रह्माण्ड III. 36. 18-20. Verses 21-41 set out the 108 names of [[Krana.|Kṛṣṇa.]] The one thousand and eight names of [[Vinnu|Viṣṇu]] are set out in [[Maba bharata,|Mahābhārata,]] [[Anusasanaparva|Anuśāsanaparva]] 149. 14-120 and the Garuḍa-purāṇa (I. 15. 1-160) contains names of [[विष्ण,|विष्णु,]] but often different from those in अनुशासन.

The Viṣṇu and Padma do not stand alone in this [[strango|strange]] story of Viṣṇu himself or through Rudra teaching false doctrines for deluding the ungodly or those that dissented from or reviled the Vedas. There were other Purāṇas that sing the same tune; for example, the [[Kūrma-purāpa|Kūrma-purāṇa]] indulges in a vendetta against many śāstras and systems in several places. A few passages are set out here. [[(Devi|(Devī]] says) [[\*|']] the various[^1601] [[sastras|śāstras]] that are found in this world and are opposed to the Vedas and [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are based on tamas (ignorance), viz. [[Kapāla,|Kāpāla,]] Bhairava, [[Yamala|Yāmala]] (a class of Tantra works), Vāma (left-hand practices of a class of Tantras), [[Arbata|Ārhata]] (Jain doctrines ), these and others are meant for deluding (persons ); I produced these [[sāstras|śāstras]] in another birth for deluding people'; 'therefore in order to protect (people) against those who were beyond the pale of the Vedas and for the destruction of sinners we shall, O Śiva, compose [[sastras|śāstras]] for deluding [[then%B|them;]] Rudra being thus advised by [[Madhava|Mādhava]] (Viṣṇu) composed śāstras that delude and Viṣṇu also urged on by Rudra did the same; [[P978]] (they produced the [[gāstras)|śāstras)]] called Kāpāla, [[Nakula,|Nākula,]][^1602] [[Vama.|Vāma,]] Bhairava (early and later), Pāñcarātra, Pāśupata and thousands of others; [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] came down to the earth, wearing garlands of skull-bones and covered with ashes from a [[cometery|cemetery]] and wearing profuse matted [[pair,|hair,]] trying to delude this world and engaged in begging for the benefit of those'; [[One|'One]] should[^1603] not honour even by words [[Pancarūsra|Pāñcarātra]] and [[Pūśu pula|Pāśupata]] people that are heretics, following prohibited avocations and those who follow left-hand [[sākta|śākta]] practices; [[whon|when]] Buddhist mendicants, Nirgranthas, those who study the [[Pancarātra|Pāñcarātra]] doctrines, Kāpālikas, Pāśupatas and other similar heretics, being bad and deluded, eat the [[sraddha|śrāddha]] food (meant for a deceased person) that śrāddha would be of no use in this world and the next.' For details of some of the [[horetical|heretical]] sects, vide [[. Heretical|'Heretical]] sects in the [[Purāpas'|Purāṇas']] by [[Sri|Śrī]] Radhakrishna Choudhary in [[A BORI|ABORI]] vol 37 (1956) pp. [[231-257.|234-257.]]

[^1601]: यानि [[शाम्राणि|शास्त्राणि]] दृश्यन्ते लोकेऽस्मिन्विविधानि तु । श्रुतिस्मृतिविरुद्धानि निष्ठा [[वेषां|तेषां]] हि [[तामसी॥|तामसी ॥]] [[कापाल|कापालं]] भैरवं चैव यामलं [[धाममाईतम्|वाममार्हतम्]] । एवंविधानि चान्यानि मोहनार्थानि तानि [[तु|तु ॥]] मया [[सुष्टानि|सृष्टानि]] [[शाखाणि|शास्त्राणि]] मोहायेषां भवान्तरे ॥ [[कर्म I. 12. 261-2633;|कूर्म I. 12. 261-263;]] vide also कूर्म I. 16 17-19 and 24-26 where कापाल, [[नाकलधाम,|नाकुल, वाम,]] भैरव, पञ्चरात्र and पाशुपत are [[men. tioned|mentioned]] as produced for the same purpose. The [[ताराभक्तिसुधाणेव|ताराभक्तिसुधार्णव]] (6th [[तरक्ष)|तरङ्ग)]] [[p.272|p. 272]] quotes the [[कर्मपुराण|कूर्मपुराण]] passage and remarks that those passages are meant only for praising the Veda and are not to be understood as [[conveyiog|conveying]] that [[Tantrik|Tāntrik]] āgamas are unauthoritative.

[^1602]: Nākula stands for what is [[Lakulisa-pāśupata-darśana|Lakulīśa-pāśupata-darśana]] [[io|in]] the [[Sarvadarśapasaigraba.|Sarvadarśanasaṅgraha.]] The [[Lingaporāṇa|Liṅgapurāṇa]] (24. 124-133 ) dilates upon [[Lakuli.|Lakulī.]] The [[Vayapurāña|Vāyupurāṇa]] (chap. 23. 221-224 ) mentions in a prophetic [[voin|vein]] [[Nakuli|Nakulī]] ( [[Lakoli)|Lakulī)]] as the founder of a Śaiva sect and [[Kayārobaṇa|Kāyārohaṇa]] ( [[modero|modern]] Karavan in Dabhoi [[Taloka|Taluka]] of former Baroda State ) as its sacred place ( siddha-kṣetra). The Mathurā Ins. of Candragupta II dated in [[Gapta|Gupta]] era 61 (380 A, D.) in [[E, I.|E. I.]] vol. XXI pp. 1 ff shows that [[Lakuli,|Lakulī,]] the founder of the [[Pasopata|Pāśupata]] sect, [[floorished|flourished]] about the first century A. D. Vide Dr. R. G. [[Bbaadarkar's|Bhandarkar's]] 'Vaiṣṇavism, [[Saivism'|Śaivism']] &c. p 166 (in collected works) and [[tbe|the]] paper [[• Antiquities|'Antiquities]] in Kārvan with reference to [[Lakulisa|Lakulīśa]] worship' in Journal of Bom. [[Uni,|Uni.]] vol. 18 part 4 pp. 43-67 by Mr. M. R. Majmudar and [[Pasopata-sūtra|Pāśupata-sūtra]] of [[Nakulisa|Nakulīśa]] in T. S. S.; [[E. I,|E. I.]] vol. XXI pp. 1-9, JBBRAS vol. XXII pp. 151-167 (both by [[D.R. Bbaadarkar|D. R. Bhandarkar]] ), IHQ. vol. 19 (1943) pp. 270–271 for the origin and [[bistory|history]] of the [[Lakulisa|Lakulīśa]] sect.

[^1603]: पाखण्डिनो [[विकर्मस्थान्चामाचारांस्तथैव|विकर्मस्थान्वामाचारांस्तथैव]] [[च।|च ।]] [[पञ्चरात्रान|पञ्चरात्रान्]] पाशुपतान् [[वाघानेणापि Aran|वाङ्मात्रेणापि नार्चयेत् ॥ कूर्म]] II. 16. 15; [[greenlaturar: COTT TAR: 1 FUT: TOTT:|वृद्धश्रावकनिर्ग्रन्थाः पञ्चरात्रविदो जनाः । कापालिकाः पाशुपताः]] पाषण्डा ये च तद्विधाः ॥ यस्याश्नन्ति [[हवींध्येते|हवींष्येते]] दुरात्मानस्तु तामसाः । न तस्य [[तद्भवेच्छावं 64 9783945 UH 11.|तद्भवेच्छ्राद्धं प्रेत्य चेह फलप्रदम् ॥ कूर्म II.]] 21. 32–33. The verse [[iografi adu: 18 q. by . On Kro|बौद्धश्रावक … तद्विधाः is q. by हे. on श्राद्ध]] p. 365 from [[10|कूर्मपुराण]] and on p. [[476&.|476 हे.]] quotes both the verses from [[and reads 1997 .|कूर्म and reads वृद्धश्रावक०.]] The [[parures|निर्ग्रन्थs]] were naked Jaina monks (vide SBE. vol. 21 p. 263 and E. I. vol. 20 p. 59 ) where a [[Jaio|Jain]] teacher is called Nigrantha [[Sramaṇācārya|Śramaṇācārya]] in Gupta year 159 [[i.e.|i. e.]] (478-79 [[A, D|A. D.]] ). The words [[are :'quou-raeft-Faupu-wurtar-warraraqurteenth Salent.|are : ‘पञ्चस्तूप-निकायिक-निग्रन्थ-श्रमणाचार्य-गुहनन्दिशिष्यप्रशिष्याधिष्ठितविहारे ।’.]]

The Gītā (in chapter 16 ) speaks of two classes of people, viz. those born to godlike endowments and those born with demoniac qualities and describes the latter in verses 7-20. Some of the verses probably indicate that the reference is to atheists and the like; for verse 8 states [[they|'they]] say that the world is devoid of truth [[P979]] [[(1. o.|(i. e.]] contains nothing that one can believe ), devoid of any fixed principle (such as virtue and vice ), devoid of a Ruler, and is produced by union brought about by lust and nothing else'. After describing their thoughts and aspirations the [[Gita|Gītā]] winds up [[these|'these]] perform sacrifices which are so in name only with hypocrisy and against the prescribed procedure; they hate me in their own bodies and in those of others; these impure and cruel enemies I continually throw down in demoniac wombs; coming into demoniac wombs and being deluded, in each birth they go to vilest states without ever coming to me'. This is entirely different from what the Padma and other Purāṇas say about Pāśupatas, Pāñcarātras and about non-Vaiṣṇavas.

Both the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] and the Padma say that[^1604] (the cult of) bhakti first arose in [[Dravida|Draviḍa]] country, it progressed or [[prosperad|prospered]] in the [[Karnātaka,|Karṇāṭaka,]] it was found in only a few places in [[Maha rastra|Mahārāṣṭra]] and declined in the Gurjara country; it was, on account of the terrible Kaliyuga, broken up by heresies and remained weak for a long time; but having reached Vṛndāvana ( near Mathurā ) it got a fresh start and assumed fine form. In book XI. the Bhāgavata again reverts to this assertion that in Kali people are solely devoted to Nārāyaṇa only in a few places but to the greatest extent in the [[Dravida|Draviḍa]] country where flow the rivers [[Tāmraparpi,|Tāmraparṇī,]] [[Kștamālā,|Kṛtamālā,]] [[Kaveri|Kāverī]] and [[Mahānadi|Mahānadī]] flowing to the west and states that those who drink the waters of these rivers are generally devotees of [[Vasudeva.|Vāsudeva.]]

[^1604]: [[उत्पना|उत्पन्ना]] द्राविडे साहं [[वृद्धि|वृद्धिं]] कर्णाटके [[गता।|गता ।]] क्वचित् [[कचिन्महाराष्ट्रे|क्वचिन्महाराष्ट्रे]] गुर्जरे [[जीर्णता|जीर्णतां]] गता ॥ तत्र [[घोरकलेयोगात् पाखण्डः|घोरकलेर्योगात् पाखण्डैः]] खण्डिताङ्गका । [[दुबैलाई|दुर्बलाहं]] चिरं जाता [[पुत्राग्यां|पुत्राभ्यां]] सह मन्दताम् [[। Tratara ga: 9767 watara Jaruft i arrana|॥ वृन्दावनं पुनः प्राप्य नवीनेव सुरूपिणी । भागवत]] I. 1. 48-50, [[VI.|पद्म VI.]] 189. 54-56. The [[प:|पद्म]] VI. 189 speaks of the भागवत as the highest among [[Puranas:|Purāṇas:]] [['कलो खलु भवि. ज्यन्ति|‘कलौ खलु भविष्यन्ति]] नारायणपरायणाः ॥ [[काचित् कचिन्महाराज द्राविडेषुच|क्वचित् क्वचिन्महाराज द्राविडेषु च]] भूरिशः । ताम्रपर्णी नदी यत्र [[कत. माला पाखिनी|कृतमाला पयस्विनी]] । कावेरी च महापुण्या प्रतीची च [[महानदी।|महानदी ।]] ये पिबन्ति जलं तासां [[मजा HOSATT I ATT TT para mudaSAMTITUT: 1 TOTAQ|मनुजा मनुजेश्वर । प्रायो भक्ता भगवति वासुदेवेऽमलाशयाः ॥ भागवत]] XI 5. 38-40

Most great moral and spiritual upheavals have a tendency to reach [[&|a]] very low level in course of time. That appears to have happened in the case of bhāgavatas. The [[Atri-smrti|Atri-smṛti]] has [[&|a]] verse containing a [[sarcastio|sarcastic]] reference to bhāgavatas[^1605] : [[Those|'Those]] who are devoid of Vedic studies learn [[sāstras|śāstras]] ( such as grammar, [[Vedānta,|vedānta,]] logic [[&o.),|&c.),]] those who are wanting in [[śāstrio|śāstric]] lore become readers of Purāṇas for others; those who cannot be readers of Purāṇas become tillers; but those who are broken down even [[P980]] [[thore,|there,]] become bhāgavatas'. Atri appears to say that bhāgavatas are idlers, who do not study Veda or śāstra or who cannot even read [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] to [[othera|others]] for their livelihood or do not engage in agriculture and who pretend to be worshippers (or _bhaktas_[[ )|)]] of Viṣṇu or [[Krspa|Kṛṣṇa]] and fatten on what is given by others that are deluded into thinking that they have forsaken everything for the sake of their love of God. They become what are called [[' buwās'|'buwās']] in [[Marāthi|Marāṭhi]] and other modern Indian languages.

[^1605]: [[वैदेविहीनाश्च पठन्ति शाजे शास्त्रेण हीनाच|वेदैर्विहीनाश्च पठन्ति शास्त्रं शास्त्रेण हीनाश्च]] पुराणपाठाः । पुराणहीना [[कृषिणी aft HUTT TTECT Tafell HET|कृषिणो भवन्ति भ्रष्टास्ततो भागवता भवन्ति ॥ अत्रिसंहिता]] verse 384.


Another striking development of the bhakti cult is the Erotic Mysticism (_madhurā bhakti_) associated with the worship of [[Kṭspa|Kṛṣṇa]] and of [[Rādha|Rādhā]] in that form of Vaiṣṇavism established by Caitanya and Vallabhācārya. For the Vaiṣṇava movement inspired by Caitanya, vide Dr. S. K. [[Do's|De's]] work on [[the|'the]] Vaiṣṇava faith and movement in Bengal' (Calcutta 1942) and the author's 'History of Sanskrit Poetics' (1951) about [[Ujjvala nilamapi|Ujjvalanīlamaṇi]] of [[Rūpa-gosyamin|Rūpa-gosvāmin]] pp. 298-302. In the bhakti cult established by Vallabhācārya great importance is attached to the _guru_, who is one of the descendants of Vallabhācārya and to whom almost divine honours are paid. A wonderful [[develop mont|development]] of bhakti towards Rāma, regarded in the [[Rāmāyana|Rāmāyaṇa]] and in popular tradition as a paragon of restraint and all manly virtues, culminated in an erotic mysticism about [[Rama|Rāma]] and [[Sitā|Sītā]] also. The devotees of this mystic cult have to consider themselves as brides of Rāma or the female friends of [[Sitā,|Sītā,]] they are supposed to seek Lord Rāma's favour through [[Sita,|Sītā,]] who graciously intercedes with the Lord for the devotees. Among the followers of Vallabhācārya the guru tells the devotee to look upon [[him the|him (the]] guru) as [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] and upon himself or herself [[ag|as]] Rādhikā.

For want of space further details about these bhakti [[culte|cults]] have to be passed over.

Supreme importance is attached to Vedic mantras and also Paurāṇika mantras. They will have to be dealt with at some length in the sections on Tantras and on [[Pūrvamimāngā.|Pūrvamīmāṁsā.]] But some treatment of mantras, particularly vedic, may be given here as well. The word _mantra_ occurs about twenty-five times in the [[Rgveda.|Ṛgveda.]] Only _once_ does the word _[[mantrakt|mantrakṛt]]_ occur in the [[Rgveda 1606|Ṛgveda]][^1606] (IX. [[114, 2)|114. 2)]] 'O sage [[Kaśyapal|Kaśyapa!]] offer obeisance [[P981]] to king Soma Lord of plants with the chants of the composers (or authors) of mantras, thereby sending forth your own voice [[&o.'|&c.']] Mr. Kapali Sastry is inaccurate when he states (on p. 67 of the translation of his [['Rgbhāsya-bhūmikā|'Ṛgbhāṣya-bhūmikā]] in English) '[[wo|we]] see _frequent_ mention made in the Rik [[Samhita|Saṁhitā]] of the rishi as the author of the mantra' and he cites only [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] IX. 114. [[%|2]] and I. 67. 2. The latter contains no word like [[mantrakst.|mantrakṛt.]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 67. 2 (addressed to Agni) [[ning|runs]] 'holding in his hand all powers (or riches), sitting in a cave (i. e. concealed) he placed the gods in fright; heroes (men or [[gods )|gods)]] that place [[(offor)|(offer)]] prayers know him (Agni) to be here when they recite mantras formed in the heart.' There is no direct reference to [[psis|ṛṣis]] here (but to [[narah)|naraḥ)]] and what is emphasized is that mantras already extant as the heart's outpourings enable the reciters of the mantras to find him (Agni). There is nothing to show that the mantras referred to in the verse are meant to be _impromptu_; the idea is rather that mantras that already existed and conveyed heartfelt devotion have to be employed to find Agni. The very next verse makes the position clear when it says, 'like the unborn (Sun) [[he ( Agni)|he (Agni)]] supports the wide earth and fixes in its place the sky on account of the mantras that turn out true'. This clarifies two things, viz. that when this mantra (I. 67. 3) was recited there already existed a host of [[mantras 1607|mantras]][^1607] and secondly, that the mantras that existed long before were deemed to have helped in supporting the earth and the sky. The ancient mantras were supposed to bring Indra to attend thrice in the day for a short time to the sacrifices of devotees when invoked with mantras addressed to him ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] III. 53. 8); similarly, mantras when recited by wise [[man|men]] bring Yama to receive offerings ([[ Rg. X. 14, 4);|Ṛg. X. 14. 4);]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 88. 14 states [[we|'we]] raise our voices with mantras towards Vaiśvānara Agni, who is wise and who shines brilliantly all days'. The word [[mantra 1608|mantra]][^1608] is [[P982]] also employed in the singular several times. A few striking passages are set out here. 'Indeed, God [[Brahmanaspati|Brahmaṇaspati]] (Lord of Prayer) proclaims the mantra fit for recitation (by the mouth of the [[hotr ),|hotṛ),]] in which the gods Indra, Varuṇa, Mitra, Aryaman make their abode, O [[Gods !';|Gods!';]] 'We utter that very mantra [[( in|(in]] which Indra and other gods are praised together) in our sacrifices, the mantra being one that produces bliss and being free from all blemishes (or is [[incomparable );|incomparable);]] [[Do|'Do]] place among the Gods a mantra that is not short, that is well-arranged and that is well ornamented; ancient fetters do not reach him who is in [[( the|(the]] good grace of) Indra by the sacrifice [[( or praise );|(or praise);]] 'I address the same mantra for you (all) and offer for you [[( all)|(all)]] one offering (into fire)'. From these passages it appears that when these verses were recited there was a body of already existing mantras; which were supposed to be inspired by the Lord of prayers (or speech) and from which the choice of a long and well-formed one was to be made. Apart from the above passages the word mantra occurs in [[Rg. 1.|Ṛg. I.]] 31. 13, [[1. 74 1. 1. 147, 4,|I. 74. 1, I. 147. 4,]] I. 152. 2, II. 35. 2, [[VI,|VI.]] 50. 14, VII. [[7.6,|7. 6,]] X. 50. 4 and 6, X. 106. 11, in only one of which ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] II. [[35.2),|35. 2),]] apart from [[Rg. I,|Ṛg. I.]] [[67.2|67. 2]] already quoted, the words [['hṣda ā sutaztam mantram'(mantra|'hṛdā ā sutaṣṭaṁ mantram' (mantra]] well chiselled from the heart) [[ara|are]] used and in another verse more simply the word 'atakṣan' (in VII. [[7.6|7. 6]] _mantram_ ye [[vāram|vāraṁ]] naryā _atakṣan_) alone is used. In two passages ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. [[95.1|95. 1]] and X. [[191, 3|191. 3]] first half) the word 'mantra' appears to mean 'consultations, holding counsel together'. In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. [[20.4,|20. 4,]] [[Rbhus|Ṛbhus]] are called 'satyamantrāḥ' and are said to have made their parents young. There are controversies about what the [[Rbbus|Ṛbhus]] stand for and it is difficult to say what is meant by 'satyamantrāḥ' in relation to [[Rbhus.|Ṛbhus.]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. 76. 4. [[is a 1609|is a]][^1609] somewhat enigmatic verse. It [[means they (Aogirases)|means 'they (Aṅgirases)]] alone, (our) ancient pitṛs, learned (or wise) and following the right path, enjoyed companionship among gods and they found out the Light (the Sun) concealed (by Svarbhānu, eclipse); they, whose mantras were true, brought forth Uṣas.' In some passages where other words like _[[sloma|stoma]]_ or _brahma_ are used, there is [[P983]] mention of the stoma or brahma being made and polished by the devotee [[(0.g.|(e. g.]] X. 39. 14 'etam vām [[stomam-Aéyināvakar mātakṣāma bhrgavo|stomam-Aśvināvakar-mātakṣāma bhṛgavo]] na ratham'). Vide [[Rg. I. 62.13|Ṛg. I. 62. 13]] [[for navyam|for 'navyam]] [[ataksad|atakṣad]] brahma', [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 29.15 (Indra brahma kriyamāṇā [[juṣa81% saviṣtha|juṣasva śaviṣṭha]] yā te navyā akarma); [[brahmakstaḥ|brahmakṛtaḥ]] (composers of prayers or praises) are mentioned in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. [[32.2|32. 2]] and X. [[50.7.|50. 7.]] Other words like _gir_ (several hundred times), [[dhiti|dhīti]] (about 100 [[times);|times),]] brahma (over a hundred times) mati (about 100 times), [[maniṣā|manīṣā]] (over 60 times), vacas and vacasyā (over 100 times), stoma (about 200 times), [[sukirti|sukīrti]] (five times), sūkta (four times) are employed in the sense of [[thought,|'thought,]] word or thought out hymn or verse of praise' and in several cases the sages of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] state that it is a new verse or hymn of praise that they employ. Vide [['Davyasibhir-girbhiḥ'|'navyasībhir-gīrbhiḥ']] and 'giram bhare [[navyasim jāya mānām'|navyasīm jāyamānām']] in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 42. 13., in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VI. 49. 1, VII. [[53.2;|53. 2;]] [[Pra|'Pra]] tavyasīm _[[navyasim dhitimagnaye|navyasīm dhītimagnaye]]_' in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 143. 1; [['vaiśvānaraya|'vaiśvānarāya]] _[[matir-navyasi|matir-navyasī]]_ [[suciḥ|śuciḥ]] soma iva [[pavato'|pavate']] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VI. 8. 1) and 'iyam [[to|te]] _[[navyast|navyasī]] matir_ agne [[adhāyyasmada'|adhāyyasmadā']] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VIII. 74. 7); iyam te agne [[navyasi manīgā'|navyasī manīṣā']] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. [[4.6;|4. 6;]] [["tā valgū 1610|'tā valgū]][^1610] Dasrā [[purusa katamā|puruśākatamā]] pratnā navyasā _[[vacasā vivāso|vacasā vivāse]]_' [[( Rg.|(Ṛg.]] VI. [[62,5);|62. 5);]] '_[[Davyasi|navyasī]]_ [[sukirtiḥ'|sukīrtiḥ']] [[ip|in]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. [[60.3;|60. 3;]] 'sa pratnavat _navyase_ [[visvavāra|viśvavāra]] _sūktāya_ [[pathah krnuhi'|pathaḥ kṛṇuhi']] in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] IX. 91. 5; 'nū _[[navyase naviyase|navyase navīyase]] sūktāya_ sādhayā [[pathah'|pathaḥ']] in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] [[LX.|IX.]] 9. 8. It is a remarkable fact that when words like [['sukirtiḥ'|'sukīrtiḥ']] and 'sūkta' that occur only four or five times in the whole of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] are qualified by the word 'new or fresh', the word 'mantra' occurring so many times does not even once bear the adjective 'new'. This emphasizes the conclusion stated above that in the times of many of the extant [[Rgvedic versos|Ṛgvedic verses]] mantras were a large group already existing, from which prayers were drawn as occasion required, though now and then new verses were added to those already existing. It may here be stated that in some places the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] looks upon prayers [[( |(]]_[[dhiti|dhīti]]_) as divine and as on the same level with [[Asyins,|Aśvins,]] Uṣas, and the Sun [[( VIII. 35. 2 )|(VIII. 35. 2)]] and that ancient prayers [[wera|were]] inherited from ancestors [[(III. 39.2 seyamasme|(III. 39. 2 'seyamasme]] sanajā pitryā [[dhih').|dhīḥ').]]

[[Angirases|Aṅgirases]] are spoken of [[de Pitps|as Pitṛs]] in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 14. 6 [[(HETHI a: Tati 74791: )|(अङ्गिरसो नः पितरो नवग्वाः)]] and in [[Rg. V,|Ṛg. V.]] 40. 9 Atris are said to have found out the Sun afflicted with darkness by [[Svarbbānu,|Svarbhānu,]] an [[Asura|Āsura]] and no one else was able to do so [[(de pierreur: I अत्रयस्तमन्वाविन्दन् नान्ये|(यं वै सूर्यं स्वर्भानुस्तमसाविध्यदासुरः । अत्रयस्तमन्वविन्दन् नह्यन्ये]] अशक्नुवन् ॥). This last is a reference to a total eclipse of the Sun, on the occurrence of which the Atris probably assured frightened people that the sun would shine forth in a short time.

[^1610]: Here the contrast is great. The [[Asvins|Aśvins]] are _pratnā_ (ancient) but the [[qși|ṛṣi]] (in [[VI, 62, 5)|VI. 62. 5)]] says that he worships the ancient Dasras ([[Asvins)|Aśvins)]] with a _new prayer_. This [[rsi|ṛṣi]] at least does not convey that the [[Asvips|Aśvins]] were seen by him.

Several [[hymng|hymns]] and verses of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] are purely [[philo sophical,|philosophical,]] cosmological, mystic and speculative as I. [[164,|164.]] 4, 6, 29, [[P984]] 32, 39, 42; [[X, 71,|X. 71,]] X. 90 (Puruṣasūkta), X. 121 ([[Hiranyagarbha),|Hiraṇyagarbha),]] X. 129, [[X, 81-82|X. 81-82]] (Viśvakarman), X. 72, X. 125 (Vāk), X. 154 (state after death), X. 190 (creation).

About the meaning and purpose of Vedic mantras there are great controversies. This much may be said here that according to the [[Pūryamimāṁsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] system the whole Veda is concerned with sacrifices, that Veda is divided into two classes, Mantra and [[Brāhmana,|Brāhmaṇa,]] that Vidhis (hortatory passages) are the most important part of the Veda, that a very large number of Vedic passages are [[mero|mere]] arthavādas [[( that|(that]] contain either praises of vidhis or are to be explained metaphorically or merely repeat what already exists or are of a legendary [[character )|character)]] and that mantras only serve the purpose of bringing to the mind of the [[sacrificor|sacrificer]] or priests what is to be done in a sacrifice and that the words used in the mantras ordinarily bear the [[game|same]] meaning as that in popular Sanskrit.

Long before [[Yaska ( several venturies|Yāska (several centuries]] before Christ) there were several schools of Vedic interpretation such as the [[Aitibāsikas ( who|Aitihāsikas (who]] acc. to Nir. II. 16 said that [[Vitra|Vṛtra]] was an Asura son of [[Trastr,|Tvaṣṭṛ,]] while acc. to the Nairuktas [[Vrtra moans|Vṛtra means]] only [[\* cloud'|'cloud']] and the Veda contains metaphorical descriptions of battles, that the twins that [[Saranyu|Saraṇyu]] is said in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 17. [[%|2]] to have given up are according to the Nairuktas Indra and the Mādhyamikā-vāk, while according to the Aitihāsikas they are Yama and [[Yami,|Yamī,]] as stated in [[Nit, XII, 10),|Nir. XII. 10),]] the school of Naidānas is mentioned about 'syāla' and 'sāma' in Nir. [[VI|VI.]] 19, the school of ancient [[yājõikas|yājñikas]] in Nir. V. 11. [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 164. 32 [[(ya im cakāra )|(ya īṁ cakāra)]] is differently explained by the Parivrājakas [[( ascetic|(ascetic]] school) and the Nairuktas [[( etymologists).|(etymologists).]] The Nirukta also mentions the names of seventeen individual predecessors [[( from|(from]] whom it differs often and who differ among [[themselves )|themselves)]] such as Āgrāyaṇa, [[Audumbarayana,|Audumbarāyaṇa,]] Kautsa, Gārgya, Gālava, [[Sākatā yana,|Śākaṭāyana,]] [[Sākapūṇi.|Śākapūṇi.]] There are several mantras of which two different meanings are given in the Nirukta as in Nir. V. 11 on [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VIII. [[77.4.|77. 4.]] In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 164 there are several verses with two meanings or more e. g. verse 21 (yatrā [[suparnā|suparṇā]] &c.) has two meanings [[(aco.|(acc.]] to Nir. III. [[13 )|13)]] one relating to the devatā Sūrya (_[[adhidairata|adhidaivata]]_[[ )|)]] and the other spiritual [[( adhyātma );|(adhyātma);]] similarly, verse 32 of the same hymn has two meanings in the Nirukta (II. 8); the verse 39 [[(rco|(ṛco]] akṣare parame vyoman) is explained in four ways by Sāyaṇa; verse 41 [[( gaurir-mimāya)|(gaurīr-mimāya)]] is explained by Sāyaṇa in two ways, both being different from [[Yagka's|Yāska's]] explanation [[( in|(in]] Nir. [[P985]] [[XI, 40 );|XI. 40);]] verse 45 [[( oatvāri|(catvāri]] vāk parimitā [[padani)|padāni)]] is explained in six ways (more or less different from each [[other )|other)]] by Sāyaṇa; it is also explained in the Mahābhāṣya (p. 7 of [[M.M. Abhyankara. gastri's|M. M. Abhyankara-śāstri's]] ed.). The [[verse' catvāri|verse 'catvāri]] [[śrógā'|śṛṅgā']] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] IV. 58. 3) has been the subject of varying explanations from very early times. [[Nir, (XIII, 7)|Nir. (XIII. 7)]] explains it as referring to Yajña. The [[Mahābhāgya|Mahābhāṣya]] (p. 6 of the Mahābhāṣya _ibid._) also does the same. [[Sayapa|Sāyaṇa]] explains it as referring to Agni [[( identified|(identified]] with yajña) and Sūrya. This verse is a riddle. [[Sabara 1611|Śabara]][^1611] in his [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] on [[P.M.S.|P. M. S.]] I. 2. 38. ('abhidhāne arthavādaḥ') explains it but Kumārila differs from him. Sāyaṇa and others explain it (e. g. the word [[\* tridhā')|'tridhā')]] with reference to Mantra, Brāhmaṇa and Kalpa, but when this verse was first proclaimed there must [[bave|have]] been no Brāhmaṇas and Kalpasūtras. In several cases the Nirukta gives the adhidaivata (physical) and adhyātma (metaphysical or spiritual) or adhiyajña and adhyātma as in Nir. X. 26 (on [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. [[82.2|82. 2]] [[Viśvakarmā|'Viśvakarmā]] vimanā), Nir. XI. 4 [[( on|(on]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 85. 3, [[adhiyajõa|adhiyajña]] and [[adhidaiyata|adhidaivata]] [[meanings ),|meanings),]] Nir. XII. 37 (on Vāj. S. [[34.55|34. 55]] 'sapta [[rsayah',|ṛṣayaḥ',]] both adhidaivata and [[adhyātma ),|adhyātma),]] Nir. [[XII, 38 (On|XII. 38 (On]] Atharva X. 8. 9 [[Tiryag-bilas-oamasa &o.'|'Tiryag-bilaś-camasa &c.']] both adhidaivata and [[adhyatma).|adhyātma).]] In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] (I. 164) verses 11-13 and 48 contain a very imaginative and poetic description of the year, the seasons, months and total days and nights.

[^1611]: [[1 (on g. st. 8. 1. 2. 38 )|शबर (on पू. मी. सू. I. 2, 38)]] explains : [[' n EeT: Profanyi प्रयोऽस्य पादा इति सवनाभिप्रायम् । वे शीर्षे इति पत्नीयजमानौ । सप्तहस्तास इति छन्दां. Ferie i Frutta formatie: 1,|'चतस्रो होत्राः शृङ्गाणीवास्य । त्रयोऽस्य पादा इति सवनाभिप्रायम् । द्वे शीर्षे इति पत्नीयजमानौ । सप्तहस्तास इति च्छन्दांस्यभिप्रेत्य । त्रिधा बद्ध इति त्रिभिर्वेदैर्बद्धः ।'.]] The [[lat|तन्त्रवार्तिक]] on this says [[राक्षेति रूपकाहारेण यागस्तुतिः कर्मकाले उत्साहं करोति। "चत्वारि रहा इति विषसयामानां ग्रहणम् । प्रयोऽस्य पादा इति शीतोष्णवर्षाकाला।वेशी प्रत्ययनाभिमायम् । सप्तहस्सा इत्य separa: 1 FUTTER Ferrori.'|'चत्वारि शृङ्गेति रूपकद्वारेण यागस्तुतिः कर्मकाले उत्साहं करोति । ...'चत्वारि शृङ्गा इति दिवसयामानां ग्रहणम् । त्रयोऽस्य पादा इति शीतोष्णवर्षाकालाः । द्वे शीर्षे इत्ययनाभिप्रायम् । सप्तहस्ता इत्यश्वस्तुतिः । त्रिधा बद्ध इति सवनाभिप्रायेण ।']] It would be noticed that the [[preparar|तन्त्रवार्तिक]] differs from [[pret|शबर]] on almost every [[clause,|clause.]]

In recent years [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo in 'Hymns to the mystic Fire' (translated in the esoteric sense, [[1946 )|1946)]] and his ardent and devout disciple Sri. T. V. Kapali Sastry (in '[[Rgbhāṣya bhūmika|Ṛgbhāṣya-bhūmikā]] in Sanskrit and its English translation, Pondicherry [[1952 )|1952)]] have started a theory about the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] mantras which must be briefly stated and examined. [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo first intended to publish an edition of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] with a word by word [[Construction|construction]] and an English translation. But he gave up that idea on account of other [[pro-occupations|pre-occupations]] and rests content in the above book with the text and translation of about 230 verses in all from the 1st, 2nd and 6th _[[mandalas|maṇḍalas]]_ of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] and a foreword of 48 pages in which he propounds his theory. While [[P986]] this was being written by the present writer, [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo's large work of 634 pages [[on|'on]] the Veda' published in 1956 reached his hands. Over 60 hymns are dealt with in this big book and 283 pages are devoted to the elaboration of his theory first [[out lined|outlined]] in the brief work of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo mentioned above and reiterated with greater emphasis. On page 9 of the work of 1956 he states "the ritual system recognized by Sāyaṇa may stand, the naturalistic sense discovered by European [[scholar sbip|scholarship]] may, in its general conceptions be accepted, _but behind there is always the true and still hidden secret of the veda_ [[-|—]] the secret words which were spoken for the purified in soul and the awakened in knowledge'. In this work also he sticks to the meaning of [[yta|ṛta]] as truth and on p. 84 translates [['ptam'|'ṛtam']] as [['Truth-conscious nass',|'Truth-consciousness',]] when in his first work he translated [['fta-cit'|'ṛta-cit']] as [[Truth-con sciousness'.|Truth-consciousness'.]] Instead of comparing the several hundred passages where the word [[ṣta|ṛta]] occurs in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] he sticks to his own rendering which is unacceptable to most scholars and makes a [[Very|very]] perfunctory attempt at finding the correct meaning. One should like to have some clear examples of the distinction between Light and Consciousness in modern times and also in the Veda. So far as the present writer knows, in the language of ancient symbolism Consciousness is identified with Light. In the recently published book he deals with [[legs|less]] than [[it th|1/18th]] of the total hymns [[(1017 or 1028 )|(1017 or 1028)]] of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda.]] Readers are asked to subscribe to his views although he [[condesconds|condescends]] to translate in the first work less than [[oth|1/40th]] of the total number of verses in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] and hardly ever enters into a discussion about the meaning of words like [[fta.|ṛta.]] [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo [[( Foreword|(Foreword]] [[p. III)|p. III.)]] [[concodes|concedes]] that Sāyaṇa does not reject the spiritual authority of the Veda and that [[sāyana|Sāyaṇa]] does not deny that there is a higher truth contained in the _[[rks.|ṛks.]]_ He further says (IX) that we must take seriously the hint of Yāska ([[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo does not quote the [[Nirukta.|Nirukta,]] but probably he has in mind Nir. I. 20 [[sākṣātkrtadharmāna|'sākṣātkṛtadharmāṇa]] [[pgayo|ṛṣayo]] babhūvuḥ'). Then he proceeds to state (XVII) that many whole hymns of the Veda bear on their face a mystic meaning and that the [[rṣis|ṛṣis]] (p. XIX) for the sake of secrecy resorted to double [[meaninga,|meanings,]] a device easily manageable in the Sanskrit language. This is a hypothesis which cannot at all be accepted and is no more than [[more|mere]] conjecture. The Vedic mantras were composed thousands of years ago, when all persons among whom the [[gages|sages]] moved must have spoken the same language, though generally not [[80|so]] polished and poetical as that of the mantras and they were not addressed to men like the moderns whose [[P987]] every day thoughts, surroundings and languages are entirely different. Both the Master and the disciple (Mr. Kapali Sastry) cause confusion (or, may be, are themselves [[confused )|confused)]] by [[sup posing|supposing]] that the difficulties of the modern students of mantras did exist even at or near the time of the mantras. The most sublime thought of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] is that there is only one Spirit behind the various gods, Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni, Yama, Mātariśvan, that originally there was only that One, there was no day and night, no death and immortality. [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo himself describes (p. XXXII) [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 164. 46 and X. [[129.2|129. 2]] as [[the|'the]] summit of the [[vedio|vedic]] teaching.' This view of the one Entity is also illustrated in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VIII. 58. [[%|2]] 'one and the same Agni is kindled in many places, the one Sun enters the whole world and becomes many; the one Dawn illumines all this (physical) world, One became all this [[( assumes|(assumes]] various forms)'. No secrecy was observed about this [[fundamontal|fundamental]] truth and it was proclaimed in mantras that can be understood even by an ordinary man of to-day who knows a little Sanskrit. Because we of these recent centuries cannot understand some mantras, that does not mean that the ancient seers were guilty of a subterfuge and purposely composed mantras with two meanings. They might have indulged in the pastime of two meanings (express and metaphorical) in a few cases as [[postic|poetic]] [[devices.1612|devices.]][^1612] It is no fault of the sages, if we cannot understand their meaning, just as a blind man, who does not see a wooden post and comes to grief by dashing against it, should not blame the post, but should blame himself; we are to blame ourselves and should not foist upon the ancient sages a stratagem created by our own imagination or lack of [[know ledge|knowledge]] (Nir. I. [[16 ).1613|16).]][^1613]

[^1612]: The Master [[( Śri|(Śrī]] [[Aurobindo )|Aurobindo)]] and the pupil are at variance about the poetic character of the [[Mantras,|Mantras.]] [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo (p. XXXIV) after saying that his translation is a literary and not a strictly literal one speaks of the hymns as '_great poetry_', magnificent in their [[colourlag|colouring]] and images, [[poble|noble]] and beautiful in rhythm. Now let us hear the disciple. On p. [[63|65]] Mr. Kapali Sastry states [[the|'the]] hymnal poetry is unusual, different from other poetry, even from the most superb specimen' and then he chides the readers and men like the present author and impliedly his own Master [[it|'it]] is not permissible (why?) to class Vedic hymns with poetry of a literary and aesthetic [[kłod'.|kind'.]] On p. 31 Mr. Sastry asserts that [[mantras|'mantras]] have two meanings, the inner which is psychological or spiritual and the true meaning and a gross or external meaning for common men' and he adds that the use of words with double meanings was _deliberate_ but was _[[sffortless|effortless]]_ and _natural_.

[^1613]: [[यथो एतवविस्पधार्थी भवन्तीति। नैव स्थाणोरपराधो पदेनमन्धो न पश्यति पुरुषा Tere: ata i furgon|यथो एतदविस्पष्टार्था भवन्तीति । नैष स्थाणोरपराधो यदेनमन्धो न पश्यति पुरुषापराधः स भवति । निरुक्त]] I. 16.

[[P988]] [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo admits (p. XXXIII) that there are some key words in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] such as _[[sta,|ṛta,]] kratu, [[gravas,|śravas,]] ketu_ and that [[elabo rate|elaborate]] work would have to be done to fix the meanings of such [[worde.|words.]] But he does not undertake a study of the key words (each of which except 'ketu' occurs hundreds of times in the [[Rgveda)|Ṛgveda)]] by comparison of [[Rgvedic|Ṛgvedic]] passages and considering the light [[shod|shed]] on these words by the other [[Samhitās|Saṁhitās]] and the Brāhmaṇas. He mentions the occurrence of the word [[ṣta|ṛta]] in I. 164. 47 and IV. 21. 3 (_[[sadanat-Flasya|sadanāt-ṛtasya]]_) and '_[[stasya pathya|ṛtasya pathyā]]_' in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] III. [[12.7|12. 7]] and jumps to the conclusion that the last two words mean 'the path of truth' and remarks that [[we|'we]] have to find the path of Truth' (Foreword p. XXX.). In the H. of Dh. vol. IV. pp. 2-5 it has been shown that [[șta|ṛta]] in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] has three meanings, viz. (1) the regular and general order in the [[cosmos ;(2)|cosmos; (2)]] the correct and ordered way of the cult of the [[gods ;|gods;]] (3)'moral conduct of [[man'|man.']] In the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] [[sta|ṛta]] is not the same as 'satya', but the two are differentiated. In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 51. 2 the [[Visve-devas 1614|Viśve-devas]][^1614] are described as [[stadhitayaḥ|ṛtadhītayaḥ]] (whose thoughts are fixed on [[ṣta)|ṛta)]] and 'satyadharmāṇaḥ' (whose ordinances are true or fixed) and the sage prays to them to come to his sacrifice and to drink (_[[ajya|ājya]]_ and soma) by the tongue of Agni. In [[Rg. X|Ṛg. X.]] 190. 1 both [[sta|ṛta]] and satya are said to have arisen from rigorous or refulgent _tapas_ (of the Creator). In the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] [[sta|ṛta]] involved a very wide conception as stated above and 'satya' had a restricted [[moaning|meaning]] viz. truth or static order. In [[Rg. IX,|Ṛg. IX.]] 113. 4 Soma is described as one who proclaims [[sta,|ṛta,]] satya and [[średdhā|śraddhā]] (faith). Therefore, [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo is quite inaccurate when he [[translatos|translates]] _[[ṣta|ṛta]]_ by the word [[Truth'|'Truth']] and draws important conclusions from his inaccurate renderings. Similarly, [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo does not give a correct rendering when he translates (Foreword p. XXX) the [[word |word ']]_[[stacit|ṛtacit]]_' as truth-conscious [[( whatever|(whatever]] that may mean [[sco.|acc.]] to [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo). Mr. Kapali Sastry (p. [[46 )|46)]] goes [[&|a]] step further than his great master by stating that in the mantras true [[know ledge|knowledge]] is termed [[stacit'|'ṛtacit']] [[Truth--consciousness|Truth-consciousness]] [[( with|(with]] a capital C). It appears that both Master and disciple are misled by the meaning of 'cit' in such an attribute as [[sat-cit-ananda',|'sat-cit-ānanda',]] applied to _brahma_. They appear to take [[ta-cit'|'ṛta-cit']] as meaning two distinct things [[yta'|'ṛta']] and 'cit'. The word [['rtacit'|'ṛtacit']] occurs [[P989]] five times in the [[Rg.;|Ṛg.;]] [[in 1615|in]][^1615] I. [[145.5,|145. 5,]] IV. [[3.4,|3. 4,]] [[V, 3.9(\*ftacit|V. 3. 9 ('ṛtacit]] is here an epithet of Agni), in [[VII 85.4|VII. 85. 4]] (it is an adjective of [[hots)|hotṛ)]] and in IV. 16. 10 it qualifies the word _nārī_ [[( in|(in]] the context, [[Saci,|Śacī,]] the wife of Indra). The present author wonders whether [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and his disciple cared to go into the meaning of [[\*ptacit'|'ṛtacit']] in the several verses where it occurs. They pay no attention to the word [['ļṇacit'|'ṛṇacit']] that occurs in [[Rg. II,|Ṛg. II.]] 23. 17 as an attribute of [[Brahmanaspati. 1616.|Brahmaṇaspati.]][^1616]

IX. 113. 4,]]
