It will be noticed that here [[m e|ऋतचित्]] and [[Fey|सत्य]] are both epithets of Agni. They must be given some separate meanings; [[A J arece a merr rret of THIRIK.|स सुक्रतुर्ऋतचिदस्तु होता य आदित्य शवसा वां नमस्वान् । ऋ.]] [[VII, 85, 4|VII. 85. 4]] (O sons of Aditi! May that [[Hotr|Hotṛ]] priest, who makes obeisance to you with strength (in a loud [[voice).' be|voice), be]] a man of good deeds (or will) and knowing [[ṣta,|ṛta,]] moral conduct [[( or|(or]] cosmic law). [[Fit:|सत्यः]] in I. [[145.5|145. 5]] would have to be rendered as truthful or pure. The word [[para|चित्]] may be derived from root [[fear|चि]] to gather or from [[Paa|चित्]] to [[kaow.|know.]]

[^1616]: [[ĦRUFURT PETTERE CHE HA wa u .|स ऋणचिदृणया ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्द्रुहो हन्ता मह ऋतस्य धर्तरि ॥ ऋ.]] [[II,|II.]] 23. 17.

The space at the author's disposal does not allow him to [[abow|show]] up the other unwarranted propositions and conclusions of [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and Mr. Kapali Sastry. The author will set out the final conclusion of [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo (Foreword p. XXIX) "what then is the secret meaning, the esoteric sense which emerges by this way of understanding the [[Veda ?|Veda?]] ......... The thought around which all is centred is the seeking after Truth, Light, Immortality. There is a truth deeper and higher than the truth of outward existence, a Light greater and higher than the light of human understanding which comes [[by:|by]] revelation and inspiration, an immortality towards which the soul has to rise. We have to find our way to that, to get into touch with this Truth and Immortality." This is a grand peroration, but all this is built on shaky and meagre foundations, such as the wrong [[mean ings|meanings]] attached to the words [[ṣta|ṛta]] and [[ștaoit.|ṛtacit.]] Mr. Kapali Sastry [[( on|(on]] p. [[46 )|46)]] practically reproduces this grand summing up of his _guru_.

Mr. Kapali Sastry launches (on pp. [[22–26 )|22-26)]] a bitter diatribe against Sāyaṇa but ultimately he has to admit (pp. [[27–28 )|27-28)]] that Sāyaṇa is not merely useful, but indispensable to the students of the Veda. On p. 23 he translates a sūtra from Jaimini's work [[the|'the]] purpose of the Veda being ritualistic, words which do not have that significance are useless' and remarks that this clearly lays down that the only purpose of the Vedas is that of ritual, those that do not pertain to that (ritualistic action) are worthless. The author doubts whether Mr. Kapali Sastry has carefully read the [[Purvamimānsāsūtras|Pūrvamīmāṁsāsūtras]] or has at least [[P990]] [[Correctly|correctly]] stated the position; what he quotes is the Pūrvapakṣa view [[( prima|(_prima]] [[facie|facie_]] view). Jaimini's position is contained in the 7th [[sūtra 1617|sūtra]][^1617] [[as|'as]] those passages (that do not directly concern themselves with ritualistic [[actions )|actions)]] form one syntactically connected whole with the passages prescribing _[[ridhis|vidhis]]_ they are to be considered as commendatory of the vidhis'. Mr. Sastry is not satisfied with saying (on p. 8) [[the|'the]] sages Madhucchandas and others are _seers of the mantra_, the _Gods were present to the vision of these seers of old_', but he adds 'this seer of the beyond is [[alao|also]] the hearer of the truth; therefore that the poet-[[geors|seers]] are truth-hearers _[[kavayah satyaśı utah|kavayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ]]_, is famous in the Veda' (p. 64). The present writer wonders whether Mr. Sastry carefully read the original passages where the words [[kayayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ'occur|'kavayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ' occur]] in the Veda. The words _[[kaziḥ|kaviḥ]]_ and _[[karayaḥ|kavayaḥ]]_ occur several hundred times in the [[Rgveda,|Ṛgveda,]] but 'satyaśrutaḥ' occurs only thrice in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 57. 8, V. [[58.8|58. 8]] and VI. [[49.6;|49. 6;]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. [[57, 8|57. 8]] and [[V.58.|V. 58.]] 8 are identical. In [[Rg. V.57. 8 and 1617a|Ṛg. V. 57. 8 and]][^1617a] V. 58. 8 it is the wind-gods (Maruts) that are addressed as _[[kavayah|kavayaḥ]]_ (wise) and _[[salyaśrutah|satyaśrutaḥ]]_ (well-known as conferring true rewards) and _not sages_. VI. [[49.6|49. 6]] (first half of which is addressed to Parjanya and Vāta-wind god) runs 'O [[esta blishers|establishers]] of the world! (O [[Maruts !)|Maruts!)]] that are wise and well-known as conferring true rewards, make the world turn towards the man by whose lauds you are praised' (this half seems to be addressed to the band of Maruts). Further comment is [[superfluous. 1618|superfluous.]][^1618]

[^1617]: [[WARET frecarerai yangulat aracunara, para tenere ueanturva Fautai 3:15. A .|आम्नायस्य क्रियार्थत्वादानर्थक्यमतदर्थानां तस्मादनित्यमुच्यते । ...'विधिना त्वेकवाक्यत्वात्स्तुत्यर्थेन विधीनां स्युः ॥ पू. मी. सू.]] I. 2. 1 and 7.

[^1617a]: हये नरो मरुतो [[मृटता|मृळता]] नस्तुवीमघासो अमृता [[ऋतज्ञाः|ऋतज्ञाः ।]] [[सत्यनुतः|सत्यश्रुतः]] कवयो युवानो [[TEACHIETTATOTT: 11.|बृहद्गिरयो बृहदुक्षमाणाः ॥ ऋ.]] [[V.57, 8|V. 57. 8]] and V. 58. 8; [[T TATHI WHITUT: fenot Ferragatura i fuga: aut TEU MITHSTA: futaiTqi yun .|पर्जन्यवाता वृषभा पृथिव्याः पुरीषाणि जिन्वतमप्यानि । सत्यश्रुतः कवयो यस्य गीर्भिर्जगतः स्थातर्जगदा कृणुध्वम् ॥ ऋ.]] VI. 49. 6. All words [[jaciudiog Fre: re:|including सत्यश्रुतः कवयः]] in the latter half of V. 57. 8 are epithets applied to Maruts in the first [[ball,|half.]] [[Neur : art:|सत्यश्रुतः कवयः]] in the latter [[balf|half]] of VI. 49. 6 are vocatives as the [[treure|पदपाठ]] shows and are addressed to Maruts as in [[V:|V.]] 57. 8 and [[V,|V.]] 58. 8. The words [[FTHA: Fra:|सत्यश्रुतः कवयः]] do not refer at all to Vedic poets in any of the three cases.

[^1618]: The present author is aware that thousands of people look upon [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo as a yogin, a prophet and as a World Teacher. But the search for truth is higher than all [[propbets|prophets]] and [[yogios|yogins]] put [[togetber.|together.]] [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and his disciple claim that he has received a new revelation about the Veda beyond the ken of all scholars of antiquity and modern times and they have very severely [[haadled|handled]] [[Yaska,|Yāska,]] Jaimini, Sāyaṇa and other commentators. The devotees and admirers of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo must allow the same freedom to the critics of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo's Vedic [[lacubrations|lucubrations]] for showing how and why they are wrong and are requested not to take umbrage at what [[tbo|the]] present writer [[bas|has]] said about his theories but only to reply to the [[aathor's|author's]] brief [[criticismo|criticisms]] on their merits or [[demerits,|demerits.]]

[[P991]] The [[Mimānsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] made [[&|a]] sweeping generalisation that the whole Veda is meant for sacrifice. They went rather too far but they had substantial grounds for doing so. On pp. [[980–982|980-982]] of the H. of Dh. vol. [[II,|II.]] it has been shown how even the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] discloses that a complicated system of sacrifices with three _savanas_, numerous priests, three fires, existed in those far-off days and sacrifices like Atirātra ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. [[103.7)|103. 7)]] and Trikadruka ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. [[32.3,|32. 3,]] II. 11. 17, [[VIII, 13, 18,|VIII. 13. 18,]] [[VIII,|VIII.]] 92. 21, [[X 14, 16)|X. 14. 16)]] were being performed. The [[Mīmānsakas bad|Mīmāṁsakas had]] a long tradition behind them. But the case of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo's theories is entirely different. On flimsy grounds and mistaken meanings he builds up an imposing structure of an exoteric and also an esoteric meaning of vedic mantras, propounds that the sages wanted secrecy for their doctrines and all that they were concerned with was Truth, Light and [[Conscious1888.|Consciousness.]] It has been already shown that there are several philosophical and speculative hymns in the [[Rgveda,|Ṛgveda.]] But there is no motive of secrecy therein. If it is only Truth, Light and Consciousness with which the [[vedio|vedic]] sages were concerned ten thousand verses would not have been necessary. One should like to know what secret or higher or deeper esoteric Truth or Light or Consciousness to be kept concealed from common men is contained in hymns such as [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. 55 [[( sleeping|(sleeping]] charm), VII. 103 [[(maṇdāka-stuti),|(maṇḍūka-stuti),]] X. 34 (gambler's song), X. 119 (exhilaration of power of Soma [[od|on]] Indra), [[X|X.]] 166 (invoking destruction of enemies), X. 190 (brief creation hymn), X. 191 (for concord and co-operation). Many more such hymns may be cited, where the theory of secrecy and Truth, Light and Consciousness would entirely fail. Besides, what is the secret (or spiritual or esoteric) element in such passages as those quoted above viz. I. 164. 46, X. [[129.%,|129. 2,]] VIII. [[58.2,|58. 2,]] which clearly express the most profound Truth. If the [[Mimāýsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] made a too wide generalization, [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo makes a far wider generalization with [[yory|very]] little basis.

The mantras of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] have a meaning and are not [[unmoaning|unmeaning]] letters as often in [[Tantrik|Tāntrik]] works. There is a [[discus sion|discussion]] in Nirukta [[I|I.]] [[15–16|15-16]] where it is said that in the absence of the Nirukta the apprehension of the meaning of the words used in mantras would not follow and the view of Kautsa is cited that the Nirukta is useless for understanding the meaning of mantras, since the mantras themselves have no sense (or are [[uselogs|useless]] or [[purposeless ).|purposeless).]] [[Yāska 1618|Yāska]][^1618a] replies that mantras do possess [[P992]] a meaning since they employ the very same words that are used in ordinary Sanskrit, and then quotes a passage of the Ait. Br. [[(1.|(I.]] 5). [[Sabara|Śabara]] on Jai. I. 2. 41 states that where one cannot get at the meaning one has to come to some meaning on a [[con. sideration|consideration]] of other Vedic passages and on the basis of roots following Nirukta and Grammar.

[^1618a]: [[अथापीदमन्तरेण मन् वर्थप्रत्ययो न विद्यते। "दिदं विधास्थानं व्याकरणस्य काय स्वार्थसाधकं । "अर्थवन्तः शब्दसामान्यात् । "यथी एखद् माह्मणेन स्पसम्पना|अथापीदमन्तरेण मन्त्रेष्वर्थप्रत्ययो न विद्यते । ...तदिदं विद्यास्थानं व्याकरणस्य कार्त्स्न्यं स्वार्थसाधकं च । ...'अर्थवन्तः शब्दसामान्यात् । ...'यथो एतद् ब्राह्मणेन रूपसम्पन्ना]] विधीयन्त इति, उदितानुवादः स भवति । [[एतबै यज्ञस्य समूद्धं यापससुद्धं यत्कर्म क्रियमाण unforestal faoi|एतद्वै यज्ञस्य समृद्धं यद्रूपसमृद्धं यत्कर्म क्रियमाणमृगभिवदति । निरुक्त]] [[I. 15-16:|I. 15-16;]] [[affrete 978:1 .12. 32; sretalen eta|अविशिष्टस्तु वाक्यार्थः । जै. I. 2. 32; अविशिष्टस्तु लोके]] प्रयुज्यमानानां वेदे च पदानामर्थः । [[सं|स]] यथैव लोके विवक्षितस्तथैव [[घेवेपि|वेदेपि]] भवितुमर्हति । [["अर्थपत्यायनार्थमेव यो मन्त्रोचारणम् । शपर भाग्य,|...'अर्थप्रत्यायनार्थमेव यज्ञे मन्त्रोच्चारणम् । शबर's भाष्य.]]

One of the important [[topios|topics]] dwelt upon at length in the Purāṇas is that of _avatāras_. This emphasis on the conception of avatāras has greatly influenced the forms of religious worship, vratas and festivals. The subject of avatāras has been dealt with in the H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. [[II,|II.]] pp. 717-724. It has been shown there that the beginnings of the doctrine of avatāras and of some of the well-known avatāras may be traced to the Vedic Literature viz. to the [[Satapatha-Brāhmana|Śatapatha-Brāhmaṇa]] (story of Manu and the fish in I. 8. 1. 1-6), story of [[Kurma|Kūrma]] [[(tortoise )|(tortoise)]] in [[Satapatha|Śatapatha]] VII. 5. 1. 5, of Varāha (Boar) in [[Sat.|Śat.]] Br. XIV. [[1.2,|1. 2,]] 11, [[Vamana|Vāmana]] (Dwarf) in [[Sat.|Śat.]] Br. I. 2. 5. 1 ff., [[Krspa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] son of [[Devaki,|Devakī,]] in Chān. Up. [[II,|III.]] 17. 6 and that the number of avatāras and the [[Dames|names]] also varied. But the treatment was not exhaustive and hence some details are added here from the [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] and from general considerations.

Avatāra (from root _tṛ._ with '_ava_') means [[descending|'descending']] or [[descent'|'descent']] and the word is applied to gods, assuming the form of a human being or even of an animal and continuing to live in that form till the purpose for which that form was assumed was carried out. Reincarnation is one of the fundamental [[dootrines|doctrines]] of Christianity. But there is a vast difference between that doctrine and the Hindu theory. Re-incarnation in [[Chris tianity|Christianity]] is single and unique, while in the Hindu theory [[28|as]] adumbrated in the [[Gita|Gītā]] [[(IV.5-8)|(IV. 5-8)]] and the Purāṇas there have been many incarnations of the Deity and there may he many more in future. It is a comforting belief for [[ordinary mon|ordinary men]] to hold that when the affairs of the world are in a mess God comes down to the earth to set matters right. And this belief is held not only by the Hindus and Buddhists but by many peoples (including some in the rich and educated West) far apart from each other. Most Hindus, however, do not believe that great [[P993]] men, saints or prophets like [[Saṅkarācārya,|Śaṅkarācārya,]] Nānak, Shivaji or [[Mahatma|Mahātmā]] Gandhi are born again as avatāras in times of crisis. The Buddhists made Buddha in their Mahāyāna teachings go through many avatāras as _bodhisattva_ before he attained [[Buddha hood.|Buddhahood.]] In modern times many persons pose or are made to pose as avatāras by their admirers or followers. Recently, Mr. J. G. Bennett (Hodder and Stoughton, 1958) has published a book called [["Subud'|'Subud']] (Sushila, Buddha, Dharma) in which he suggests his firm belief that one Pak Subuh who hails from Indonesia is an _avatar_, the messenger from above for whom mankind is waiting. The Indian theory of avatāras is connected with the theory about yugas and [[manyantaras.|manvantaras.]] When the world is in [[gerious troubles|serious trouble,]] people believe that deliverance will come by the grace of God and they are often justified in their belief by the appearance of [[godly.|godly]] men who appear with some noble mission and masterly idea suited to the particular time and place when they [[appear,|appear.]]

In medieval and modern times the avatāras of Viṣṇu have been regarded as ten, viz. Matsya, Kūrma, Varāha, [[Nșsimha|Nṛsiṁha]] or [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] (Man-lion), Vāmana, Paraśurāma, [[Rama|Rāma]] (son of [[Dasaratha),|Daśaratha),]] [[Krṣṇa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Buddha and Kalkin. The Varāha-purāṇa mentions these [[ton|ten]] in that [[order.1619)|order.]][^1619] In an inscription on the lintel above the figure of [[Śhaṅkara-Nārāyana|Śaṅkara-Nārāyaṇa]] in the [[Varāba Perumal 1620|Varāha-Perumal]][^1620] temple, [[thia Paurāṇik|this Paurāṇik]] verse except the first six letters (which cannot be read) is seen inscribed. The _locus classicus_ about the descent of God in different [[formas|forms]] is in the [[Bhagavad gita 1621|Bhagavadgītā]][^1621] viz. 'Whenever piety or righteousness (_dharma_) [[P994]] declines and [[impiaty|impiety]] grows up, I [[oreate|create]] myself. Age after age I am born for the protection of the good, for the [[destruc tion|destruction]] of evil-doers and the establishment of piety'. The [[game|same]] idea occurs in some of the _parvans_ of the Mahābhārata, as in Vanaparva [[272.71|272. 71]] and [[Aśramedhika-parva|Āśvamedhika-parva]] 54. 13. None of the ten avatāras, except those of [[Krṣṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] and probably [[Rama,|Rāma,]] [[("Rāmaḥ sastrabhṛtām-ahaun',|('Rāmaḥ śastrabhṛtām-aham',]] [[Gitā|Gītā]] X. [[31 )|31)]] are, however, [[men. tioned|mentioned]] by [[Dame|name]] in the [[Bhagavad-gitā.|Bhagavad-gītā.]] The number and names of the avatāras are not uniform in the Mahābhārata. In the [[Nārdyapiya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section of the [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] (chap. 339. verses 77-102) only six avatāras and their exploits are expressly mentioned, [[viz,|viz.]] Varāha (bringing up the earth submerged in the ocean), [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] (killing demon [[Hiraṇyakasipu ),|Hiraṇyakaśipu),]] Vāmana [[(van quishing|(vanquishing]] Bali and making him dwell in [[Patāla),|Pātāla),]] [[Bhargava Rama ( extirpating ksatriyas),|Bhārgava Rāma (extirpating kṣatriyas),]] Rāma, son of [[Dasaratha|Daśaratha]] (killing [[Ravaṇa),|Rāvaṇa),]] [[Krṣṇa ( killing|Kṛṣṇa (killing]] or vanquishing [[Kamsa, Narakasura, Bapa, Kalayavana,|Kaṁsa, Narakāsura, Bāṇa, Kālayavana,]] Jarāsandha, [[Siśupāla).|Śiśupāla).]] Then the same [[chapter 1622|chapter]][^1622] mentions [[ton|ten]] avatāras as follows: [[Hamsa,|Haṁsa,]] Kūrma, Matsya, Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] [[Vamana,|Vāmana,]] Rāma [[(Bhargava),|(Bhārgava),]] Rāma [[Dasarathi,|Dāśarathi,]] Sātvata, Kalki. Here Buddha is omitted and [[Kṭspa|Kṛṣṇa]] is called Sātvata and [[Hamsa|Haṁsa]] is added. In the [[Harivarsa 1623|Harivaṁśa]][^1623] it is said that the past avatāras have been thousands and in future also they would be thousands. [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] says the same thing. The [[Harivamsa|Harivaṁśa]] (I. 41. 27 ff) [[Dames|names]] only the following and their exploits, viz. [[Varāba,|Varāha,]] [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] Jāmadagnya [[(Parasurama),|(Paraśurama),]] Rāma, [[Kroṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] and [[Vedavyasa,|Vedavyāsa.]] But as [[Kośava|Keśava]] is said to be the 9th (I. [[41.6)|41. 6)]] it is to be [[under stood|understood]] that Matsya and Kūrma were counted, though not expressly named and Kalki [[Viṣpuyabas|Viṣṇuyaśas]] is mentioned as a [[P995]] [[futuro avatara.|future avatāra.]] The usual [[ton|ten]] avatāras are named in [[Vardha 1634|Varāha]][^1624] [[(4. %,|(4. 2,]] 48. 17-22, 55. 36-37), [[Mataya'|Matsya]] 285. 6-7, Agni [[(ohap.|(chap.]] 2-16 stories about all [[ton),|ten),]] [[Narasimhapurdna|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]] (chap. 36), Padma VI. 43. 13-15). In [[Vayu|Vāyu]] 98. 68-104 the text presents the avatāras in a confused manner and appears to mention [[ton|ten]] avatāras, viz. Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Māndhātę,|Māndhātṛ,]] Jāmadagnya, [[Rama ( Dasarathi),|Rāma (Dāśarathi),]] Veda-vyāsa, [[Vasu deva,|Vāsudeva,]] Kalkin [[Vispuyaśas.|Viṣṇuyaśas.]] In [[Brahminda|Brahmāṇḍa]] III. [[73.75 \#|73. 75 ff]] [[thote|there]] are lists of avatāras different from the present ten. In the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] the avatāras of Viṣṇu are mentioned in several places. In I. 3. 1-25 twenty-two avatāras including [[Brahma,|Brahmā,]] [[Dovarṣi|Devarṣi]] Nārada (who promulgated the [[Satvata|Sātvata]] system), [[Nara Nārāyana,|Nara-Nārāyaṇa,]] Kapila [[( who|(who]] taught to Asuri the [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] [[system ),|system),]] [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Rsabha,|Ṛṣabha,]] (son of [[Nabhi|Nābhi]] and [[Merudeyi),1625|Merudevī),]][^1625] [[Dhanvan tari,|Dhanvantari,]] [[Mohini,|Mohinī,]] [[Vedavyāga,|Vedavyāsa,]] Balarāma and [[Kṛspa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Buddha, Kalki are mentioned. In II. 7 twenty-three avatāras are mentioned, many of which are the same as in I. 3, but in II. 7 Dhruva, [[Pythu|Pṛthu]] son of Vena, [[Hayagriva|Hayagrīva]] are mentioned, the first two of which are mentioned as avatāras hardly anywhere else. In Bhāgavata [[XI,|XI.]] 40. 17-22 the following avatāras are mentioned, viz. Matsya, [[Hayasirsa,|Hayaśīrṣa,]] Kūrma, Sūkara, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Bhargava|Bhārgava]] Rāma, Rāma, Vāsudeva, Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna, Aniruddha, Buddha, Kalki. In [[Bhagavata XL|Bhāgavata XI.]] 4. 17-22 sixteen avatāras are set out, viz. usual ten plus [[Hamsa,|Haṁsa,]] Datta (Dattātreya), Kumāra (Nārada), [[Reabha,|Ṛṣabha,]] Vyāsa and [[Hayagriva.|Hayagrīva.]] Twelve avatāras are noted in Matsya 99. 14 and Padma V. 13. 182-186. The [[Prapancasāratantra|Prapañcasāratantra]] (ascribed to the great advaita teacher [[Saṅkarācārya)|Śaṅkarācārya)]] mentions [[( in|(in]] [[Patala|Paṭala]] 20. [[59 )|59)]] Matsya, Kūrma, Varāha, [[Nșsimha,|Nṛsiṁha,]] Kubja (i. e. [[Vamana),|Vāmana),]] three [[Ramas|Rāmas]] [[P996]] [[(i.e.|(i. e.]] [[Bhārgavarama,|Bhārgavarāma,]] Dāśarathi [[Rama|Rāma]] and [[Balarama),|Balarāma),]] [[Krona|Kṛṣṇa]] and Kalkin (i. e. it omits Buddha). The Ahirbudhnya [[Samhita|Saṁhita]] (5. 50-57) enumerates 39 avatāras of [[Vasudeva,|Vāsudeva,]] that are set out by Otto Schrader in his Introduction to the [[Panoarātra|Pañcarātra]] and the Ahirbudhnya [[Samhita,|Saṁhitā,]] pp. 42-43. The [[Viṣṇupurāṇa 1626|Viṣṇupurāṇa]][^1626] states that [[Lakṣmi|Lakṣmī]] follows Viṣṇu in his avatāras. The Purāṇas are full of the descriptions of the exploits of Viṣṇu in his several avatāras. But it should not be supposed that Śiva had no [[ayatāras.|avatāras.]] The Vāyu (chap. 23) mentions 28 [[avatārag|avatāras]] of Maheśvara the last of which was [[Nakuli (Lakuli)|Nakulī (Lakulī)]] as verse 221 [[gays.|says.]] In Varāha 15. 10-19 all avatāras are praised except that of Buddha. But [[Varāba ( 48,|Varāha (48.]] 20-22) provides that worship of [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] frees men from the fear of sins, of [[Vamana|Vāmana]] leads to removal of delusion, of [[Parasurama|Paraśurāma]] to wealth, one should worship Dāśarathi [[Rama|Rāma]] for the destruction of cruel enemies, one desirous of a son should worship [[Balarama|Balarāma]] and [[Kșsna,|Kṛṣṇa,]] one who desires a handsome form should worship Buddha and one should worship Kalkin for the slaughter of [[enemies 1627|enemies.]][^1627] The Agnipurāṇa (chap. 49. 1-9) describes what characteristics the images of the ten avatāras should possess and says that the image of Buddha should be represented as having a quiet face, long ear-lobes, fair complexion, wearing an upper garment, seated in Padmāsana posture and his hands should have the _varada_ and _abhaya_ poses.

[[Vido un|Vide भागवत]] [[1.|I.]] 3. 26 and [[arrêt 16,|अग्नि 16.]] 11-12 for similar [[words. .|words.]]

[^1624]: The passage of Matsya [[( 285.|(285.]] [[6–7)|6-7)]] is very likely a [[later loter polation,|later interpolation,]] because in another place in the Matsya, the names of avatāras are different. In [[Mataya|Matsya]] 47. 106 there is a reference to the curse on [[Viggo|Viṣṇu]] by [[Bhrga|Bhṛgu]] that since [[Viṣgu kūled|Viṣṇu killed]] his wife he would have to be [[bora|born]] seven times as a human being and the seven avatāras are [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Māndbāta,|Māndhātā,]] [[Jāmadagoya (Bhargava)|Jāmadagnya (Bhārgava)]] [[Rama,|Rāma,]] Rāma Dāśarathi, Vedavyāsa, [[Baddba,|Buddha,]] [[Kalkio|Kalkin]] and three more [[(io|in]] [[47,|47.]] [[237--240 ),|237-240),]] viz. [[Nārāyaga,|Nārāyaṇa,]] [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] and [[Vimana|Vāmana]] are [[added: 1o|added; in]] Matsya 54. 15-19, [[Nakgatrapuruṣa-vrata|Nakṣatrapuruṣa-vrata]] is described and the usual ten avatāras are [[named,|named.]]

[^1625]: [[Rṣabba,|Ṛṣabha,]] son of Nābhi, appears to be the first [[tirthankars|tīrthaṅkara]] of the Jainas and was probably raised to the status of an incarnation of [[Visgu|Viṣṇu]] as Buddha was raised. In [[I.|भागवत I.]] 3. 24 [[G|बुद्ध]] is referred to as follows [[: :|:]] [[कलो समवृत्ते संमोहाय अरविवार दो नाम्नाऽजनत: कीकवेषु भविष्यति। नमो पदाप grerear uangHirtaa|ततः कलौ संप्रवृत्ते संमोहाय सुरद्विषाम् । बुद्धो नाम्नाऽजनसुतः कीकटेषु भविष्यति ॥; नमो बुद्धाय शुद्धाय दैत्यदानवमोहिने । भागवत]] X. [[40.22.|40. 22.]]

[^1626]: एवं यथा जगत्स्वामी देवराजो जनार्दनः । अवतारं करोत्येष तथा [[श्रीस्तत्सहा यिनी॥|श्रीस्तत्सहायिनी ॥]] पुनश्च [[पद्मादुता|पद्मादुद्भूता]] [[यदादित्योऽभवद्धरिः।|यदादित्योऽभवद्धरिः ।]] यदा च भार्गवो [[रामस्तदाभूखरणी वियम्|रामस्तदाभूद्धरणी त्वियम्]] । राघवत्वेऽभवत्सीता रुक्मिणी कृष्णजन्मनि । अन्येषु चावतारेषु विष्णोरेषा [[सहायिनी।|सहायिनी ॥]] विष्णु I. 9. 139-141; आदित्य means [[धामन|वामन]] here.

[^1627]: [[TERAT 3774 nuia Tirgama: I get arcuata rara ar997: n sa|शान्तात्मा लम्बकर्णश्च गौराङ्गश्चाम्बरावृतः । ऊर्ध्वं पद्मस्थितो बुद्धो वरदाभयदायकः ॥ अग्नि]] 49. 8; [[TEFIAT|बृहत्संहिता]] 57. 4 [[Traianino: A nta: miten? Tataare face para wara : N;|पद्माङ्कितकरचरणः प्रसन्नमूर्तिः सुनीचकेशश्च । पद्मासनोपविष्टो पितेव जगतो भवति बुद्धः ॥;]] vide [[TR|वराह]] 48. 20-22 [[a HE TUTTE विधार्थे|वामनं मोहनाशाय वित्तार्थे]] जमदग्निजम् । [[करशत्रुविनाशाय पजेदाशरथि|क्रूरशत्रुविनाशाय यजेद्दाशरथिं]] बुधः । [[पलकृष्णौ पजेजीमान्|बलकृष्णौ यजेच्छ्रीमान्]] पुत्रकामो न संशयः । रूपकामो [[भजेत्|भजेद्]] बुद्धं [[शवधाताय|शत्रुघाताय]] [[कल्किनम् ।.|कल्किनम् ॥.]]

From the facts that Kṣemendra in his [[Dasavatāra-carita 1628|Daśāvatāra-carita]][^1628] (composed in 1066 [[A, D.),|A. D.),]] and the [[Gitagovinda|Gītagovinda]] of Jayadeva (court poet of [[Laksmanasena)|Lakṣmaṇasena)]] mention the [[ugual ton|usual ten]] avatāras with Fish as the first and that the Matsya passage about ten avatāras is quoted by Aparārka (first half of 12th century), it follows that all the ten avatāras of Viṣṇu had become recognised throughout India at least about the 10th century A. D.

[^1628]: [[HAFT: ATTE: goue hageprat erhara: filmu: ATT भुगतमुनिः ककिनामा च विष्णु दशावतारचरित|मत्स्यः कूर्मो वराहः पुरुषहरिवपुर्वामनो जामदग्न्यः काकुत्स्थः कंसहन्ता स च सुगतमुनिः कल्किनामा च विष्णुः ॥ दशावतारचरित]] [[1.2.|I. 2.]]

[[P997]] Kumārila (7th century A. D.) did not accept Buddha as an avatāra, though about that [[contury|century]] some people had come to recognize him as such (vide note 1629). Besides, as shown above, there were many views about the total number of avatāras, their names and the order in which they appeared. Vide Dr. Katre's paper in Allahabad University Studies, vol. X. pp. [[37–130|37-130]] for discussion on 33 avatāras. The [[Varāba|Varāha]] avatāra is mentioned in the Eran stone [[Boar 1629|Boar]][^1629] Inscription of Toramāṇa (Gupta inscriptions pp. 158-160) in the first quarter of the 6th century. The [[Raghuvansa|Raghuvaṁśa]] (IV. 53 and [[58 )|58)]] refers to the recovery of land near the Sahya mountain from the Western Sea by Rāma [[(Bhargava);|(Bhārgava);]] vide pp. 89-90 and note 224 above for references in the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] and [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] to Paraśurāma's exploits. The [[Sarvānukramani|Sarvānukramaṇī]] p. 42 on [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X, 110 names as [[ļṣi|ṛṣi]] Jamadagni or his son Rāma The Meghadūta mentions the planting of the left foot of Viṣṇu on Bali (i.e. the [[Vamana|Vāmana]] avatāra). Māgha in [[Sisupala-vadha|Śiśupāla-vadha]] (XV. 58) regards Bodhisattva (Buddha) as an avatāra of Hari and as sought to be tempted by the hordes of Māra. [[Māgha 1630|Māgha]][^1630] flourished about 725-775 A. D. The [[know ledge|knowledge]] of [[Vamana|Vāmana]] and [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] avatāras can be carried back centuries before the Mahābhāṣya of Patañjali, since it refers to works and dramatic representations of the imprisonment of Bali and the killing of [[Kamsa|Kaṁsa]] (vide pp. 130, 203 and notes 330, 521 above). In the Daśāvatāra cave at Ellora one sees the [[repre sentations|representations]] of Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana and [[Krsna.|Kṛṣṇa.]] Those caves are ascribed to the 8th century [[A. D.1631|A. D.]][^1631] Therefore, it appears almost certain that some at least [[( viz.|(viz.]] [[Vamana,|Vāmana,]] [[Parasu rāma|Paraśurāma]] and [[Krsna)|Kṛṣṇa)]] of the usual [[ton|ten]] avatāras had been recognised some centuries before Christ and all ten had come to be recognised by some writers and other people before the 7th century A. D.

[^1629]: The first verse of the [[Iascription|Inscription]] is: [[a true to earra forarter: 1 at T a ste TATUETTA: 1|जयति धरण्युद्धरणे घनघोणाघातघूर्णितमहीध्रः । देवो वराहमूर्तिस्त्रैलोक्यमहागृहस्तम्भः ॥]] Gupta Inscriptions p. [[159,|159.]] This was engraved in the first year of the [[Hūna|Hūṇa]] king [[TATUT'S reiga|तोरमाण's reign]] on 10th of [[Pbālguda,|Phālguna,]] when the temple of Nārāyaṇa in his Boar avatāra was built. The probable date is about 500 to 510 A. D. This [[gaar|अवतार]] is sometimes called [[ITQATTIE, uie, stre, Heracle.|आदिवराह, यज्ञवराह, श्वेतवराह, महावराह.]] Compare [[para III' Autotu: t3 TETET E qua: 11.|हर्षचरित III 'महावराहपीवरस्कन्धपीठे नरकासुर इव भुवो गर्भादुद्भूतः ।'.]] Vide 'Royal conquests and [[caltural|cultural]] migrations' by C. [[Śivarama|Śivarāma]] Mūrti ([[Calcutta,|Calcuttā,]] 1955) for plate II C. for [[\* Adivarāba'|'Ādivarāha']] of 4th century A. D.

[^1630]: इति तत्तदा [[विकृतरूपमभजदविभिनचेतसम्|विकृतरूपमभजदविभिन्नचेतसम्]] । मारबलमिव [[भयङ्करता हरिबोधि HATUTOSTAVE F ORU|भयङ्करतां हरिबोधिसत्त्वमभिराजमण्डलम् ॥ शिशुपालवध]] XV. 58. For Māgha's date, vide the author's [[History|'History]] of Sanskrit Poetics' [[(1951 )|(1951)]] pp. 112-113 and 139.

[^1631]: Vide 'Cave Temples of India' by [[Fergasson|Fergusson]] and Burgess p. 438; Archaeological Survey of Western India by Burgess, Vol. V. p. 25.

[[P998]] The conception of avatāras contributed largely to the increase of Dharmaśāstra material. They gave rise to numerous vratas and festivals. For example, the [[Varāhapurāpa|Varāhapurāṇa]] devotes [[chaptera|chapters]] 39-48 to [[dvādaśi-vratas|dvādaśī-vratas]] in honour of the [[ton|ten]] avatāras from Matsya to Kalkin. There are separate festivals called [[Jayantis|Jayantīs]] of the avatāras such as [[Narasimha-jayanti|Narasiṁha-jayantī]] on [[Vaisakha|Vaiśākha]] śu. 14, [[Paraśurāma jayanti ( on Vaiśākha su. 3).|Paraśurāma-jayantī (on Vaiśākha śu. 3).]] Vide pp. [[262 263|262-263]] for the tithis and months in which the different avatāras [[appeared. 16316|appeared.]][^1631a]

[^1631a]: The following verse is cited from the [[curreain fa. Ph.|पुराणसमुच्चय in नि. सि.]] p. 81 : [[मत्योऽभूद्धतमुग्दिने|मत्स्योऽभूद्भुतभुग्दिने]] मधुसिते, [[कूमो विधी|कूर्मो विधौ]] माधवे, वाराहो [[गिरिजाइते|गिरिजासुते]] नभसि, [[यमते|यन्नृते]] सिते [[माधवे।|माधवे ।]] सिंहो, भाद्रपदे सिते [[इरितिधौ श्रीवामनी,|हरितिथौ श्रीवामनो,]] माधवे रामो [[गौरितिधावतः परमपूर|गौरितिथावतः परमभूद्]] रामो नवम्यां मधोः ॥ कृष्णोष्टम्यां नभसि सितपरे, [[चाश्चिने यहशम्यां वृद्धः,|चाश्विने यद्दशम्यां बुद्धः,]] कल्की नभसि [[समभूषष्ठवां क्रमेण ।.|समभूच्छुक्लषष्ठ्यां क्रमेण ॥]] The भक्तिप्रकाश p. 79 (of the [[वीरमिनोदय)|वीरमित्रोदय)]] also quotes this. The [[A.FA.|नि. सि.]] remarks that others cite some stray verses where some of the tithis are [[differeot|different]] and that some [[Koikana|Koṅkaṇa]] writers cite some verses as occurring in [[a regem|वराहपुराण]] in which [[A 4|मत्स्यजयन्ती]] is on [[Hmey|आषाढशुक्ल]] eleventh, [[Baddba|Buddha]] should be worshipped [[op die|on पौषशुक्ल]] 7th and so on.

Descriptions of each of the ten well-known [[avatūras|avatāras]] occur in several Purāṇas; for example, Matsya avatāra is described in Matsya chap. 1-2, Agni 2, [[Narasimua|Narasiṁha]] 37; Kūrma in Agni 3, Bhāgavata I. 3. 16, VIII. 7-8-10, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 38; [[Varāba|Varāha]] in Matsya 247-248, Vāyu 6. [[11–26,|11-26,]] Bhāgavata III. 13. 18-45, III. 19. 25-30, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 39; [[Narasimha avatara|Narasiṁha avatāra]] in Brahma 58. 12 ff., [[213.43|213. 43]] ff., Matsya 161-163, Bhāgavata I. 3. 18, VII. 8. 18, [[Narasimha-purāṇa|Narasiṁha-purāṇa]] 41-44; [[Vimana|Vāmana]] in Brahma 73 and 213. 80-185, [[Vāgu|Vāyu]] 98, 74-87, Vāmanapurāṇa [[78.51,|78. 51,]] [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] VIII. 18. 12 to [[VIII,|VIII.]] 22. 33, [[Narasimla|Narasiṁha]] 45; [[Parasurama|Paraśurāma]] in Matsya [[244–246,|244-246,]] [[Bhagavata IX,|Bhāgavata IX.]] 15. 13, [[IX,|IX.]] 16. 1-26 and vide above pp. 89-90 and note 204; Rāma in Brahma 176, Agni 5-11 (the seven _[[kāndas|kāṇḍas]]_ of the Rāmāyaṇa are summarised), Vāyu 88. [[183 198,|183-198,]] [[Bhag.|Bhāg.]] IX. 10-11, Padma [[IV.1-68,|IV. 1-68,]] [[Narasiinha|Narasiṁha]] 47-52; [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] in Brahma 14-17, [[180-21%,|180-212,]] Agni 12-15, Bhāg. X. 3, 44-45, [[50 52|50-52]] &c., [[XL|XI.]] 1 and 30, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 53; Buddha in Brahma 180. 27-39, Agni [[16,|16.]] 1-3, [[Varāba|Varāha]] 180. [[27–29,|27-29,]] [[213,|213.]] 32 ff., Padma VI. 31. 13-15, [[Bhag. I 3.24;|Bhāg. I. 3. 24;]] [[Kalkin -|Kalkin —]] Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] III. pp. [[923 925|923-925]] for references. The avatāras and their _[[jayantis|jayantīs]]_ are described in many Dharmaśāstra works, but the [[ļodarānanda,|Ṭoḍarānanda,]] vol. I. edited by Dr. P. L. Vaidya in the Ganga Oriental Series probably contains the longest account of the [[ton|ten]] avatāras [[(pp. 39-386 ).|(pp. 39-386).]] Thousands of verses are devoted by the Purāṇas to each of the topics of _dāna_ (gifts), [[sraddha,|śrāddha,]] [[tirtha|tīrtha]] and vrata and they have been quoted at [[longth|length]] by works on Dharmaśāstra. It would be [[P999]] best to bring together by way of illustration a few of the pages of the previous volumes of the History of Dharmaśāstra on these subjects.

Vide vol. II. pp. 880-881 (for gifts of certain dānas called _dhenus_ from Matsya chap. 82, Varāha, chap. 99-110, Agni, chap. 210), p. 882 (for gifts called parvata or meru from Matsya 83-92, Agni 210 quoted by Aparārka pp. 344-454), pp. 884-885 (for grahaśānti from Matsya 93 and about images of planets from Matsya [[84 ),|84),]] p. 892 [[( for|(for]] dedication of a reservoir of water to the public from Matsya 58), pp. [[895–896|895-896]] for the planting of trees and the dedication of a garden to the public [[( Matsya|(Matsya]] 59, Agni 70), pp. 896-899 (for dedication of temples and consecration of images in Matsya [[264–266,|264-266,]] Agni 60 and 66); vol. IV. pp. [[162–164|162-164]] about various hells (Agni 203, 371, Brahma 22, 214-215, Brahmavaivarta, [[Prakrtikhanda|Prakṛtikhaṇḍa]] 29 and 33, Nāradapurāṇa I. 15, Padma [[IV,|IV.]] 227, Bhaviṣya, Brāhmaparva 192, [[Bhāgayata|Bhāgavata]] V. 26, Viṣṇu V. 6, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 12 and 14); vol. IV. p. 170 about heaven being really happiness of the mind (Brahma 22. 44 and 47, Viṣṇu IV. 6. 46); [[VOL|vol.]] IV. pp. [[177–178|177-178]] on the doctrine of [[Karmevipāka ( Vāmana|Karmavipāka (Vāmana]] 12, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 15, Varāha 203, [[Viṣṇu dharmottara|Viṣṇudharmottara]] II. 102); vol. IV. p. 181 on the signs of approaching death [[( Vayu|(Vāyu]] 19, [[Linga|Liṅga]] 91, Mārk. 43. 29-39 or chap. 40 of Venk. ed. and [[Viṣṇudharmottara II, 218);|Viṣṇudharmottara III. 218);]] vol. IV. p. 212 on cremation (Varāha 187, Garuḍa II. 4); vol. IV. p. 256 on the persons entitled to perform funeral rites ([[Viṣṇu II,|Viṣṇu III.]] 13, Mārk. chap. 30 of B. I. ed. and 27 of [[Vonk,|Venk.]] ed.); vol. IV. pp. [[261–262|261-262]] the rites after death being called pūrva or nava, madhyama (navamiśra) and uttara (or purāṇa) in Viṣṇupurāṇa [[II. 13;|III. 13;]] vol. IV. p. 265 about an [[ativāhika|ātivāhika]] body being [[29gumed|assumed]] by the soul of a dead person (Brahma 214, [[Mārk,|Mārk.]] 16, Agni 230 and 371); vol. IV. p. 272 on [[aśauoa ( impurity|āśauca (impurity]] on birth and death) from Kūrma II. 23, [[Linga|Liṅga]] I. 89, Garuḍa, [[protakhanda|pretakhaṇḍa]] 5, Agni [[157–158,|157-158,]] Vāmana 14. It is unnecessary to refer to the pages of this volume itself for numerous references to Purāṇas as regards tithis, various vratas, times for religious rites, astrological matters [[&o.|&c.]]

It should not be supposed that the Purāṇas are restricted to [[topios|topics]] called religious in popular parlance and to the five [[charac teristio|characteristic]] topics of creation, re-creation [[( |(]]_sarga_, _pratisarga_ &c.) Some of the Purāṇas contain an exhaustive treatment of the duties of kings, ministers, commander-in-chief, judge, envoy scribes, court [[physioian|physician]] and of coronation, invasion [[&o|&c]]

[[P1000]] Many of these matters have already been dealt with in vol. III. of the H. of Dh. The most exhaustive treatment of what may be called political matters is found in Matsya chap. 215-226 and 240, Agni [[218–242,|218-242,]] Viṣṇudharmottara [[II.|II,]] chap. 2-7, 18-21, 24-26, 28, 61-63, [[66–72,|66-72,]] [[145–152,|145-152,]] 177. Among other Purāṇas Garuḍa I. 108-115, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 24 (in Venk. [[od,|ed,]] or 27 in [[Bagerji's|Banerji's]] ed.), Kālikā 87 contain some discussion of matters political. It should be noticed that both Matsya [[( 240.2)|(240. 2)]] and Agni [[(228.1)|(228. 1)]] employ the two technical words, [['Akranda'|'Ākranda']] and [['Pārspigrāha'|'Pārṣṇigrāha']] that are part of the theory of _[[Mandala|Maṇḍala]]_ in [[Kautilya|Kauṭilya]] (VI. 2. p. 260) explained in H. of Dh. vol. III. p. 222. The Matsyapurāṇa is profusely quoted by one of the earliest extant digests on Dharmaśāstra viz. the [[Kṛty& kalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] on Rājadharma (pp. 23, 25-30, 34-38, [[42–43,|42-43,]] 55-61, 118-122, [[123–124,|123-124,]] 158-161 and many verses from chap. 227 and 241 of the Matsya on the administration of justice in [[Vyavahāra kānda|Vyavahārakāṇḍa]] pp. 342, 345, 348, 377, 406, 409-10, 562, 581, 594-95, 599). The same digest quotes many verses [[(pp. 9–13,|(pp. 9-13,]] 128, 166, 178-181) from the Brahmapurāṇa which are not found in the printed Brahma ([[Ādan,|Ānan.]] ed.), but which are quoted by the [[Rājanīti. prakāśa|Rājanītiprakāśa]] of Mitramiśra (borrowing from [[Kr̥tyakalpataru)|Kṛtyakalpataru)]] pp. 138, 158, 283, 416-419 and some by the Rājadharmakaustubha of [[Anantadova|Anantadeva]] [[(pp. 326–330).|(pp. 326-330).]] The Viṣṇudharmottara on rājadharma is not quoted in the Kṛtyakalpataru on [[rajadharma,|rājadharma,]] but it is frequently quoted by the Rājanītiprakāśa e. g. Vi. Dh. II. [[18.1,|18. 1,]] 5-14 are quoted by R. N. P. on pp. 32-33, Vi. Dh. II. 18. 2-4 are quoted by R. N. P. p. 61, [[Vi. Dh. II,|Vi. Dh. II.]] 22. 1-185 are quoted by R. N. P. pp. 66-81 (on mantras to be recited and the several divinities to be invoked at a king's coronation), [[Vi, Dh.|Vi. Dh.]] II. 23. 1-13 are quoted by R. N. P. on pp. 82-83 (describing the [[towards|rewards]] of the mantras recited in Vi. Dh. II. 22). Vi. Dh. is quoted 21 times by the [[Rajadharma-kaustubha.|Rājadharma-kaustubha.]] These three Purāṇas alone ([[Mataya,|Matsya,]] Agni, [[Viṣoudharmottara )|Viṣṇudharmottara)]] devote several thousand verses to the topic of rāja-dharma and allied matters. The Garuḍa-purāṇa (I. 108-115) devotes about four hundred [[yerges|verses]] to [[Rājansti|Rājanīti]] (political thought) but many of them are like subhāṣitas (_bon mots_) and are borrowed from Manu (e. g. Garuḍa I. 109. 1 and 52, [[110.7,|110. 7,]] [[115 63|115. 63]] are respectively Manu [[VII,|VII.]] 213, VIII. 26, [[II,|II.]] 239, IX. 3), the Mahābhārata, the [[Nāradasmrti|Nāradasmṛti]] (e. g. 'na sā sabhā' in Garuḍa [[115.52|115. 52]] is Nārada III. [[18),|18).]] The Garuḍa itself states that it will expound the essence of _[[niti|nīti]]_ ([[Rajadharma)|Rājadharma)]] based upon [[P1001]] [[Artham sāstra 1632|Arthaśāstra]][^1632] and the like; while the colophons at the end of chapters 108-114 [[sver|aver]] that they contain the [[Nitisāra|Nītisāra]] promulgated by Bṛhaspati. One verse is almost the same as the [[Introduotory|Introductory]] verse 5 of Bāṇa's [[Kadambari 1633|Kādambarī]][^1633] and I am disposed to hold that it is the Garuḍa that is the borrower.

[^1632]: नीतिसारं प्रवक्ष्यामि [[अर्थशाचादिसंश्रितम्|अर्थशास्त्रादिसंश्रितम्]] । [[राजाविम्यो|राजादिभ्यो]] हितं [[पुण्यमायुःखर्गादि. aran N THE|पुण्यमायुःस्वर्गादिदायकम् ॥ गरुड]] I. 108. 1.

[^1633]: [[अकारणाविष्कृतकोपधारिणः खलाडय|अकारणाविष्कृतकोपधारिणः खलाद्भयं]] कस्य न नाम जायते । [[विष महाविषमस्य gta : HAARHET I TE|विषं महाहेर्विषमस्य दुर्वचः सुदुःसहं संनिपतेत्सदा मुखे ॥ गरुड]] I. 112. 16; compare [[fiicare|कादम्बरी]] Introductory verse 5 [['अकारणाविष्कृतघरदारुणादसजनात्|'अकारणाविष्कृतवैरदारुणादसज्जनात्]] कस्य भयं न [[जायते।|जायते ।]] विषं [[महारिष यस्य दुर्वचः Ti E Pia Frer te.|महाहेरिव यस्य दुर्वचः सुदुःसहं संनिहितं सदा मुखे ॥'.]]

Certain verses from the [[Mārkandeya-purāṇa|Mārkaṇḍeya-purāṇa]] (24. 5, 23-33 or chap. 27 and 21-31 in Banerji's ed.) are quoted by R. N. P. pp. 30-31 [[( about|(about]] the duties of kings and their acting in the peculiar ways of five gods, viz. Indra, Sūrya, Yama, Soma and Vāyu). The Dāyabhāga quotes the [[Markandeya-purāṇa|Markaṇḍeya-purāṇa]] for pointing out that _[[sāpindya|sāpiṇḍya]]_ in the matter of inheritance and succession is different from [[sāpiṇdya|sāpiṇḍya]] for the periods of aśauca [[( impurity|(impurity]] on [[death 1634|death]][^1634]). The Kṛtyakalpataru on Rājadharma [[(pp. 182-183 )|(pp. 182-183)]] quotes a passage from Skandapurāṇa on the festival of [[Kaumudimahotsava|Kaumudīmahotsava]] to be celebrated by the king. The same passage is quoted by R. N. P. pp. 419-421.

[^1634]: [[matare a few more abogada por are inte पुराणे|अशौचाद्यर्थे तु पिण्डलेपभुजामपि तत्तत्पिण्डलेपभोक्तृत्वेन सपिण्डत्वं मार्कण्डेयपुराणे]] निर्दिष्टं [[यथा-'पिण्डलेपभुजश्चान्ये|यथा—'पिण्डलेपभुजश्चान्ये]] पितामहपितामहात् । [[समुत्युकासारस्तेषां यजमानव सतमः|प्रभृत्युक्तास्त्रयस्तेषां यजमानश्च सप्तमः]] ॥ इत्येवं मुनिभिः [[मोक्ता|प्रोक्तः]] सम्बन्धः [[सातपौरुषः।।|साप्तपौरुषः ।']] [[अशौचकर इस्पर्थ:|अशौचकर इत्यर्थः]] । दायभाग XI para 41. The verses [['fore FATTT: 1 are are feu|'पिण्डलेप...साप्तपौरुषः' are मार्कण्डेय]] 28. 4-5 with [[slightly.|slightly]] [[differrent|different]] readings (chap. 31 in B. I.).

The [[Kr̥tyakalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] (on [[Rāja dharma)|Rājadharma)]] quotes a long passage (pp. 201-212) from Bhaviṣyapurāṇa [[on. |on ']]_Vasordhārā_', which is quoted by the R.N.P. [[(pp. 447-457 )|(pp. 447-457)]] from the [[Devipurāṇa.|Devīpurāṇa.]] The [[Kālikāpurāpa|Kālikāpurāṇa]] in chapter 87 [[dovotos|devotes]] 131 verses to [[Rājaniti,|Rājanīti,]] in which a summary is furnished of the course of conduct that should be followed by the king. This chapter expressly mentions the works of Uśanas and [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]] (verses 99 and 130) and advises the king to honour brāhmaṇas that are advanced in knowledge, learning, _tapas_ and age, to control his senses, to employ the four _upāyas_ (sāma, dāna, [[danda|daṇḍa]] and [[bheda ),|bheda),]] to avoid the vices of gambling, drinking, indulgence in sexual matters and hunting, to practise the six guṇas [[( yāna,|(yāna,]] āsana [[&c. ),|&c.),]] to test the princes, councillors, the queens and other female relatives by _upadhās_ [[( investigation|(investigation]] of character by various [[trioks) 1635|tricks).]][^1635]

[^1635]: Compare the heading of [[the|कौटिल्य]] I. 10 [['quit: persiana ममात्यानाम् ,|'उपधाभिः शौचाशौचज्ञानममात्यानाम्']] and the following [[passages);|passages;]] क्षीरस्वामी, while commenting on the word [[39u,|उपधा,]] quotes this heading about [[get from recy|उपधा from कौटिल्य]] (expressly [[named ):|named):]]

[[P1002]] [[. It|It]] appears that [[Kautilya's|Kauṭilya's]] Arthaśāstra was not available to most medieval writers and therefore they relied on the purāṇas for the treatment of [[Rajadharma.|Rājadharma.]] But the early [[Puranas|purāṇas]] like the Matsya appear to have made use of [[Kautilya's|Kauṭilya's]] work. Vide the author's paper on [['Kautilya|'Kauṭilya]] and the Matsya-purāṇa' in Dr. B. C. Law presentation volume II. pp. 13-15.

In the matter of the administration of justice and the law of [[SUCCession|succession]] and inheritance also some of the Purāṇas have influenced the views of writers of _nibandhas_. The [[Kr̥tya kalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] on Vyavahāra quotes about twelve verses from the Kālikā-purāṇa on the marks of a truthful party or witness and on the appropriate ordeals in the case of the several [[varṇas, 1636|varṇas,]][^1636] their procedure and the different causes of action (vide pp. 79, 205, 210, 211, 221, 231, 238). Three verses from the Kālikā (chap. 91. 35-37) about the twelve kinds of sons and the [[impro priety|impropriety]] of making a punarbhava, [[svayandatta|svayaṁdatta]] and a _dāsa_ successor to a kingdom are quoted in the R. N. P. pp. 35 and 42. [[Verges|Verses]] 38-41 of Kālikā, chapter 91, about the sons that can be adopted and the age up to which they can be adopted are quoted by the [[Dattakamimamsā|Dattakamīmāṁsā]] p. 60 [[( Ānan.|(Ānan.]] ed.) and by the [[Vyava haramayūkba|Vyavahāramayūkha]] p. 114, though the latter remarks that they were not found in two or three mss. of the [[Purāṇa, 1637|Purāṇa.]][^1637] In connection with the question of the seniority among [[twing|twins]] the [[Mayakha|Mayūkha]] quotes [[Sridhara's|Śrīdhara's]] comment on Bhāgavata (III. 19. 18) that the son born first is the younger one; but the [[Vyavahāramayūkha1638|Vyavahāramayūkha]][^1638] makes the interesting remark that in the Purāṇas practices opposed to the [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are frequently [[soon.|seen.]]

[^1636]: The three verses on pp. 210-211 beginning with [['paradārābhl lāpe'|'paradārābhiśāpe']] &c. [[In|in]] Kṛtyakalpataru are quoted also by the [[Vyavabāramayūkba|Vyavahāramayūkha]] p. 45 and the Rājadharmakaustubha p. 408.

A.|व्य. म.]] 114 ([[B. O,|B. O.]] R. I. ed. 1926).
